Then the set

\Lambda_{C}=\{\mu\in L^{\infty}_{\rm w}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}))\,:\,\int_{[0,T]\times\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\varsigma(\mathbf{f})\,{\rm d}\mu_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{f}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}\leq C\quad\mbox{for any $T\geq 0$ and $\hat{K}\Subset\mathbb{R}$}\}

is a compact subset in
L^{\infty}_{\rm w}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}))
,
where
C
is a positive constant, and

\hat{K}\Subset\mathbb{R}
means
\hat{K}
is a compact subset of
\mathbb{R}
.

The proof of Proposition
4.1
is in the spirit of Ball
[
2
]
;
see Appendix
A.4
.

For the initial law
\Im
given in Theorem
2.3
,
by
[
6
, Corollary 2.6.4]
, there exist random variables
(\rho_{0},m_{0})=(\rho_{0},\rho_{0}u_{0})

with law
\Im
.
We now construct
(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0},m^{\varepsilon}_{0})
satisfying the conditions
in Theorem
3.1
.
By cut-off technique and mollification, we can directly
construct
\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}
such that
\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\rho_{\infty}\in H^{5}({\mathbb{R}})

\mathbb{P}
-
a.s.
,

\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\omega)\geq c_{0}(\varepsilon)>0

a.e.
in
\mathbb{R}
for all
\omega\in\Omega
,
and
\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}\to\rho_{0}
in
L^{p}(\Omega;L^{\gamma_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))
for

1\leq p<\infty
.
Using the cut-off technique and mollification again, we can construct
\vartheta_{0}^{\varepsilon}
such that
\vartheta_{0}^{\varepsilon}\to\vartheta_{0}:=\frac{m_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}
in
L^{p}(\Omega;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))
for

1\leq p<\infty
,

\,m^{\varepsilon}_{0}:=\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}}\,\vartheta_{0}^{\varepsilon}\in H^{5}({\mathbb{R}})

\mathbb{P}
-
a.s.
,
m^{\varepsilon}_{0}\to m_{0}
in
L^{p}(\Omega;L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))
for
1\leq p<\infty
,
and
U^{\varepsilon}_{0}=(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0},m^{\varepsilon}_{0})^{\top}
satisfies

\displaystyle U^{\varepsilon}_{0}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}}\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-{\it a.s.}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{(m^{\varepsilon}_{0})^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}}+e^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\Big]\leq C\,\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{m_{0}^{2}}{\rho_{0}}+e^{*}(\rho_{0},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big]\qquad\text{for}\ p>3.

See also
[
17
, Appendix A]
for the construction of the initial data
in the multi-dimensional case when the initial density
\rho_{0}\to\rho_{\infty}
at the far field.

Therefore, with the initial data
U^{\varepsilon}_{0}
constructed above,
Theorem
3.1
yields the existence of
strong solutions
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
to the parabolic approximation (
3.1
).
We now establish suitable compactness and perform the vanishing viscosity limit
\varepsilon\to 0

to obtain the solution of the stochastic Euler system (
1.1
).

Let
\mu^{\varepsilon}:=\delta_{(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})}

be the random Young measures (Young measure-valued random variables)
associated with the parabolic approximation
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
.
We now define the path space

\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{X}_{\mu}\times\mathcal{X}_{W}\times\mathcal{X}_{\rho}\times\mathcal{X}_{m}\times\mathcal{X}_{\rho_{0}}\times\mathcal{X}_{m_{0}}\times\mathcal{X}_{\frac{m_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}}
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{\mu}=L^{\infty}_{\rm w}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})),\qquad\mathcal{X}_{W}=C([0,T];\mathfrak{A}_{0}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{\rho}=C_{\rm w}([0,T];L^{\gamma_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))\cap L^{\gamma_{2}+1}_{\rm w,loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{m}=C_{\rm w}([0,T];L^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))\cap L^{\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{\gamma_{2}+3}}_{\rm w,loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{\rho_{0}}=L^{\gamma_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}),\qquad\mathcal{X}_{m_{0}}=L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}),\qquad\mathcal{X}_{\frac{m_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}}=L^{2}(\mathbb{R}).

We use
\mathcal{L}_{g}:=\mathcal{L}(g)
to denote the law of random variable
g
. The joint law of all variables
(\mu^{\varepsilon},W,\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0},m^{\varepsilon}_{0},\vartheta_{0}^{\varepsilon})
is denoted by
\mathcal{L}^{\varepsilon}
.

Proposition 4.2
.

The joint law
\mathcal{L}^{\varepsilon}
is tight on
\mathcal{X}
.

Proof.

The proof is divided into four steps.

1
.
\{\rho^{\varepsilon}\,:\,\varepsilon>0\}
is tight on
\mathcal{X}_{\rho}
:
On one hand, by (
2.27
)
and the energy estimate (
3.7
), we immediately see that

\{\rho^{\varepsilon}\,:\,\varepsilon>0\}
is uniformly bounded
in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{\gamma_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))
.

On the other hand, recall equation
\eqref{eq-parabolic-approximation-seperate}_{1}
satisfied by the density:

{\rm d}\rho+\partial_{x}m\,{\rm d}t=\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\rho\,{\rm d}t.

Since
|m|^{q}\leq\rho u^{2}+\rho^{\gamma_{2}}
for
q=\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}
,
it follows from the energy estimate (
3.7
) that

\{m^{\varepsilon}\,:\,\varepsilon>0\}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))
, which implies
\{\partial_{x}m^{\varepsilon}\,:\,\varepsilon>0\}

is uniformly bounded in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{\infty}([0,T];W^{-1,\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))
.
Morover, since
\{\rho^{\varepsilon}\,:\,\varepsilon>0\}
is uniformly bounded
in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{\gamma_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))
,
then
\{\partial_{x}^{2}\rho^{\varepsilon}\,:\,\varepsilon>0\}
is uniformly bounded
in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{\infty}([0,T];W^{-2,\gamma_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))
.
As a consequence of the continuity equation
\eqref{eq-parabolic-approximation-seperate}_{1}
,
we conclude that
\{\rho^{\varepsilon}\,:\,\varepsilon>0\}
is uniformly bounded
in
L^{1}(\Omega;C^{\alpha}([0,T];W^{-2,\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))
for any
0<\alpha<1
.

Therefore, the required tightness of
\{\rho^{\varepsilon}\,:\,\varepsilon>0\}

on
C_{\rm w}([0,T];L^{\gamma_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))
follows due to the compact embedding:

L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{\gamma_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))\cap C^{\alpha}([0,T];W^{-2,\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))\hookrightarrow C_{\rm w}([0,T];L^{\gamma_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))

from Lemma
A.8
.

The tightness in

L^{\gamma_{2}+1}_{\rm w,loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})

follows directly from Proposition
3.1
.

2
.
\{m^{\varepsilon}\,:\,\varepsilon>0\}
is tight on
\mathcal{X}_{m}
:
From Step
1
, we know that
\{m^{\varepsilon}\,:\,\varepsilon>0\}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))
.

Next, we prove the uniform time-regularity of
m^{\varepsilon}
.
We rewrite equation
\eqref{eq-parabolic-approximation-seperate}_{2}
into the integral form:

\displaystyle m^{\varepsilon}(t)

\displaystyle=m^{\varepsilon}(0)-\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{x}\big(\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+P(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)\,{\rm d}\tau+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{x}^{2}m^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}W(\tau)

\displaystyle:=D_{\varepsilon}(t)+Z_{\varepsilon}(t),

where
Z_{\varepsilon}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}W(\tau)
,
and
D_{\varepsilon}(t)
denotes the deterministic part.

We first show the uniform time-regularity of the deterministic part
D_{\varepsilon}(t)
.
The energy estimate (
3.7
) implies that

\{\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is uniformly bounded
in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))
, and hence uniformly bounded
in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{\infty}([0,T];W^{-1,q}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))
for
q>1
by the Sobolev
embedding:
L^{1}\hookrightarrow W^{-1,q}
for
q>1
.
Then
\{\partial_{x}(\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}})\}_{\varepsilon>0}

is uniformly bounded in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{\infty}([0,T];W^{-2,q}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))
for
q>1
.
The same argument yields that, for
q>1
,
\{\partial_{x}P(\rho^{\varepsilon})\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is uniformly
bounded in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{\infty}([0,T];W^{-2,q}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))
.
Since
\{m^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is uniformly bounded
in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))
,
we obtain that
\{\partial_{x}^{2}m^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is uniformly bounded
in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{\infty}([0,T];W^{-2,\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))
.
Therefore,
\{D_{\varepsilon}(t)\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is uniformly bounded
in
L^{1}(\Omega;C^{\alpha_{1}}([0,T];W^{-2,k_{1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))

with
k_{1}\leq\min\{q,\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}\}
for any
0<\alpha_{1}<1
.

We next show the uniform time-regularity for the stochastic part
Z_{\varepsilon}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}W(\tau)
. By the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality,
for any
r_{1}\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}W(\tau)\Big\|_{H^{-1}(\hat{K})}^{r_{1}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\sum_{k}\|a_{k}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{-1}(\hat{K})}^{2}\,{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{\frac{r_{1}}{2}}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\sum_{k}\sup_{\varphi\in H^{1}_{0}(\hat{K}),\|\varphi\|_{H^{1}_{0}(\hat{K})}\leq 1}|\langle a_{k}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon},\varphi\rangle|^{2}\,{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{\frac{r_{1}}{2}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\sum_{k}\Big\|\frac{a_{k}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(\hat{K})}^{2}\|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L^{2}(\hat{K})}^{2}\,{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{\frac{r_{1}}{2}}\big],

where, in the last inequality, we have used the Sobolev
embedding:
H^{1}_{0}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}
.
Using
\rho\leq 1+\rho^{\gamma_{2}}

and (
2.27
), we obtain

\|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L^{2}(\hat{K})}^{2}\leq(1+\bar{C}_{\gamma_{2}}\rho_{\infty}^{\gamma_{2}})|\hat{K}|+\bar{C}_{\gamma_{2}}\int_{\hat{K}}e^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho_{\infty})\,{\rm d}x.

By the same argument and using (
3.5
), we have

\sum_{k}\Big\|\frac{a_{k}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(\hat{K})}^{2}\leq C(|\hat{K}|)+C\int_{\hat{K}}\big(\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+e^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x.

(4.2)

Therefore, combining the above estimates and applying the energy
estimate (
3.7
), we have

\mathbb{E}\big[\Big\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}W(\tau)\Big\|_{H^{-1}(\hat{K})}^{r_{1}}\big]\leq C(|\hat{K}|,E_{0,r_{1}})|t_{2}-t_{1}|^{\frac{r_{1}}{2}}.

Choosing
\frac{r_{1}}{2}>1
and applying the Kolmogorov continuity criterion, we obtain

\mathbb{E}\big[\Big\|\int_{0}^{\cdot}\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}W\Big\|^{r_{1}}_{C^{\alpha}([0,T];H^{-1}(\hat{K}))}\big]\leq C(r_{1},|\hat{K}|,E_{0,r_{1}}),

where
\alpha\in(0,\frac{\frac{r_{1}}{2}-1}{r_{1}})
.

Hence, combining the uniform time-regularity of
D_{\varepsilon}(t)
and
Z_{\varepsilon}(t)
,
we finally obtain that
\{m^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is uniformly bounded in

L^{r_{1}}(\Omega;C^{\alpha}([0,T];W^{-2,k_{1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))
with
k_{1}\leq\min\{q,\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}\}
and some
r_{1}>2
.

By Lemma
A.8
again,
for
k_{1}\leq\min\{q,\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}\},q>1,\alpha\in(0,\frac{\frac{r_{1}}{2}-1}{r_{1}})
, and
r_{1}>2
, the following embedding:

L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))\cap C^{\alpha}([0,T];W^{-2,k_{1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))\hookrightarrow C_{\rm w}([0,T];L^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))

is compact. Thus, the required tightness of
\{m^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is proved.

Since
|m|^{\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{\gamma_{2}+3}}\leq\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}+\rho^{\gamma_{2}+1}
,
the tightness in
L^{\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{\gamma_{2}+3}}_{\rm w,loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})

follows
from Propositions
3.1
–
3.2
.

3
.
\{\mu^{\varepsilon}\,:\,\varepsilon>0\}
is tight on
\mathcal{X}_{\mu}
:    Denote

\Lambda_{R}=\left\{\mu\in L^{\infty}_{\rm w}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}))\,:\,{\begin{array}[]{lll}&\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\big(\rho^{\gamma_{2}}+|m|^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}\big)\,{\rm d}\mu_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\leq R\\[1.42262pt]
&\,\,\mbox{for any $T\geq 0$ and $\hat{K}\Subset\mathbb{R}$}\end{array}}\right\}.

By Proposition
4.1
,

\Lambda_{R}
is a compact subset in
L^{\infty}_{\rm w}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}))
.

By the Chebyshev inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mu^{\varepsilon}\notin\Lambda_{R})\leq\frac{1}{R}\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{[0,T]\times\hat{K}}\big((\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{2}}+|m^{\varepsilon}|^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\leq\frac{1}{R}C(T,|\hat{K}|,E_{0,1}),

where, in the last inequality, we have used the fact that

|m^{\varepsilon}|^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{2}}
, (
2.27
),
and the energy estimate (
3.7
).
Thus, the required tightness of
\{\mu^{\varepsilon}\,:\,\varepsilon>0\}
follows.

4
.
\{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is tight on
\mathcal{X}_{\rho_{0}}
,

\,\{m^{\varepsilon}_{0}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is tight on
\mathcal{X}_{m_{0}}
,
and
\{\frac{m^{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}}}\}_{\varepsilon>0}

is tight on
\mathcal{X}_{\frac{m_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}}
:
The conclusion follows from Prokhorov’s theorem
[
4
, Theorem 5.2]
,
since the constructed initial data
(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0},m^{\varepsilon}_{0})
satisfies
\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}\to\rho_{0}
in
L^{\gamma_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})
,
m^{\varepsilon}_{0}\to m_{0}
in
L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})
,
and
\frac{m^{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}}}\to\frac{m_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

Finally, since the singleton
\mathcal{L}_{W}
is a Radon measure on the Polish space
\mathcal{X}_{W}
, it is, of course, tight.
Therefore, combining the above results, we obtain our final conclusion.
∎

Note that the path space
\mathcal{X}
is not a Polish space, since the weak topology is generally not metrizable.
However,
\mathcal{X}
belongs to the class of sub-Polish spaces, which enables us to use the Jakubowski-Skorokhod
representation theorem
[
29
]
.
More specifically, recall that

L^{\infty}_{\rm w}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}))=\big(L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}))\big)^{*}

and
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}))
is a separable Banach space (
cf.

[
24
, Section 8.18]
).
Thus,
\mathcal{X}_{\mu}=L^{\infty}_{\rm w}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}))
equipped with the weak-star
topology is a sub-Polish space.
Moreover,
C_{\rm w}([0,T];W^{k,p}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))
with
k\geq 0
and
1<p<\infty

also belongs to the class of sub-Polish spaces (
cf
.
[
39
, Proposition B.3]
).
Hence,
\mathcal{X}_{\rho}=C_{\rm w}([0,T];L^{\gamma_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))
and
\mathcal{X}_{m}=C_{\rm w}([0,T];L^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))
are also sub-Polish spaces. Therefore, by Proposition
4.2
, we apply the Jakubowski-Skorokhod representation theorem to obtain the following result:

Proposition 4.3
.

There exists a complete probability space
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})

with
\mathcal{X}
-valued Borel measurable random variables

\tilde{X}^{\varepsilon}:=(\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{W}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}_{0},\tilde{\vartheta}^{\varepsilon}_{0})
for
\varepsilon>0

and
\tilde{X}:=(\tilde{\mu},\tilde{W},\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m},\tilde{\rho}_{0},\tilde{m}_{0},\tilde{\vartheta}_{0})

such that the following properties hold
(
up to a subsequence
)
:

(i)

The law of
\tilde{X}^{\varepsilon}
is equal to
\mathcal{L}^{\varepsilon}
;

(ii)

\tilde{X}^{\varepsilon}
converges
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s. to
\tilde{X}
in
\mathcal{X}
.

We identify the initial data as follows:

Proposition 4.4
.

For a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
\,\,\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}=\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}|_{t=0},\,\,\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}_{0}=\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}|_{t=0}
,
and
\,\tilde{\vartheta}^{\varepsilon}_{0}=\frac{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}}

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s..

Proof.

\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}=\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}|_{t=0}
and

\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}_{0}=\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}|_{t=0}
directly follow
from the equality of laws in Proposition
4.3
.

We now prove that
\tilde{\vartheta}^{\varepsilon}_{0}=\frac{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}}
.
Denote
\mathcal{L}^{\varepsilon}_{m,\rho,\frac{m}{\sqrt{\rho}}}:=\mathcal{L}(m^{\varepsilon}_{0},\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0},\frac{m^{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}}})

and
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\varepsilon}_{m,\rho,\frac{m}{\sqrt{\rho}}}:=\mathcal{L}(\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}_{0},\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0},\frac{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}})
.
Then, by Proposition
4.3
(i),
we see that
\mathcal{L}^{\varepsilon}_{m,\rho,\frac{m}{\sqrt{\rho}}}=\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\varepsilon}_{m,\rho,\frac{m}{\sqrt{\rho}}}
. Thus, for any
\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(m^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}}\frac{m^{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}}}\big)\varphi\,\,{\rm d}x\Big|\big]

(4.3)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{X}_{m}\times\mathcal{X}_{\rho}\times\mathcal{X}_{\frac{m}{\sqrt{\rho}}}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}(z-\sqrt{y}v)\varphi\,\,{\rm d}x\Big|\,{\rm d}\mathcal{L}^{\varepsilon}_{m,\rho,\frac{m}{\sqrt{\rho}}}(z,y,v)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{X}_{m}\times\mathcal{X}_{\rho}\times\mathcal{X}_{\frac{m}{\sqrt{\rho}}}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}(z-\sqrt{y}v)\varphi\,\,{\rm d}x\Big|\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\varepsilon}_{m,\rho,\frac{m}{\sqrt{\rho}}}(z,y,v)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}\tilde{\vartheta}^{\varepsilon}_{0}\big)\varphi\,\,{\rm d}x\Big|\big],

where we have used the fact that the map:

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{m}\times\mathcal{X}_{\rho}\times\mathcal{X}_{\frac{m}{\sqrt{\rho}}}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle(z,y,v)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\int_{\mathbb{R}}(z-\sqrt{y}v)\varphi\,\,{\rm d}x,

is continuous and hence measurable. Since
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is arbitrary and

\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}\tilde{\vartheta}^{\varepsilon}_{0}\in L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})
,
we obtain from (
4.3
) that,
for
a.e.

x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\tilde{\vartheta}^{\varepsilon}_{0}=\frac{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}}

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
.
∎

5
Stochastic Compensated Compactness Framework

This section is devoted to establishing the following stochastic compensated compactness framework in
L^{p}
:

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
be a sequence of random variables satisfying that,
for any
K\Subset\mathbb{R}
, there exists
C(K,p,T)>0
independent of
\varepsilon
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{K}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}P(\rho^{\varepsilon})+\frac{|m^{\varepsilon}|^{3}}{(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}}\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{p}\big]\leq C(K,p,T)\qquad\mbox{for any $t\in[0,T]$},

where
P(\rho)
satisfies (
2.4
)–(
2.5
),
so that
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
are tight in

L^{\gamma_{2}+1}_{\rm w,loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\times L^{\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{\gamma_{2}+3}}_{\rm w,loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})

and possess a Skorokhod representation
(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})
.
Assume that

\big\{\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{t}+q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{x}\big\}_{\varepsilon>0}\qquad\text{are tight in $W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}

for all weak entropy pairs
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
of system (
1.1
) defined in (
2.13
) with generating function
\psi\in C_{\rm c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.
Then there exist random variables
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})
such that
almost surely
(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\,\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})\to(\tilde{\rho},\,\tilde{m})

almost everywhere and
(
up to a subsequence
)
almost surely,

(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\,\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})\to(\tilde{\rho},\,\tilde{m})\qquad\text{in $L^{\hat{p}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\times L^{\hat{q}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}

for
\hat{p}\in[1,\gamma_{2}+1)
and
\hat{q}\in[1,\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{\gamma_{2}+3})
.

We first provide the following result regarding the admissible test functions and
the convergence of the random Young measure
\tilde{\mu}
obtained
in Proposition
4.3
.
The proposition below is a version of Proposition 5.1 in
[
14
]

for the random Young measure (however, we don’t use the compactification technique here).
From now on, we denote
\mathfrak{T}:=\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\rho>0\}
.

Proposition 5.1
.

For the limit random Young measure
\tilde{\mu}
obtained in
Proposition
4.3
, the following properties hold
:

(i)

\tilde{\mu}
satisfies the following additional integrability property
:

For any
\hat{K}\Subset\mathbb{R}
and any
T>0
, there exists

\hat{C}>0

such that

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\big(\rho P(\rho)+\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\big)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\leq\hat{C}.

(5.1)

(ii)

The space of admissible test functions for
\tilde{\mu}
can be extended as follows
:

Let
\phi
satisfy

(a)

\phi\in C(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
with
\phi|_{\partial{\mathfrak{T}}}=0
, where
\partial{\mathfrak{T}}
denotes the boundary of
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
;

(b)

{\rm supp}(\phi)\subset\{(\rho,m)\,:\,w_{1}(\rho,m)\leq a,w_{2}(\rho,m)\geq-a\}

for some constant
a>0
, where
(w_{1},w_{2})
are the Riemann invariants
defined in (
2.10
)
;

(c)

|\phi(\rho,m)|\leq C\rho^{\beta(\gamma_{2}+1)}
uniformly in
m
for large
\rho>1
and some
\beta\in(0,1)
, where
C
is a constant independent of
(\rho,m)
.

Then, for any
\hat{K}\Subset\mathbb{R}
and any
T>0
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\phi(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]<\infty,

(5.2)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\Big(\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\phi(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}(\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}-\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)})(\rho,m)\Big)\varphi(t,x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big|\big]=0

(5.3)

for any
\varphi\in L^{q}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
with
q=\frac{1}{1-\beta}
.

Proof.

We first prove (i). Let
w_{k}(\rho,m)\geq 0
be a continuous cut-off function such that
w_{k}\equiv 1

on the set
\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\frac{1}{k}\leq\mathcal{K}(\rho)\leq k,|m|\leq k\}

and
w_{k}\equiv 0
outside the set
\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\frac{1}{2k}\leq\mathcal{K}(\rho)\leq 2k,|m|\leq 2k\}
.
Then
\big(\rho P(\rho)+\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\big)w_{k}(\rho,m)\in C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
.
We obtain that, for any
\hat{K}\Subset\mathbb{R}
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\big(\rho P(\rho)+\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\big)w_{k}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\to\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\big(\rho P(\rho)+\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\big)w_{k}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\qquad\mbox{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

Additionally, by the equality of laws from Proposition
4.3
,
it follows from
Propositions
3.1
–
3.2

that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\big(\rho P(\rho)+\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\big)w_{k}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}P(\rho^{\varepsilon})+\frac{|m^{\varepsilon}|^{3}}{(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}}\big)w_{k}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\leq{\hat{C}},

where
C

is independent of
(\varepsilon,k)
. Thus, by Fatou’s lemma, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\big(\rho P(\rho)+\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\big)w_{k}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\,\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\big(\rho P(\rho)+\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\big)w_{k}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\leq C,

which then implies from the monotone convergence theorem that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\big(\rho P(\rho)+\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\big)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]

(5.4)

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\to\infty}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\big(\rho P(\rho)+\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\big)w_{k}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\leq C.

Then the conclusion in (i) follows.

Next, we prove (ii) by two steps.

1
. Without loss of generality, we may suppose that
\phi\geq 0
; otherwise,
we can use the decomposition
\phi=\phi^{+}-\phi^{-}
with
\phi^{+}=\max\{\phi,0\}

and
\phi^{-}=\max\{-\phi,0\}
satisfying the same conditions as
\phi
.
Let
w_{k}
be as in the proof of (i). Then
\phi w_{k}\in C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})

and
\langle\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)},\phi w_{k}\rangle
is well-defined. Since

\phi w_{k}\leq C\rho^{\beta(\gamma_{2}+1)}\leq C\big(1+\rho^{\gamma_{2}+1}\big),

by the conclusion of (i) and (
2.21
),
we apply the dominated convergence theorem to yield

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\phi\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\to\infty}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\phi w_{k}\,\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}C(1+\rho^{\gamma_{2}+1})\,\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]<\infty.

Thus, the first conclusion (
5.2
) in (ii) is proved.

2
. We now prove the second conclusion (
5.3
) in (ii).
We first assume
\varphi\in L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
.
Using Proposition
4.3
and similar arguments as in Step
1
,
it is direct to adapt those estimates in the deterministic case (see
[
14
, Proposition 5.1]
)
to the stochastic case here (hence we omit the details) to derive (
5.3
)
for
\varphi\in L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
.

We now prove (
5.3
) for more general
\varphi\in L^{q}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})

with
q=\frac{1}{1-\beta}
.
Let
\nu_{(t,x)}\in\{\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}\}_{\varepsilon>0}\cup\{\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}\}
.
Propositions
3.1
–
3.2

enable us to utilize similar arguments as those in the proof of (i) above to obtain

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\big(\rho P(\rho)+\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\big)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\leq{\hat{C}}.

(5.5)

Thus, the Hölder inequality gives

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\varphi\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\phi(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\nu_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big|\big]

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}|\phi(\rho,m)|^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\,{\rm d}\nu_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\Big)^{\beta}\Big(\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}|\varphi|^{q}\,{\rm d}\nu_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq{\hat{C}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}|\varphi|^{q}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

Since

\varphi\mapsto\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\varphi\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\phi(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\nu_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big|\big]

is linear, by density argument, then
the second conclusion (
5.3
)
for more general
\varphi\in L^{q}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
with
q=\frac{1}{1-\beta}
follows.
∎

From now on, for an admissible function
f(\rho,m)
, we denote

\bar{f}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}f(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)=\langle\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)},f\rangle.

We now collect some properties for the entropy pairs generated by compactly supported functions.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
be a weak entropy pair generated by

\psi\in C_{\rm c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
as defined
in (
2.13
).
Then the following statements hold
:

(i)

For the general pressure law, if
\eta^{\psi}
is regarded as a function of
(\rho,u)
, then

\displaystyle|\eta^{\psi}_{\rho}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}(1+\rho^{\theta_{1}})\qquad\text{when $\rho\leq\rho^{\star}$},

(5.6)

\displaystyle|\eta^{\psi}_{\rho}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{\theta_{2}}\qquad\qquad\,\,\,\text{when $\rho\geq\rho^{\star}$}.

(5.7)

If
\eta^{\psi}
is regarded as a function of
(\rho,m)
, then

|\eta_{m}^{\psi}(\rho,m)|+|\rho\partial_{m}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}.

(5.8)

If
\eta^{\psi}_{m}
is regarded as a function of
(\rho,u)
, then

\displaystyle|\rho^{1-\theta_{1}}\partial_{m\rho}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}\qquad\text{when $\rho\leq\rho^{\star}$},

(5.9)

\displaystyle|\rho^{1-\theta_{2}}\partial_{m\rho}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C_{\psi}\qquad\text{when $\rho\geq\rho^{\star}$}.

(ii)

If
\eta^{\psi}
is regarded as a function of
(\rho,m)
, then

|(\partial_{x}U)^{\top}\,\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U)\,\partial_{x}U|\leq C_{\psi}(\partial_{x}U)^{\top}\,\nabla^{2}\eta_{E}(U)\,\partial_{x}U.

(5.10)

Proof.

For (i),
all the estimates come from
[
11
, Lemmas 4.5 and 4.7]
.

(ii) follows from (
5.8
)–(
5.9
) once we notice that

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}U)^{\top}\,\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U)\,\partial_{x}U

\displaystyle=(\partial_{x}\rho)^{2}\big(\mathcal{K}^{\prime}(\rho)\big)^{2}\partial_{u}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)+2\partial_{x}\rho\partial_{x}u\big(\partial_{\rho u}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)-\frac{1}{\rho}\partial_{u}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)\big)+(\partial_{x}u)^{2}\partial_{u}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle=(\partial_{x}\rho)^{2}\big(\mathcal{K}^{\prime}(\rho)\big)^{2}\rho^{2}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta^{\psi}+2\partial_{x}\rho\partial_{x}u\rho\partial_{m\rho}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)+(\partial_{x}u)^{2}\rho^{2}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta^{\psi}.

∎

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
be a weak entropy pair generated by

\psi\in C_{\rm c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
as defined
in (
2.13
).
Suppose that
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
with
m^{\varepsilon}=\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}

is a sequence of solutions to the parabolic approximation equations (
3.1
).
Then
\{\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\}_{\varepsilon>0}

is tight in
H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
.

Proof.

We prove the conclusion by verifying that
\varepsilon\partial_{x}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\to 0

in probability in
L^{2}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
.
Note that

|\partial_{x}\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})|=|(\eta^{\psi}_{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+u^{\varepsilon}\eta^{\psi}_{m^{\varepsilon}})\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}+\eta_{m^{\varepsilon}}^{\psi}\rho^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}|.

Notice that, for the general pressure law case,
\eta^{\psi}_{\rho^{\varepsilon}}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})+u^{\varepsilon}\eta^{\psi}_{m^{\varepsilon}}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})=\eta^{\psi}_{\rho^{\varepsilon}}(\rho^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon})
. Thus,
for any
\hat{K}\Subset\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\varepsilon^{2}|\partial_{x}\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\varepsilon^{2}|\eta^{\psi}_{\rho^{\varepsilon}}(\rho^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon})|^{2}|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+C_{\psi}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\varepsilon^{2}(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}|\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t.

For the first term on the right-hand side, by (
5.6
)–(
5.7
), we have

|\eta^{\psi}_{\rho^{\varepsilon}}(\rho^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon})|^{2}|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\leq\begin{cases}C_{\psi}\big(1+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{1}-1}\big)|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\qquad\text{when $\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}$},\\[1.42262pt]
C_{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{2}-1}|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\quad\qquad\quad\,\,\,\,\text{when $\rho^{\varepsilon}\geq\rho^{\star}$}.\end{cases}

We first treat
(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{1}-1}|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}
and
(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{2}-1}|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}
.
From (
2.18
), (
2.20
), and
Theorem
3.1
(ii), we see that,
when
\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho_{\star}
,
(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{1}-2}\leq C\frac{P^{\prime}(\rho^{\varepsilon})}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}=C\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{\prime\prime}
and

(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\theta_{1}}\leq C\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}
. When
\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}
, by direct calculation and again Theorem
3.1
(ii),
we obtain that
(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{1}-2}\leq C(\rho_{\star},\rho^{\star})\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{\prime\prime}

and
C(\rho_{\star},\rho^{\star})\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{\star}\big)\leq\mathcal{K}(\rho^{\varepsilon})\leq\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}
.
Therefore, we have

(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{1}-1}|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\leq\begin{cases}C(\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon})^{\frac{1}{\theta_{1}}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{\prime\prime}|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\qquad\text{when $\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho_{\star}$},\\[1.42262pt]
C\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{\prime\prime}|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\qquad\qquad\,\text{when $\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}$}.\end{cases}

Similarly, from (
2.21
), (
2.23
), and
Theorem
3.1
(ii),
we obtain that, when
\rho^{\varepsilon}\geq\rho^{\star}
,

(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{2}-1}|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon})^{\frac{1}{\theta_{2}}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{\prime\prime}|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}.

By the energy estimate (
3.7
) and the Chebyshev inequality, we have

\mathbb{P}\Big\{\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big((\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon}))^{\prime\prime}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\leq R\Big\}\to 1\qquad\,\,\text{as}\ R\to\infty.

Denote

A_{R}:=\Big\{\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big((\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon}))^{\prime\prime}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\leq R\Big\}.

Then, restricted to the event
A_{R}
, letting
i=1
when
\rho_{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}

and
i=2
when
\rho_{\varepsilon}\geq\rho^{\star}
, we have

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\varepsilon^{2}(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2\theta_{i}}|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\to 0\qquad\text{as}\ \varepsilon\to 0

when
1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\theta(\rho^{\varepsilon})}>0
for
\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho_{\star}

or
\rho^{\varepsilon}\geq\rho^{\star}
and
1-\alpha_{1}>0
for
\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}
.

We now deal with
|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}
for
\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}
. When
\gamma_{1}\leq 2
, using similar arguments as above, we have

|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}=(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2-\gamma_{1}}(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{1}-2}|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\leq\begin{cases}C(\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon})^{\frac{2-\gamma_{1}}{\theta_{1}}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{\prime\prime}|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\qquad\text{when $\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho_{\star}$},\\[1.42262pt]
C(\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon})^{2-\gamma_{1}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{\prime\prime}|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\qquad\text{when $\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}$}.\end{cases}

Thus, restricted to
A_{R}
, when
1-\alpha_{1}\frac{2-\gamma_{1}}{\theta_{1}}>0
for
\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho_{\star}
and
1-\alpha_{1}(2-\gamma_{1})>0
for
\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}
, we have

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\varepsilon^{2}|\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\to 0\qquad\,\text{as}\ \varepsilon\to 0.

When
\gamma_{1}>2
, we multiply
\eqref{eq-parabolic-approximation-seperate}_{1}
by
\big(\delta-(\rho^{\varepsilon}\land\delta)\big)\phi^{2}
, where
\delta>0
is a constant and
\phi\in C_{c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
with
{\rm supp}(\phi)=K

and
\phi|_{\hat{K}}=1
,
and then integrate by parts to obtain

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}I_{\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\delta}\phi^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=2\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}\big(\delta-(\rho^{\varepsilon}\land\delta)\big)\phi\phi_{x}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\partial_{t}\rho^{\varepsilon}\big(\delta-(\rho^{\varepsilon}\land\delta)\big)\phi^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\rho^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\big(\delta-(\rho^{\varepsilon}\land\delta)\big)\phi^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}\big(\delta-(\rho^{\varepsilon}\land\delta)\big)\phi^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{4}J_{k}.

By the Hölder inequality,

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon\delta\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}\phi^{2}I_{\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\delta}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\phi_{x}^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\delta\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}\phi^{2}I_{\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\delta}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\varepsilon\delta C(|K|,T).

Integrating by parts again gives

J_{2}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\partial_{t}\Big(\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}(\delta-\rho^{\varepsilon})+\frac{(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{2}\big)I_{\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\delta}\phi^{2}\Big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\leq\delta^{2}C(|K|).

By the Hölder inequality again, we have

\displaystyle J_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\delta\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\rho^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\rho^{\varepsilon}I_{\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\delta}\phi^{4}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}C(T,|K|)\Big(\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\rho^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By Theorem
3.1
(ii),
we know that
u^{\varepsilon}\leq\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}=c_{1}\varepsilon^{-\alpha_{1}}
.
Thus, the Hölder inequality again gives

\displaystyle J_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\delta\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}\phi^{2}I_{\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\delta}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\phi^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}\phi^{2}I_{\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\delta}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\delta c_{1}^{2}\varepsilon^{-2\alpha_{1}}C(T,|K|).

Combining the above estimates for
J_{1}
–
J_{4}
, we obtain that, by taking
\delta
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}I_{\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\delta}\phi^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\varepsilon-\delta\varepsilon-\delta}\Big(\varepsilon\delta C(T,|K|)+\delta^{2}C(|K|)+\frac{\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}C(T,|K|)\Big(\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}\rho^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\delta c_{1}^{2}\varepsilon^{-2\alpha_{1}}C(T,|K|)\Big).

Therefore, restricted to the event
A_{R}
, when
1-2\alpha_{1}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{K}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}I_{\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\delta}\phi^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\varepsilon-\delta\varepsilon-\delta}\big(\varepsilon^{2}\delta C(T,|K|)+\varepsilon\delta^{2}C(|K|)+\sqrt{\varepsilon}\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}C(T,|K|)R^{\frac{1}{2}}+\delta c_{1}^{2}\varepsilon^{1-2\alpha_{1}}C(T,|K|)\big)

\displaystyle\to 0\qquad\,\,\text{as $\varepsilon\to 0$},

which implies

\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}I_{\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\delta}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\to 0\qquad\,\,\text{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

On the other hand, since
(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{1}-2}\leq C\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{\prime\prime}
by (
2.18
),

\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}I_{\rho^{\varepsilon}>\delta}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta^{\gamma_{1}-2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\varepsilon(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{1}-2}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\to 0\qquad\text{as}\ \varepsilon\to 0,

when restricted to the event
A_{R}
. Hence,
restricted to the event
A_{R}
, when
1-2\alpha_{1}>0
, we arrive at

\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\to 0\qquad\,\text{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

For the last term
C_{\psi}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\varepsilon^{2}(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}|\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t
, similarly, restricted to the event
A_{R}
, we have

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\varepsilon^{2}(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}|\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t=\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\rho^{\varepsilon}\varepsilon\rho^{\varepsilon}|\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\to 0\qquad\text{as}\ \varepsilon\to 0,

when
1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\theta(\rho^{\varepsilon})}>0
for
\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho_{\star}

or
\rho^{\varepsilon}\geq\rho^{\star}
and
1-\alpha_{1}>0
for
\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}
.

Combining the above results and choosing
\alpha_{1}
satisfying

\begin{cases}\alpha_{1}<\theta_{2}\qquad\qquad\quad\text{for $\gamma_{1}\leq 2$},\\
\alpha_{1}<\min\{\frac{1}{2},\theta_{2}\}\quad\,\,\text{for $\gamma_{1}>2$},\end{cases}

we obtain that, conditionally on the event
A_{R}
,

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\varepsilon^{2}|\partial_{x}\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\to 0\qquad\text{as}\ \varepsilon\to 0.

Thus,
\{\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is tight in
H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
be a weak entropy pair generated by

\psi\in C_{\rm c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
as defined
in (
2.13
).
Suppose that
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
with
m^{\varepsilon}=\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}
is a sequence of solutions to parabolic approximation equations (
3.1
).
Then
\{\varepsilon(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\,\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\,\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is tight in
W^{-1,q_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})

for any
q_{2}\in(1,2)
.

Proof.

By
(
5.10
), we have

\displaystyle|(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\,\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\,\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}|\leq C_{\psi}(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\,\nabla^{2}\eta_{E}(U^{\varepsilon})\,\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}=C_{\psi}\big((\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon}))^{\prime\prime}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\big),

which implies, by the energy estimate (
3.7
),
that
\varepsilon(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\,\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\,\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}

is uniformly bounded in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}))
.
Thus, by the Sobolev compact embedding:
L^{1}\hookrightarrow W^{-1,q_{2}}
for any
q_{2}\in(1,2)
,

\{\varepsilon(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\,\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\,\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}

is tight in
W^{-1,q_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
for any
q_{2}\in(1,2)
.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
be a weak entropy pair generated by

\psi\in C_{\rm c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
as defined
in (
2.13
).
Suppose that
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})

with
m^{\varepsilon}=\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}
is a sequence of solutions to the parabolic
approximation equations (
3.1
).
Then
\{\partial_{t}M^{\varepsilon}(t)\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is tight in
H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
, where
M^{\varepsilon}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\Phi^{\varepsilon}(U^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}W(\tau)
.

Proof.

We are going to use the Aubin-Simon lemma
[
40
, Corollary 9]
to prove the conclusion.
To do this, we first estimate

\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\|M^{\varepsilon}(t)-M^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H^{-\ell}(\hat{K})}^{r_{1}}}{|t-\tau|^{s_{1}r_{1}+1}}\,{\rm d}t{\rm d}\tau\big],

where
\hat{K}
is any compact subset of
\mathbb{R}
,
\ell>\frac{1}{2}
, and
1\leq r_{1}\leq\infty

and
s_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
will be determined later.

Similar to the arguments in Step
2
in the proof of Proposition
4.2
,
by the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|M^{\varepsilon}(t)-M^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H^{-\ell}(\hat{K})}^{r_{1}}\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{\tau}^{t}\sum_{k}\Big\|\frac{a_{k}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(\hat{K})}^{2}\|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}\eta^{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(\hat{K})}^{2}\,{\rm d}r\Big)^{\frac{r_{1}}{2}}\big],

where
we have used the
Sobolev embedding:
H^{\ell}_{0}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}
since
\ell>\frac{1}{2}
. Using
\rho\leq 1+\rho^{\gamma_{2}}
and (
2.27
), we obtain

\|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}\eta^{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(\hat{K})}^{2}\leq C_{\psi}\big(1+\bar{C}_{\gamma_{2}}\rho_{\infty}^{\gamma_{2}}\big)|\hat{K}|+C_{\psi}\bar{C}_{\gamma_{2}}\int_{\hat{K}}e^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho_{\infty})\,{\rm d}x,

where we have used the important fact
that
|\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}\eta^{\psi}|\leq C_{\psi}
since
\psi\in C_{c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
; see
(
5.8
).
The estimate of

\sum_{k}\Big\|\frac{a_{k}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(\hat{K})}^{2}

is the same as (
4.2
).
Combining the above estimates and applying the energy estimate (
3.7
), we obtain

\mathbb{E}\big[\|M^{\varepsilon}(t)-M^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H^{-\ell}(\hat{K})}^{r_{1}}\big]\leq C(\psi,|\hat{K}|,E_{0,r_{1}})|t-\tau|^{\frac{r_{1}}{2}},

(5.11)

which implies that, when
\ell=2
and
s_{1}<\frac{1}{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\|M^{\varepsilon}(t)-M^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H^{-2}(\hat{K})}^{r_{1}}}{|t-\tau|^{s_{1}r_{1}+1}}\,{\rm d}t{\rm d}\tau\big]\leq C(\psi,|\hat{K}|,E_{0,r_{1}})\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{|t-\tau|^{\frac{r_{1}}{2}}}{|t-\tau|^{s_{1}r_{1}+1}}\,{\rm d}t{\rm d}\tau<\infty,

and, by letting
\ell=2
and
\tau=0
,

\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\|M^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{-2}(\hat{K})}^{r_{1}}\,{\rm d}t\big]\leq C(\psi,|\hat{K}|,E_{0,r_{1}},r_{1},T).

Thus,
M^{\varepsilon}(t)
is uniformly bounded in
L^{r_{1}}(\Omega;W^{s_{1},r_{1}}(0,T;H^{-2}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))

when
s_{1}<\frac{1}{2}
.

On the other hand, letting
\tau=0
and choosing
\frac{1}{2}<\ell<1
in (
5.11
),
we have

\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\|M^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{-\ell}(\hat{K})}^{p}\,{\rm d}t\big]\leq C(\psi,|\hat{K}|,E_{0,p},p,T),

which shows that
M^{\varepsilon}(t)
is uniformly bounded in
L^{p}(\Omega;L^{p}(0,T;H^{-\ell}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})))

with
\frac{1}{2}<\ell<1
and
1\leq p<\infty
whenever
E_{0,p}<\infty
.

By the Sobolev imbedding theorem, for
\ell<1
,

H^{-\ell}(\hat{K})\hookrightarrow H^{-1}(\hat{K})\hookrightarrow H^{-2}(\hat{K}),

where
\hat{K}
is any compact subset of
\mathbb{R}
. Moreover, the imbedding
H^{-\ell}(\hat{K})\hookrightarrow H^{-2}(\hat{K})
is compact. Therefore, by the Aubin-Simon lemma
[
40
, Corollary 9]
, the embedding:

L^{2}(0,T;H_{\rm loc}^{-\ell}(\mathbb{R}))\cap W^{\frac{1}{4},2}(0,T;H_{\rm loc}^{-2}(\mathbb{R}))\hookrightarrow L^{2}(0,T;H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))

is compact. The tightness of
M^{\varepsilon}(t)
in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}))
then follows, which implies that
\{\partial_{t}M^{\varepsilon}(t)\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is tight in
H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
.
∎

Remark 5.1
.

To obtain the tightness of
M^{\varepsilon}(t)
, it is essential to note that
\psi\in C_{\rm c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
which is the key point of our estimates here, compared to the proof in
[
3
, Proposition 5.3]
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
be a weak entropy pair generated by

\psi\in C_{\rm c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
as defined
in (
2.13
).
Suppose that
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
with
m^{\varepsilon}=\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}

is a sequence of solutions to the parabolic approximation equations (
3.1
).
Then
\{\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\}_{\varepsilon>0}

is tight in
W^{-1,q_{3}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
for
1<q_{3}<2
.

Proof.

By
(
5.8
),

|\rho^{\varepsilon}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})|\leq C_{\psi}
.
Thus, for any
\hat{K}\Subset\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\frac{1}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(\psi,T,|\hat{K}|)+C(\psi)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\big(\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+e^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t,

where, in the last inequality, we have used (
4.2
).
Then the energy estimate (
3.7
)
again implies that
\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k}(a_{k}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}

is uniformly bounded in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{1}([0,T]\times\hat{K}))
.
Therefore, by the Sobolev compact embedding:
L^{1}\hookrightarrow W^{-1,q_{3}}
for any
q_{3}\in(1,2)
,
we obtain that
\{\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is tight in
W^{-1,q_{3}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
for
1<q_{3}<2
.
∎

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
be a weak entropy pair generated by

\psi\in C_{\rm c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
as defined
in (
2.13
).
Suppose that
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
with
m^{\varepsilon}=\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}

is a sequence of solutions to the parabolic approximation equations (
3.1
). Then,
for the general pressure law case,
\{\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{t}+q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{x}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is tight in

W^{-1,p_{0}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
with
1\leq p_{0}<2
.

Proof.

For the general pressure law case, by
[
11
, Lemmas 4.5, 4.7, and 4.10]
, we have

|\eta^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho,\qquad|q^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}(\rho+\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}).

Thus, by Proposition
3.1
and (
2.21
),
we obtain that
\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
and
q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
are uniformly bounded in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{2}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}))
.
Thus,
\{\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{t}+q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{x}\}_{\varepsilon>0}

is stochastically bounded in
H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
(see Lemma
A.9
).

On the other hand, by Theorem
3.1
(iii),
we apply the Itô formula to
\eta^{\psi}
to obtain

\displaystyle{\rm d}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{x}q^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}t-\varepsilon(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\,\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\,\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\Phi^{\varepsilon}(U^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}W(t)+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,{\rm d}t.

It follows from Lemmas
5.2
and
5.4

that
\{\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\}_{\varepsilon>0}
and

\{\partial_{t}M^{\varepsilon}(t)\}_{\varepsilon>0}
are tight in
H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
.
Lemmas
5.3
and
5.5
imply that

\{\varepsilon(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\,\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\,\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\{\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\}_{\varepsilon>0}

are tight in
W^{-1,q}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
for all
q\in(1,2)
.
These also imply

\{\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{t}+q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{x}\}_{\varepsilon>0}\qquad\text{are tight in $W^{-1,q_{0}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$ with $1\leq q_{0}\leq q$}.

(5.12)

Therefore, our conclusion follows from Lemma
A.9
and (
5.12
).
∎

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\tilde{\mu}
be the limit random Young measure obtained in

Proposition
4.3
.
Then,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s. for a.e.
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
,

\overline{\chi(s_{1})\sigma(s_{2})-\chi(s_{2})\sigma(s_{1})}=\overline{\chi(s_{1})}\,\overline{\sigma(s_{2})}-\overline{\chi(s_{2})}\,\overline{\sigma(s_{1})}\qquad\mbox{for any $\,s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{R}$}.

(5.13)

Proof.

For the general pressure law case, from
[
11
, Lemmas 4.5, 4.7, and 4.10]
, we have

|\eta^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho,\qquad|q^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}\big(\rho+\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}\big).

(5.14)

It is clear that
{\rm supp}(\eta^{\psi}),\,{\rm supp}(q^{\psi})\subset\{(\rho,m)\,:\,\frac{m}{\rho}+\mathcal{K}(\rho)\geq a_{*},\ \frac{m}{\rho}-\mathcal{K}(\rho)\leq a^{*}\}
,
where
a_{*}
and
a^{*}
depend only on the support of
\psi
.
Moreover, by the representation of the entropy pairs (
2.13
),

\eta^{\psi}(0,m)=q^{\psi}(0,m)=0
since
\psi\in C_{\rm c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.
Therefore, in the following proof, if necessary,
we always use the fact that, with
\phi=\eta^{\psi}
or
q^{\psi}
, and
\nu\in\{\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{\mu},\mu^{\varepsilon}\}
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\nu_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)=\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\phi(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\nu_{(t,x)}(\rho,m).

For any
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in C_{\rm c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
,

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}:=(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m),\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}q^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)),

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}:=(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}q^{\psi_{2}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m),\ -\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta^{\psi_{2}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m))

are well-defined on
[0,T]\times\hat{K}
for any
T>0

and any
\hat{K}\Subset\mathbb{R}

by (
5.5
).
Applying Proposition
5.1
(ii) with
\beta=\frac{1}{2}
,
we obtain that, for any
\varphi\in L^{2}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathbb{R}^{2})
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}(\tilde{X}_{i}^{\varepsilon}-\tilde{X}_{i})\varphi(t,x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big|\big]=0\qquad\mbox{for $i=1,2$},

where

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{1}:=(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m),\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}q^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m))=(\overline{\eta^{\psi_{1}}},\ \overline{q^{\psi_{1}}})

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{2}:=(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}q^{\psi_{2}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m),\ -\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta^{\psi_{2}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m))=(\overline{q^{\psi_{2}}},\ -\overline{\eta^{\psi_{2}}}).

Therefore, for each
\varphi\in L^{2}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathbb{R}^{2})
, up to a subsequence, we have

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}(\tilde{X}_{i}^{\varepsilon}-\tilde{X}_{i})\varphi(t,x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t=0\qquad\mbox{for $i=1,2,$}\quad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}

Since
L^{2}
is separable, there exists a further subsequence (without changing the notation) such that

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}(\tilde{X}_{i}^{\varepsilon}-\tilde{X}_{i})\varphi(t,x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t=0\qquad\mbox{for any $\varphi\in L^{2}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathbb{R}^{2})$}\quad\mbox{for $i=1,2$,}\,\,\,\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}},

which implies that

\tilde{X}_{i}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\tilde{X}_{i}\ \qquad\text{in}\ L^{2}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathbb{R}^{2})\quad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}\,\,\,\mbox{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

(5.15)

Notice that, for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
,

\mu^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)=\delta_{(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})}
and
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\in L^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}(\Omega;L^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}))

by Proposition
4.2
,
which implies that
\mu^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)

is an
L^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}
-random Dirac mass (see
[
3
, Definition 4.2]
).
Thus, utilizing
[
3
, Proposition 4.5]
and
the equality of laws from Proposition
4.3
,

\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)
is also an
L^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}
-random Dirac mass,
which enables us to calculate

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}q^{\psi_{2}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta^{\psi_{2}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}q^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\big(\eta^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)q^{\psi_{2}}(\rho,m)-\eta^{\psi_{2}}(\rho,m)q^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)\big)\,\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m).

(5.16)

By
(
5.14
), we have

|\eta^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)q^{\psi_{2}}(\rho,m)-\eta^{\psi_{2}}(\rho,m)q^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho^{\beta_{0}(\gamma_{2}+1)}

(5.17)

with
\beta_{0}=\frac{2+\bar{\theta}}{\gamma_{2}+1}<1
.
Thus, by similar arguments as in the justification of (
5.15
) above, we have

\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\,\rightharpoonup\,\overline{\eta^{\psi_{1}}q^{\psi_{2}}-\eta^{\psi_{2}}q^{\psi_{1}}}\qquad\text{in}\ L^{\frac{1}{\beta_{0}}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\quad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}\,\,\,\mbox{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

(5.18)

On the other hand, we claim that
{\rm div}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}
and
\text{curl}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
are tight in
W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
and
W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2})
, respectively.
Denote

\displaystyle X_{1}^{\varepsilon}:=(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\mu^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m),\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}q^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\mu^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)),

\displaystyle X_{2}^{\varepsilon}:=(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}q^{\psi_{2}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\mu^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m),\ -\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta^{\psi_{2}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\mu^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)).

Proposition
5.2
implies that

{\rm div}_{(t,x)}\,X_{1}^{\varepsilon}
and

\text{curl}_{(t,x)}\,X_{2}^{\varepsilon}
are tight in
W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})

and
W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2})
respectively.
Therefore, to prove the claim, it suffices to prove that
\mathcal{L}({\rm div}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon})=\mathcal{L}({\rm div}_{(t,x)}\,X_{1}^{\varepsilon})
in
W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
and
\mathcal{L}(\text{curl}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon})=\mathcal{L}(\text{curl}_{(t,x)}\,X_{2}^{\varepsilon})
in
W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2})
. By Lemma 2.1.4 in
[
6
]
, it suffices to verify

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\varphi\,{\rm div}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\varphi\,{\rm div}_{(t,x)}\,X_{1}^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big],

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\hat{\varphi}\,\text{curl}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\hat{\varphi}\,\text{curl}_{(t,x)}\,X_{2}^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big],

for any
T>0
, any
\hat{K}\Subset\mathbb{R}
,
and any
\varphi\in W^{1,\infty}_{0}([0,T]\times\hat{K})
and

\hat{\varphi}\in W^{1,\infty}_{0}([0,T]\times\hat{K};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2})
.

To do this, let
w_{k}(\rho,m)\geq 0
be a continuous cut-off function such that
w_{k}\equiv 1

on the set
\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\frac{1}{k}\leq\mathcal{K}(\rho)\leq k,\ |m|\leq k\}

and
w_{k}\equiv 0
outside the set
\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\frac{1}{2k}\leq\mathcal{K}(\rho)\leq 2k,\ |m|\leq 2k\}
.
Then
\eta^{\psi_{i}}(\rho,m)w_{k}(\rho,m),\,q^{\psi_{i}}(\rho,m)w_{k}(\rho,m)\in C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
for
i=1,2
.
Thus, it follows from the equality of laws
in Proposition
4.3

that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)w_{k}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}q^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)w_{k}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)\partial_{x}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)w_{k}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\mu^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}q^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)w_{k}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\mu^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)\partial_{x}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big].

Utilizing (
5.5
),
Proposition
3.1
, and the dominated convergence theorem, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\to\infty}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)w_{k}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)w_{k}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\mu^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\mu^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big].

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}q^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)\partial_{x}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}q^{\psi_{1}}(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\mu^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)\partial_{x}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big].

Therefore, integrating by parts implies

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\varphi\,{\rm div}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\varphi\,{\rm div}_{(t,x)}\,X_{1}^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big].

Similarly, we have

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\hat{\varphi}\,\text{curl}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\hat{\varphi}\,\text{curl}_{(t,x)}\,X_{2}^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big].

These prove our claim that

{\rm div}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}
and
\text{curl}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}

are tight in
W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
and
W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2})
,
respectively.

Since
{\rm div}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}
and
\text{curl}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
are tight
in
W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
and
W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2})
respectively,
we see that, for any
\beta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
,
there exist compact subsets
K_{\beta}\Subset W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})

and
\bar{K}_{\beta}\subset W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2})
such that

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\big\{\omega\,:\,{\rm div}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\in K_{\beta}\,\,\text{and}\,\,\text{curl}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\in\bar{K}_{\beta}\big\}>1-\beta.

Denote
A_{\beta}:=\{\omega\,:\,{\rm div}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\in K_{\beta}\,\,\text{and}\,\,\text{curl}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\in\bar{K}_{\beta}\}
.

The tightness of
{\rm div}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}
and
\text{curl}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
is
weaker than that required in
[
37
]
,
so we need an improvement of the classical div-curl lemma (see Lemma
A.12
below).
Therefore, we need to prove additionally that
\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
is equi-integrable.
By (
5.5
) and
(
5.16
)–(
5.17
),
we obtain

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\big|\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\big|^{\frac{1}{\beta_{0}}}{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\leq{\hat{C}}

for any
T>0
and any

\hat{K}\Subset\mathbb{R}
.
Thus, by the Chebyshev inequality,
for any
0<\beta<\frac{1}{2}
, there exists a constant
R_{\beta}>0
such that

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\big\{\omega\,:\,\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\big|\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\big|^{\frac{1}{\beta_{0}}}{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\leq R_{\beta}\big\}>1-\beta.

Then, restricted to the event
B_{\beta}:=\big\{\omega\,:\,\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\big|\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\big|^{\frac{1}{\beta_{0}}}{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\leq R_{\beta}\big\}
,
since
\frac{1}{\beta_{0}}>1
, we see that

\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
is equi-integrable uniformly in
\varepsilon
. Therefore,
restricted to the event
A_{\beta}\cap B_{\beta}
(note that
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\{A_{\beta}\cap B_{\beta}\}>0
), by Lemma
A.12
, we obtain

\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\tilde{X}_{1}\cdot\tilde{X}_{2}=\overline{\eta^{\psi_{1}}}\,\overline{q^{\psi_{2}}}-\overline{q^{\psi_{1}}}\,\overline{\eta^{\psi_{2}}}\qquad\text{in}\ \mathcal{D}^{\prime}.

(5.19)

Combining (
5.18
) with (
5.19
) implies that

\overline{\eta^{\psi_{1}}}\,\overline{q^{\psi_{2}}}-\overline{q^{\psi_{1}}}\,\overline{\eta^{\psi_{2}}}=\overline{\eta^{\psi_{1}}q^{\psi_{2}}-\eta^{\psi_{2}}q^{\psi_{1}}}\qquad\text{in}\ \mathcal{D}^{\prime}\cap L^{\frac{1}{\beta_{0}}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}),

(5.20)

and particularly, in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\cap L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
. Therefore,
equality (
5.20
) holds pointwise almost everywhere in
\left[0,\infty\right)\times\mathbb{R}
.

Choose a decreasing sequence
\{\beta_{n}\}
satisfying
\beta_{n}<\frac{1}{2}
with
\beta_{n}\to 0
and
an increasing sequence
\{\mathcal{B}_{\beta_{n}}\}
with
\mathcal{B}_{\beta_{n}}:=A_{\beta_{n}}\cap B_{\beta_{n}}
.
Denoting
\mathcal{B}:=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{B}_{\beta_{n}}
, then
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{B})=1
.
Equality (
5.20
) holds on
\mathcal{B}
, hence
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely and pointwise almost everywhere in
\left[0,\infty\right)\times\mathbb{R}
. Recall the representation of
weak entropy pairs in (
2.13
), by arbitrariness of test functions
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
,
we obtain that, for
a.e.

(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
, the Tartar commutation (
5.13
)
holds
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

If
\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}
is the random Young measure
obtained in
Proposition
4.3
,
then

\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}=\delta_{(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})}
.

Proof.

From the proof of Lemma
5.6
,
we already know that
\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}
is an
L^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}
-random Dirac mass.
We first assume
\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}=\delta_{(\hat{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\hat{m}^{\varepsilon})}
.
Then
(\hat{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\hat{m}^{\varepsilon})
are

L^{\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{2}+1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
-valued random variables
and hence also
L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
-valued random variables.

Since
\mu^{\varepsilon}=\delta_{(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})}
, we see that, for
\phi(\rho,m)=\rho
or
m
,

\mathbb{P}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi w_{k}\,{\rm d}\mu^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}\Big)\varphi\,{\rm d}t{\rm d}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})w_{k}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}t{\rm d}x\,\,\,\,\mbox{for any $\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}\Big\}=1,

where
w_{k}(\rho,m)\geq 0
is a continuous cut-off function such that
w_{k}\equiv 1

on the set
\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\frac{1}{k}\leq\mathcal{K}(\rho)\leq k,|m|\leq k\}

and
w_{k}\equiv 0
outside the
set
\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\frac{1}{2k}\leq\mathcal{K}(\rho)\leq 2k,|m|\leq 2k\}
.
Then, by the equality of laws from Proposition
4.3
, we have

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi w_{k}\,{\rm d}\mu^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}\Big)\varphi\,{\rm d}t{\rm d}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})w_{k}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}t{\rm d}x\,\,\,\,\mbox{for any $\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\varphi\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi w_{k}\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}\,{\rm d}t{\rm d}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})w_{k}(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}t{\rm d}x\,\,\,\,\mbox{for any $\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}\Big\},

which implies that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\big(\phi(\hat{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\hat{m}^{\varepsilon})w_{k}(\hat{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\hat{m}^{\varepsilon})-\phi(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})w_{k}(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})\big)\,\varphi\,{\rm d}t{\rm d}x=0\qquad\mbox{for any $\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}\,\,\,\,\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

Notice that

\phi(\hat{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\hat{m}^{\varepsilon})w_{k}(\hat{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\hat{m}^{\varepsilon})-\phi(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})w_{k}(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})\in L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
,
so that

\phi(\hat{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\hat{m}^{\varepsilon})w_{k}(\hat{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\hat{m}^{\varepsilon})-\phi(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})w_{k}(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})=0

holds
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
pointwise almost everywhere
in
\left[0,\infty\right)\times\mathbb{R}
.
Letting
k\to\infty
gives

(\hat{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\hat{m}^{\varepsilon})=(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
almost everywhere in
\left[0,\infty\right)\times\mathbb{R}
. Thus, the conclusion follows.
∎

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
\tilde{\mu}
be the limit random Young measure obtained
in
Proposition
4.3
.
Then it holds
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s. that, for a.e.
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
,
either
\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}
is concentrated on the vacuum region
\mathcal{V}:=\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\rho=0\}
or
\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}
is reduced to a Dirac mass
\delta_{(\rho^{*}(t,x,\omega),m^{*}(t,x,\omega))}
on the region
\mathfrak{T}=\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\rho>0\}
.

Proof.

If
\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}
is not concentrated on the vacuum region

\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\rho=0\}
,
we fix
(t,x,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}\times\Omega
such that
the Tartar commutation (
5.13
) is satisfied. Then
we use
[
11
, Theorem 8.5]
to obtain that
\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}
is reduced to a Dirac mass
\delta_{(\rho^{*}(t,x,\omega),m^{*}(t,x,\omega))}
on the region
\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\rho>0\}
.
∎

Remark 5.2
.

Notice that all the entropy pairs under our consideration are weak entropy pairs, i.e.,
they vanish at the vacuum. Moreover, in the vacuum, the entropy kernel
\chi(\rho=0)=0
.

We now give a generalized version of Proposition
5.1

without proof, since it can be proved similarly as Proposition
5.1
.

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}
and
\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}

be the random Young measures obtained in
Proposition
4.3
.
Then

(i)

For
\nu_{(t,x)}\in\{\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}\}_{\varepsilon>0}\cup\{\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}\}
,
there exists some
p_{0}>1
such that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\big(\rho P(\rho)+\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\big)\,{\rm d}\nu_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big)^{p_{0}}\big]\leq{\hat{C}}(p_{0}).

(5.21)

(ii)

Let
p_{0}>1
be as in
(i)
above.
Let
\phi(t,x,\rho,m;\omega)\in L^{p_{0}}(\Omega,L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};C(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})))

satisfy that
\phi(t,x,0,m;\omega)=0
and, for any
\delta>0
, there exists
C_{\delta}>0
such that,
for a.e.
(t,x,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\times\tilde{\Omega}
,

|\phi(t,x,\rho,m;\omega)|\leq C_{\delta}+h(t,x,\omega)+\delta\big(\rho^{\gamma_{2}+1}+\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\big)\qquad\mbox{for large $\rho$ and $|m|$},

where
h(t,x;\omega)\in L^{p_{0}}(\Omega;L^{q_{0}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}))
with
q_{0}>1
.
Then, for any compact subset
\hat{K}\Subset\mathbb{R}
and any
T>0
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi(t,x,\rho,m;\omega)\,{\rm d}(\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}-\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)})(\rho,m)\Big)\varphi(t,x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big|\big]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow 0\qquad\,\,\mbox{
as $\varepsilon\to 0\quad$ for any $\varphi\in L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}.

(5.22)

Corollary 5.1
.

Let
(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})
and
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})

be the random variables, and let
\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}
be the limit random Young measure obtained
in
Proposition
4.3
. Then

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\rho\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)},\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}m\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)})=(\tilde{\rho}(t,x),\,\tilde{m}(t,x))\qquad\,\mbox{almost everywhere}.

(5.23)

In particular,

(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})=(0,0)

(5.24)

on the set
\{(t,x,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\times\tilde{\Omega}\,:\,{\rm supp}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}=\mathcal{V}\}
,
while

(\rho^{*}(t,x,\omega),\,m^{*}(t,x,\omega)=(\tilde{\rho}(t,x,\omega),\,\tilde{m}(t,x,\omega))

(5.25)

on the set
\{(t,x,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\times\tilde{\Omega}\,:\,\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}=\delta_{(\rho^{*}(t,x,\omega),m^{*}(t,x,\omega))},\ \rho^{*}(t,x,\omega)>0\}
.
Furthermore, there exists a function
\tilde{u}
such that
\tilde{m}=\tilde{\rho}\tilde{u}
and
\tilde{u}=0
on the set
\{(t,x,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\times\tilde{\Omega}\,:\,\tilde{\rho}=0\}
.
In addition,

(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\,\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})\to(\tilde{\rho},\,\tilde{m})\qquad\mbox{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-{\it a.s.} almost everywhere}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1
. Let

\phi(\rho,m)=\rho
or
m
. Note that
\phi(\rho,m)
is a natural weak entropy,
which vanishes at the vacuum, so
\phi
satisfies the conditions in
Proposition
5.4
(ii). Therefore,
we apply Proposition
5.4
(ii) to obtain that,
for any
\varphi\in L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}(\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}-\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)})(\rho,m)\Big)\varphi(t,x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big|\big]=0.

(5.26)

As in the proof of Proposition
5.1
(ii),
we can also extend the above convergence.
More specifically, since
|m|^{\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{\gamma_{2}+3}}\leq\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}+\rho^{\gamma_{2}+1}
,
by Proposition
5.4
(i) and the same arguments as
the justification of (
5.3
), we see that
the convergence (
5.26
) also holds
for any
\varphi\in L^{q_{0}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})

with
q_{0}=\max\{\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{2\gamma_{2}},\frac{\gamma_{2}+1}{\gamma_{2}}\}>1
.
Hence, by Lemma
5.7
,
for each
\varphi\in L^{q_{0}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
, up to a subsequence,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\Big(\phi(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)\Big)\varphi(t,x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t=0\qquad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

Since
L^{q_{0}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
is separable, there exists a further subsequence (without changing the notation)
such that, for any
\varphi\in L^{q_{0}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\Big(\phi(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)\Big)\varphi(t,x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t=0\qquad\,\,\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

On the other hand, by Proposition
4.3
,
we know that
(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})\to(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.

in
\mathcal{X}_{\rho}\times\mathcal{X}_{m}
.
Then we obtain that, for any
\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\big(\phi(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})-\phi(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})\big)\varphi(t,x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t=0\qquad\,\,\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

Therefore, it follows from the uniqueness of the limit that, for any
\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
,

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)-\phi(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})\Big)\varphi(t,x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t=0\qquad\,\,\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi(\rho,m)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)-\phi(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})\in L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
, we obtain

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\rho\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)},\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}m\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)})=(\tilde{\rho}(t,x),\,\tilde{m}(t,x))\qquad\,\,\mbox{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-{\it a.s.} almost everywhere}.

Note that, on the set
