\{(t,x,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\times\tilde{\Omega}\,:\,{\rm supp}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}=\mathcal{V}\}
,
the weak entropies vanish on the vacuum so that

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\rho\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)},\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}m\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)})=(0,0)=(\tilde{\rho}(t,x,\omega),\tilde{m}(t,x,\omega)).

On the set
\{(t,x,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\times\tilde{\Omega}\,:\,\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}=\delta_{(\rho^{*}(t,x,\omega),m^{*}(t,x,\omega))},\ \rho^{*}(t,x,\omega)>0\}
, we obtain (
5.25
).
Thus, if we define
\tilde{u}:=0
on the set

\{(t,x,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\times\tilde{\Omega}\,:\,\tilde{\rho}=0\}

and
\tilde{u}:=\frac{\tilde{m}}{\tilde{\rho}}

on the set
\{(t,x,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\times\Omega\,:\,\tilde{\rho}>0\}
,
then
\tilde{m}=\tilde{\rho}\tilde{u}\,\,

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
almost everywhere.

2
. Now we apply Proposition
5.4
(ii)
to prove that
(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})\to(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
almost everywhere. To do this, let
\phi(t,x,\rho,m;\omega)=|\rho-\tilde{\rho}(t,x,\omega)|
or
|m-\tilde{m}(t,x,\omega)|
, then
\phi(t,x,\rho,m;\omega)
satisfies the conditions in Proposition
5.4
(ii)
by using (
5.24
), again the fact
|m|^{\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{\gamma_{2}+3}}\leq\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}+\rho^{\gamma_{2}+1}
, and (
5.21
).
Thus, (
5.22
) yields that,
for any

\hat{K}\Subset\mathbb{R}
and for any
T>0
,

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi(t,x,\rho,m;\omega)\,{\rm d}\big(\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}-\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}\big)(\rho,m)\Big)\varphi(t,x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big|\big]\to 0\qquad\mbox{as $\varepsilon\to 0$},

(5.27)

for any
\varphi\in L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
.
Moreover, as the arguments in Step
1
above,
we can also extend this convergence to be satisfied by a more general

\varphi\in L^{q_{0}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
with
q_{0}=\max\{\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{2\gamma_{2}},\frac{\gamma_{2}+1}{\gamma_{2}}\}>1
.
Similarly, the separability of
L^{q_{0}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
enables us to obtain that, for any
\varphi\in L^{q_{0}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
,

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
(up to a subsequence),

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi(t,x,\rho,m;\omega)\,{\rm d}\big(\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}-\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}\big)(\rho,m)\Big)\varphi(t,x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\to 0\qquad\mbox{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

Choosing
\varphi\equiv 1
and noting by
(
5.24
)–(
5.25
) that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\phi(t,x,\rho,m;\omega)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m)=0,

we obtain that, as
\varepsilon\to 0
,

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}|\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}-\tilde{\rho}|\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}|\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}-\tilde{m}|\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\to 0\qquad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}},

which implies that there exists a further subsequence (without changing the notation) such that
(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})\to(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})\,\,

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
almost everywhere.
∎

6
Martingale Solutions:
Proof of Theorems
2.3
–

2.4

We are now ready to prove our main results: Theorem
2.3
and Theorem
2.4
.

6.1
The Entropy Inequality for Global Martingale Solutions

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})
and
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})
be the random variables,
and let
\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}
and
\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}
be
the random Young measures obtained
in
Proposition
4.3
. Then,
for
p\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C(T,E_{0,p})>0
such that

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\tilde{m}^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}}+e(\tilde{\rho},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x\Big\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T])}^{p}\big]\leq C(T,E_{0,p}).

Proof.

From (
3.7
), we obtain that, for any
t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
,

\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+e^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{p}\big]\leq(t_{2}-t_{1})^{p}\,C(T,E_{0,p}).

Let
\tilde{w}_{k}(\rho,m)\geq 0
be a continuous cut-off function such that
\tilde{w}_{k}\equiv 1

on the set
\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\mathcal{K}(\rho)\leq k,\ |m|\leq k\}

and
\tilde{w}_{k}\equiv 0
outside the set
\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\mathcal{K}(\rho)\leq 2k,\ |m|\leq 2k\}
.
Let
B_{n}\subset\mathbb{R}
be an open ball centered at the origin with radius
n
.
Then, by the equality of laws from
Proposition
4.3
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{B_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{m^{2}}{\rho}+e^{*}(\rho,\rho_{\infty})\big)\tilde{w}_{k}\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}^{\varepsilon}_{(t,x)}{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{p}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{B_{n}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+e^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho_{\infty})\big)\tilde{w}_{k}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C(T,E_{0,p})(t_{2}-t_{1})^{p}.

Thus, by the monotone convergence theorem
and the fact that the weak entropies vanish at the vacuum, we obtain that,
for any
t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
,

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{B_{n}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}}+e^{*}(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{p}\big]\leq C(T,E_{0,p})(t_{2}-t_{1})^{p}.

(6.1)

By Corollary
5.1
,
we know that
\tilde{m}=0
on the set
\{(t,x,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\times\tilde{\Omega}\,:\,\tilde{\rho}=0\}
and
(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})\to(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
almost everywhere, which implies that
\frac{(\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}}\to\frac{\tilde{m}^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}}
almost everywhere on the set
\{(t,x,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\times\tilde{\Omega}\,:\,\tilde{\rho}>0\}

and
e^{*}(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\rho_{\infty})\to e^{*}(\tilde{\rho},\rho_{\infty})
almost everywhere. Therefore, it follows from Fatou’s lemma that, for any
t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
,

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{B_{n}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\tilde{m}^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}}+e^{*}(\tilde{\rho},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{p}\big]\leq C(T,E_{0,p})(t_{2}-t_{1})^{p},

which implies, by Fatou’s lemma again, that, for almost every
t\in[0,T]
,

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big(\int_{B_{n}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\tilde{m}^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}}+e^{*}(\tilde{\rho},\rho_{\infty})\big)(t)\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big]\leq C(T,E_{0,p}).

Then, by the monotone convergence theorem, we have

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\tilde{m}^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}}+e^{*}(\tilde{\rho},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x\Big\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T])}^{p}\big]\leq C(T,E_{0,p}).

∎

In the general pressure law case, the explicit formula for the entropies is unavailable,
which makes it difficult to calculate the entropies and their derivatives.
We first recall the asymptotic expansions for the entropy kernel
\chi(\rho,u)
and the entropy flux
kernel
\sigma(\rho,u)
when
\rho
is bounded.

Lemma 6.1

(
[
11
]
, Lemmas 4.2–4.3)
.

The entropy kernel
\chi(\rho,u)
and entropy flux kernel
\sigma(\rho,u)
admit the expansion
:
for
\rho\in\left[0,\infty\right)
,

\displaystyle\chi(\rho,u)=\hat{f}_{1}(\rho)\hat{F}_{\lambda_{1}}(\rho,u)+\hat{f}_{2}(\rho)\hat{F}_{\lambda_{1}+1}(\rho,u)+a_{1}(\rho,u),

\displaystyle(\sigma-u\chi)(\rho,u)=-u\big(\hat{g}_{1}(\rho)\hat{F}_{\lambda_{1}}(\rho,u)+\hat{g}_{2}(\rho)\hat{F}_{\lambda_{1}+1}(\rho,u)\big)+a_{2}(\rho,u),

where

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}=\frac{3-\gamma_{1}}{2(\gamma_{1}-1)}>0,\qquad\,\hat{F}_{\lambda}(\rho,u)=[\mathcal{K}(\rho)^{2}-u^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}\,\,\,\,\,\mbox{for any $\lambda>0$}.

The support of entropy kernel
{\rm supp}(\chi(\rho,u))\subset\{(\rho,u):|u|\leq\mathcal{K}(\rho)\}
,
and
\chi(\rho,u)>0
in
\{(\rho,u):|u|<\mathcal{K}(\rho)\}
.
The remainder terms
a_{1}(\rho,u)
and
a_{2}(\rho,u)
are Hölder continuous.
Moreover, for any fixed
\rho_{\rm max}>0
, there exist
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)

and
C(\rho_{\rm max})>0
depending only on
\rho_{\rm max}
such that,
for any
\rho\in[0,\rho_{\rm\max}]
,

|a_{1}(\rho,u-s)|+|a_{2}(\rho,u-s)|\leq C(\rho_{\rm max})[\mathcal{K}(\rho)^{2}-(u-s)^{2}]^{\lambda_{1}+\delta_{0}+1}_{+}.

Furthermore, for any
\rho\in[0,\rho_{\rm\max}]
,

|\hat{f}_{1}(\rho)|+|\hat{f}_{2}(\rho)|+|\hat{g}_{1}(\rho)|+|\hat{g}_{2}(\rho)|\leq C(\rho_{\rm max}).

The next lemma shows what the growth of the generating function in the entropy is allowed
in the general pressure law case.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
\psi\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
be convex and satisfy

|\psi(s)|\leq C|s|^{2-\delta},\qquad|\psi^{\prime}(s)|\leq C|s|^{1-\delta}.

Then, when
\rho\geq\rho^{\star}
and
\delta\geq\frac{3}{2}\big(1-\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}}\big)
,

\displaystyle|\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+|q^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+\frac{1}{\rho}|\partial_{u}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|^{2}+\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}|\partial_{u}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\sum_{k}\zeta_{k}^{2}\leq C\big(1+\rho|u|^{3}+\rho P(\rho)\big),

and, when
\rho\leq\rho^{\star}
and
\delta\geq 0
,

|\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+|q^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+\frac{1}{\rho}|\partial_{u}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|^{2}+\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}|\partial_{u}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\sum_{k}\zeta_{k}^{2}\leq C(\rho^{\star})\big(1+\rho|u|^{3}+\rho P(\rho)\big).

Proof.

The proof can be done by direct
calculation. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\mathcal{K}(\rho)}^{\mathcal{K}(\rho)}\chi(\rho,s)\psi(u-s)\,{\rm d}s,

\displaystyle q^{\psi}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\mathcal{K}(\rho)}^{\mathcal{K}(\rho)}\sigma(\rho,s)\psi(u-s)\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=\int_{-\mathcal{K}(\rho)}^{\mathcal{K}(\rho)}\big(\sigma-u\chi\big)(\rho,s)\psi(u-s)\,{\rm d}s+u\int_{-\mathcal{K}(\rho)}^{\mathcal{K}(\rho)}\chi(\rho,s)\psi(u-s)\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle:=g^{\psi}(\rho,u)+u\eta^{\psi},

where we have used the fact that

{\rm supp}(\chi(\rho,u))\cup{\rm supp}(\sigma(\rho,u))\subset\{(\rho,u):|u|\leq\mathcal{K}(\rho)\}

(see
[
12
, Theorem 2.1]
).

Then, using the following bounds (see
[
11
, Lemma 4.6 and 4.9]
):

\|\chi(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{u}}\leq C\rho,\quad\|\big(\sigma-u\chi\big)(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{u}}\leq C\rho^{1+\theta_{2}},

we obtain that, when
\rho\geq\rho^{\star}
,

\displaystyle|\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C\big(\rho^{1+\theta_{2}}|u|^{2-\delta}+\rho^{1+(3-\delta)\theta_{2}}+\rho^{\gamma_{2}}|u|^{1-\delta}\big),

\displaystyle|q^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C\big(\rho^{\gamma_{2}}|u|^{2-\delta}+\rho^{(4-\delta)\theta_{2}+1}+\rho^{3\theta_{2}+1}|u|^{1-\delta}+\rho^{\theta_{2}+1}|u|^{3-\delta}+\rho^{\gamma_{2}}|u|^{2-\delta}\big),

\displaystyle|\partial_{u}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C\big(\rho^{\theta_{2}+1}|u|^{1-\delta}+\rho^{(2-\delta)\theta_{2}+1}\big),

\displaystyle|\partial_{u}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C\big(\rho|u|^{1-\delta}+\rho^{(1-\delta)\theta_{2}+1}\big).

We now take
\partial_{u}^{2}\eta^{\psi}
for an example, since the other estimates are similar.
Since
\psi
is convex, we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{u}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{-\mathcal{K}(\rho)}^{\mathcal{K}(\rho)}\chi(\rho,s)\psi^{\prime\prime}(u-s)\,{\rm d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\|\chi(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{u}}\big|\psi^{\prime}(u-\mathcal{K}(\rho))-\psi^{\prime}(u+\mathcal{K}(\rho))\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\rho\big(|u|+\mathcal{K}(\rho)\big)^{1-\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\rho|u|^{1-\delta}+\rho^{(1-\delta)\theta_{2}+1}\big),

where, in the last inequality,
we have used (
2.23
). Then the conclusion for
\rho\geq\rho^{\star}
follows easily.

When
\rho
is bounded, let
z=\frac{s}{\mathcal{K}(\rho)}
.
By Lemma
6.1
, we have

\displaystyle\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)

\displaystyle\leq C(\rho_{\rm max})\mathcal{K}(\rho)^{2\lambda_{1}+1}\int_{-1}^{1}|\psi(u-\mathcal{K}(\rho)z)|[1-z^{2}]^{\lambda_{1}}_{+}(\cdots)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq C(\rho_{\rm max})\big(\rho|u|^{2-\delta}+\rho^{\gamma_{1}-\frac{\delta}{2}(\gamma_{1}-1)}+\rho^{\theta_{1}}|u|^{1-\delta}\big),

where
(\cdots)=1+\mathcal{K}(\rho)^{2}[1-z^{2}]_{+}+\mathcal{K}(\rho)^{2\alpha_{0}+2}[1-z^{2}]^{\alpha_{0}+1}_{+}\leq C(\rho_{\rm max})

and, in the second inequality, we have used (
2.20
).
Similarly, using the convexity of
\psi
, we obtain

\displaystyle|q^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C(\rho_{\rm max})\big(\rho|u|^{2-\delta}+\rho^{\gamma_{1}-\frac{\delta}{2}(\gamma_{1}-1)}+\rho^{\theta_{1}}|u|^{1-\delta}+\rho|u|^{3-\delta}+\rho^{\gamma_{1}-\frac{\delta}{2}(\gamma_{1}-1)}|u|+\rho^{\theta_{1}}|u|^{2-\delta}\big),

\displaystyle|\partial_{u}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C(\rho_{\rm max})\big(\rho|u|^{1-\delta}+\rho^{(1-\delta)\theta_{1}+1}\big),

\displaystyle|\partial_{u}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\leq C(\rho_{\rm max})\big(\rho|u|^{1-\delta}+\rho^{(1-\delta)\theta_{1}+1}\big).

Then the conclusion for
\rho\leq\rho^{\star}
follows after a direct calculation.
∎

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
(\eta,q)=(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
be the entropy pair defined
in (
2.13
) with convex generating
function
\psi\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying (
2.33
).
Then, for any nonnegative
\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R})
, the following entropy inequality holds
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\eta(\tilde{U})\varphi_{t}+q(\tilde{U})\varphi_{x}\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})\Phi(\tilde{U})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tilde{W}(t)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\geq 0,

where
\tilde{U}=(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})^{\top}
.

Proof.

By the Itô formula, the solution
U^{\varepsilon}
of the parabolic approximation (
3.1
) satisfy
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
the following entropy balance equation:

{\rm d}\eta(U^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{x}q(U^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}t=\varepsilon\nabla\eta(U^{\varepsilon})\partial_{x}^{2}U^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}t+\partial_{m}\eta(U^{\varepsilon})\Phi^{\varepsilon}(U^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}W(t)+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta(U^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,{\rm d}t,

which implies that, for any
\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R})
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\eta(U^{\varepsilon})\varphi_{t}+q(U^{\varepsilon})\varphi_{x})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta(U^{\varepsilon})\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}\eta(U^{\varepsilon})\Phi^{\varepsilon}(U^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}W(t)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta(U^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}(U^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\,\nabla^{2}\eta(U^{\varepsilon})\,\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\quad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

Since the entropy
\eta
is convex, then, for any
\varphi\geq 0
, we have

\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\,\nabla^{2}\eta(U^{\varepsilon})\,\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\geq 0.

Therefore, by the equality of laws from Proposition
4.3

and applying
[
6
, Theorem 2.9.1]
for the stochastic integral, we obtain that,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
for any nonnegative

\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R})
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\varphi_{t}+q(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\varphi_{x})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}W(t)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\geq 0,

where
\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon}=(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})^{\top}
.

We now perform the limit
\varepsilon\to 0
.
Recall that the entropy pair
(\eta,q)
vanishes at the vacuum.
On the set
\{(t,x,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\times\tilde{\Omega}\,:\,\tilde{\rho}>0\}
,
by Lemma
6.2
,
(
5.21
) provides the equi-integrability
of
(\eta,q)(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})
for the entropy
pair
(\eta,q)(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})
with generating function
\psi

satisfying (
2.33
).
This equi-integrability, the
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
almost everywhere convergence in Corollary
5.1
,
and the Vitali convergence theorem are enough
to deduce that, up to a subsequence,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\varphi_{t}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\,\to\,\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta(\tilde{U})\varphi_{t}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\quad\,\,\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}q(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\varphi_{x}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\,\to\,\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}q(\tilde{U})\varphi_{x}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\quad\,\,\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

Notice that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\leq\sum_{k}|a_{k}|^{2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big|\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\big|\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big].

Using Lemma
6.2
and

\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})=\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}^{2}}\partial_{\tilde{u}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u})
,
we have

\sum_{k}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big|\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\big|(\zeta_{k})^{2}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\leq C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}(\varphi)}\big(1+\frac{|\tilde{m}|^{3}}{\tilde{\rho}^{2}}+\tilde{\rho}P(\tilde{\rho})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\leq{\hat{C}},

(6.2)

where
we have used (
5.21
) in the last inequality.
Similarly,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\leq\sum_{k}|a_{k}|^{2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big|\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\big|(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]

and, using
(
3.5
)

and Lemma
6.2
,
we have

\sum_{k}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big|\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\big|(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\leq{\hat{C}}.

(6.3)

Applying the Dirichlet criterion, these two bounds
(
6.2
)
and (
6.3
),
combined with the fact that
|a_{k}|
is monotone and
|a_{k}|\to 0
as
k\to\infty
,
imply the uniform convergence of the sums

\sum_{k}|a_{k}|^{2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big|\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\big|\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]

and
\sum_{k}|a_{k}|^{2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big|\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\big|(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]
.
Therefore, for any
\delta>0
, there exists
N_{\delta}>0

such that, for any
N\geq N_{\delta}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=N}^{\infty}|a_{k}|^{2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big|\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\big|\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\leq\frac{\delta}{2}

(6.4)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=N}^{\infty}|a_{k}|^{2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big|\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\big|(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\,\leq\frac{\delta}{2}.

Note that, in the definition of
\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}
,
we use the cut-off such that
\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}=0
when
k>\lfloor\varepsilon^{-1}\rfloor
;
in fact, without this cut-off, the above bounds are also valid.
For simplicity,
we do not distinguish between them.
For
N_{0}=N_{\delta}+1
, we choose
\varepsilon_{0}
such that, for any

\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
,

\lfloor\varepsilon^{-1}\rfloor\geq N_{0}
.
Then

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-1}\rfloor}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,-\,\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\,\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\Big)\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big|\big]

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k=1}^{N_{0}}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,-\,\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\sum_{k=1}^{N_{0}}a_{k}^{2}\,\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\Big)\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big|\big]

\displaystyle+\,\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k=N_{0}+1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-1}\rfloor}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,-\,\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\sum_{k=N_{0}+1}^{\infty}a_{k}^{2}\,\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\,\Big)\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big|\big].

For the second term on the right-hand side, (
6.4
) implies that it is less than
\delta
.
For the first term on the right-hand side,
using the
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
almost everywhere
convergence
\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon}\to\tilde{U}
, it can be directly verified
that

\Big(\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k=1}^{N_{0}}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,-\,\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\sum_{k=1}^{N_{0}}a_{k}^{2}\,\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\Big)\varphi\,\to 0.

Then, again by Lemma
6.2
, this convergence, combined with the equi-integrability provided by (
5.21
), enables us to
apply the Vitali convergence theorem to deduce that

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k=1}^{N_{0}}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,-\,\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\sum_{k=1}^{N_{0}}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k})^{2}(\tilde{U})\Big)\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big|\big]\to 0.

Thus combing together leads to

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-1}\rfloor}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,-\,\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\,\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\Big)\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big|\big]\to 0,

(6.5)

which implies that, up to a subsequence,

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-1}\rfloor}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,-\,\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\,\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\Big)\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\to 0\qquad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

For the term:
\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t
,
by Lemma
6.2

and (
5.21
), we have

\varepsilon\,\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big|\big]\leq\varepsilon{\hat{C}}\to 0,

which implies that

\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\to 0\qquad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

We now look at the stochastic integral.
By Proposition
4.3
,
we know that
\tilde{W}^{\varepsilon}\to\tilde{W}
in
C([0,T];\mathfrak{A}_{0})
.
Therefore, by
[
6
, Lemma 2.6.6]
, to prove the convergence of the stochastic integral, it remains to show that

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\to\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})\Phi(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\qquad\ \text{in $L^{2}(0,T;L_{2}(\mathfrak{A};\mathbb{R}))\,\,\,\,\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}$}.

(6.6)

In fact, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})a_{k}\zeta_{k}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}t\big]\leq\sum_{k}|a_{k}|^{2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})\zeta_{k}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}t\big].

Using
conditions (
2.30
)–(
2.31
),
Lemma
6.2
,
and
\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})=\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}}\partial_{\tilde{u}}\eta(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u})
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})\zeta_{k}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}t\big]

(6.7)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}(\varphi)}\big(\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})\varphi\big)^{2}\,{\rm d}x\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}(\varphi)}\sum_{k}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}}}\zeta_{k}(\tilde{U})\big)^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle\leq C(\varphi,B_{0},\gamma,\rho_{\infty})\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}(\varphi)}\big(1+\frac{\tilde{m}^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}}+e^{*}(\tilde{\rho},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}(\varphi)}\big(1+\frac{\tilde{m}^{3}}{\tilde{\rho}^{2}}+\tilde{\rho}P(\tilde{\rho})\big){\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t\Big)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C(T,E_{0,2})+{\hat{C}}(2),

where we have used similar arguments to those
for (
3.18
)
in the second inequality
and used
Proposition
6.1
and (
5.21
)
in the last inequality.
Similarly,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})a_{k}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}t\big]\leq\sum_{k}|a_{k}|^{2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}t\big].

Using
(
3.5
), (
5.21
),
and (
6.1
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}t\big]\leq C(T,E_{0,2})+{\hat{C}}(2).

(6.8)

By the same argument as that for
(
6.4
),
these two bounds
(
6.7
)–(
6.8
) imply that, for any
\delta>0
, there exists
N_{\delta}>0
such that,
for any
N\geq N_{\delta}
,

\sum_{k=N}^{\infty}|a_{k}|^{2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})\zeta_{k}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}t\big]+\sum_{k=N}^{\infty}|a_{k}|^{2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}t\big]\leq\delta.

As above, for
N_{0}=N_{\delta}+1
, we choose
\varepsilon_{0}

such that
\lfloor\varepsilon^{-1}\rfloor\geq N_{0}

for any
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
.
Note that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})\Phi(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\Big\|_{L_{2}(\mathfrak{A};\mathbb{R})}^{2}\,{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})a_{k}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})a_{k}\zeta_{k}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{0}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})a_{k}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})a_{k}\zeta_{k}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle+2\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\sum_{k=N_{0}+1}^{\lfloor\varepsilon^{-1}\rfloor}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})a_{k}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\,+\sum_{k=N_{0}+1}^{\infty}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})a_{k}\,\zeta_{k}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\Big)\,{\rm d}t\big].

Therefore, similar arguments to the justification of (
6.5
) yield that

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})\Phi(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\Big\|_{L_{2}(\mathfrak{A};\mathbb{R})}^{2}\,{\rm d}t\Big|\big]\to 0,

(6.9)

which implies (
6.6
).
Then, by
[
6
, Lemma 2.6.6]
, we have

\int_{0}^{\cdot}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tilde{W}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\to\int_{0}^{\cdot}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})\Phi(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tilde{W}(\tau)

in
L^{2}(0,T)
in probability.
Thus, up to a subsequence, for
a.e.

t\in[0,T]
,

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tilde{W}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})\Phi(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tilde{W}(\tau)\qquad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}},

(6.10)

which implies
that

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tilde{W}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\to\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})\Phi(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tilde{W}(\tau)\qquad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

Combining the above results yields that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\eta(\tilde{U})\varphi_{t}+q(\tilde{U})\varphi_{x}\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})\Phi(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tilde{W}(\tau)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\,\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\geq 0\qquad\text{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-{\it a.s.}}.

∎

For the general pressure law case, we now show that
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})

is a weak solution to (
1.1
).

Proposition 6.3
.

For any
\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R})
,
the mass and momentum equations hold
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\rho}\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{m}\partial_{x}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t=0,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{m}\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{\tilde{m}^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}}+P(\tilde{\rho})\big)\partial_{x}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Phi(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tilde{W}(t)=0.

The proof is similar to that of
Proposition
6.2
and even simpler, so we omit the details.

6.2
Higher-Order Energy Estimates and More Convex Entropy Pairs for the Entropy Inequality

We now establish a higher-order energy estimate,
under which the entropy inequality holds for more entropy pairs.

Proposition 6.4
.

Assume that
U^{\varepsilon}_{0}
satisfy

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0},m^{\varepsilon}_{0})\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big]\leq C\,\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho_{0},m_{0})\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big]<\infty\qquad\mbox{for $1\leq p<\infty$},

and let
(\rho^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon})
be the strong solution of the parabolic approximation (
3.1
), which is predictable and satisfies
(\rho^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{\infty},\,u)\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}))

\mathbb{P}
-a.s..
Then the following higher-order energy estimates hold
:

For
1\leq p<\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})(r)\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big]\leq C(p,T,E_{0,p})+C\,\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho_{0},m_{0})\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big]\quad\mbox{for any $t\in[0,T]$},

(6.11)

where
C(p,T,E_{0,p})
is independent of
\varepsilon
.

Proof.

Theorem
3.1
(iii)
guarantees that, for any fixed
x
, the equations in (
3.1
) hold
as a finite-dimensional stochastic differential system. Thus,
applying the Itô formula to the relative higher-order energy
\eta^{*}_{\diamond}
and
integrating by parts yield that, for
i=1
or
2
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})(\tau)\,{\rm d}x+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\nabla^{2}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon}_{0})\,{\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nabla\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})\Psi^{\varepsilon}(U^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}W(r)+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})\big(\mathfrak{G}_{i}^{\varepsilon}\big)^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r,

where
\mathfrak{G}_{i}^{\varepsilon},i=1,2
, are defined in the beginning of §
3
.

Since it is not clear
whether
\eta_{\diamond}^{*}
is convex with respect to all
U
owing to the generality of the pressure,
we need to estimate
\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\nabla^{2}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r
carefully.
Note that

\nabla^{2}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{4}}{(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{5}}+\big(\frac{e(\rho^{\varepsilon})}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\big)^{\prime\prime}(m^{\varepsilon})^{2}+g^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{\varepsilon})&\quad-\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{3}}{(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{4}}+2m^{\varepsilon}\big(\frac{e(\rho^{\varepsilon})}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\big)^{\prime}\\[5.69054pt]
-\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{3}}{(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{4}}+2m^{\varepsilon}\big(\frac{e(\rho^{\varepsilon})}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\big)^{\prime}&\quad\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{3}}+2\frac{e(\rho^{\varepsilon})}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\end{pmatrix}=:\begin{pmatrix}A_{11}&\ A_{12}\\
A_{21}&\ A_{22}\end{pmatrix}.

We are going to prove first that
\eta_{\diamond}^{*}
is convex, when
\rho^{\varepsilon}<\rho_{\star}

or
\rho^{\varepsilon}>\rho^{\star}
.
When
\rho^{\varepsilon}<\rho_{\star}
, without loss of generality,
we assume that
\kappa_{1}=1
in (
2.4
).
On one hand, a direct calculation yields that

\displaystyle A_{11}

\displaystyle=\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{4}}{(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{5}}+\Big(\frac{e(\rho^{\varepsilon})}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\Big)^{\prime\prime}(m^{\varepsilon})^{2}+g^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{4}}{(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{5}}+\Big(\frac{(\gamma_{1}-2)(\gamma_{1}-3)}{\gamma_{1}-1}+f_{1}\Big)(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{1}-4}(m^{\varepsilon})^{2}+\Big(\frac{2\gamma_{1}}{\gamma_{1}-1}+f_{2}\Big)(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2\gamma_{1}-3}

\displaystyle=a_{1}\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{4}}{(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{5}}+b_{1}(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{1}-4}(m^{\varepsilon})^{2}+c_{1}(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2\gamma_{1}-3},

where
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
are sufficiently small when
\rho_{\star}
is sufficiently small. Let

\Delta:=b_{1}^{2}-4a_{1}c_{1}=\Big(\frac{(\gamma_{1}-2)(\gamma_{1}-3)}{\gamma_{1}-1}+f_{1}\Big)^{2}-4\Big(\frac{2\gamma_{1}}{\gamma_{1}-1}+f_{2}\Big).

When
1<\gamma_{1}<\frac{3}{2}
, we see that
b_{1}>0
since
f_{1}
is sufficiently small.
Therefore, the roots of
A_{11}=0
are all less than zero,
so that
A_{11}>0
.
When
\frac{3}{2}\leq\gamma_{1}<3
, we find that
\Delta<0
since
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
are sufficiently small, so that
A_{11}>0
. In conclusion,
A_{11}>0
.
On the other hand,

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})|

\displaystyle=A_{11}A_{22}-A_{12}A_{21}

\displaystyle=(m^{\varepsilon})^{4}(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{1}-7}(\gamma_{1}+\tilde{f}_{1})+(m^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2\gamma_{1}-6}\Big(\frac{4}{\gamma_{1}-1}+\tilde{f}_{2}\Big)+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{3\gamma_{1}-5}\Big(\frac{4\gamma_{1}}{(\gamma_{1}-1)^{2}}+\tilde{f}_{3}\Big),

where
\tilde{f}_{i},\ i=1,2,3
, are all sufficiently small
when
\rho_{\star}
is sufficiently small.
Then it is direct to obtain that
|\nabla^{2}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})|>0
.
Thus,
\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})
is convex, when
\rho^{\varepsilon}<\rho_{\star}
.
Similarly, we obtain that

\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})
is convex, when
\rho^{\varepsilon}>\rho^{\star}
.
Therefore, we conclude that

\varepsilon\iint_{([0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R})\cap\{\rho^{\varepsilon}<\rho_{\star}\}\cap\{\rho^{\varepsilon}>\rho^{\star}\}}(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\nabla^{2}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\geq 0.

When
\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}
,

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\nabla^{2}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{3}}\big(\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}-\partial_{x}m^{\varepsilon}\big)^{2}+2\frac{e(\rho^{\varepsilon})}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\big(\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}-\partial_{x}m^{\varepsilon}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle+\big(\frac{e^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{\varepsilon})}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}-2\frac{e^{\prime}(\rho^{\varepsilon})}{(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}}\big)(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}(m^{\varepsilon})^{2}+4m^{\varepsilon}\frac{e^{\prime}(\rho^{\varepsilon})}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}m^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\big(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\big)^{2}+2\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\big)^{2}+\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{\prime\prime}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}+4\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}e^{\prime}(\rho^{\varepsilon})\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon},

where

\displaystyle|4\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}e^{\prime}(\rho^{\varepsilon})\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}|

\displaystyle\leq\beta\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon})^{2}+C_{\beta}\rho^{\varepsilon}\big(e^{\prime}(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{2}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\beta\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon})^{2}+C_{\beta}C(\rho_{\star},\rho^{\star})\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{\prime\prime}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}.

Thus, when
\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}
, we have

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\nabla^{2}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\geq(1-\delta)\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\big(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\big)^{2}+2\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\big)^{2}+\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{\prime\prime}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}

\displaystyle-C_{\delta}C(\rho_{\star},\rho^{\star})\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{\prime\prime}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon\iint_{([0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R})\cap\{\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}\}}(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\nabla^{2}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r

\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon\iint_{\{\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}\}}\big((1-\delta)\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\big(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\big)^{2}+2\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\big)^{2}+\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{\prime\prime}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\big){\rm d}x{\rm d}r

\displaystyle\quad\,\,-C_{\delta}C(\rho_{\star},\rho^{\star})\iint_{\{\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}\}}\varepsilon\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{\prime\prime}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}{\rm d}x{\rm d}r.

It
can directly be proved that the stochastic integral

G_{\diamond}^{\varepsilon}(\tau):=\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nabla\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})\Psi^{\varepsilon}(U^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}W(r)

is a local martingale. Then, by the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}|G_{\diamond}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)|^{p}\big]\leq C\,\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k}\Big(\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})}\big(\frac{1}{3}(u^{\varepsilon})^{3}+2e(\rho^{\varepsilon})u^{\varepsilon}\big)\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{2}{\rm d}r\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\big].

Denote
K_{1}^{\varepsilon}=\{0\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}<\rho_{\star}\}
and
K_{2}^{\varepsilon}=\{\rho^{\varepsilon}>\rho^{\star}\}
.
Notice that,
for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sum_{k}\Big(\iint_{([0,t]\times{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}))\cap\,K_{i}^{\varepsilon}}\big(\frac{1}{3}(u^{\varepsilon})^{3}+2e(\rho^{\varepsilon})u^{\varepsilon}\Big)\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x\big)^{2}{\rm d}r\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\delta\,\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{4}+\frac{(e(\rho^{\varepsilon}))^{2}}{(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{i}-2}}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\big)\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big]

\displaystyle+C_{\delta}\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sum_{k}\iint_{([0,t]\times{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}))\cap\,K_{i}^{\varepsilon}}\big(\frac{(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{i}-2}\big)(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big].

For the second term on the right-hand side of the above inequality, we only show the estimate for Case 3 (see Hypotheses
3.1
–
3.2
). The estimates for the other cases are similar and easier.
For Case 3, by assumption (
3.5
),
we obtain that, for
\mathfrak{G}_{1}^{\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sum_{k}\iint_{([0,t]\times{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}))\cap\,K_{i}^{\varepsilon}}\Big(\frac{(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{i}-2}\Big)(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq B_{0}^{2p}(1+\varepsilon^{2})^{p}t^{p}E_{0,p}+C(p,t)

\displaystyle+C\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\iint_{([0,t]\times{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}))\cap\,K_{i}^{\varepsilon}}B_{0}^{2}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{i}}\big)\big((u^{\varepsilon})^{2}+e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big],

where we have used (
2.19
)–(
2.22
) in the
last inequality.
Since, by similar arguments
to (
2.24
)–(
2.27
),

\displaystyle\rho^{2\gamma_{1}-1}\leq\bar{C}_{\gamma_{1}}\big(e_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho,\rho_{\infty})+\rho_{\infty}^{2\gamma_{1}-1}\big)\qquad\text{ when $\rho\in\left(0,\rho_{\star}\right]$},

\displaystyle\rho^{2\gamma_{2}-1}\leq\bar{C}_{\gamma_{2}}\big(e_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho,\rho_{\infty})+\rho_{\infty}^{2\gamma_{2}-1}\big)\qquad\text{ when $\rho\in\left[\rho^{\star},\infty\right)$},

it follows from (
2.19
)–(
2.22
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\iint_{([0,t]\times{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}))\cap\,K_{i}^{\varepsilon}}B_{0}^{2}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma_{i}}\big)\big((u^{\varepsilon})^{2}+e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\iint_{([0,t]\times{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}))\cap\,K_{i}^{\varepsilon}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{4}+\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}+e_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho,\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big]+\big(t|{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})|\rho_{\infty}^{2\gamma_{i}-1}\big)^{p}.

For the term
\big(t|{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})|\rho_{\infty}^{2\gamma_{i}-1}\big)^{p}
, in Case 3,
{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})\subset\mathbb{K}
,
so this term is uniformly bounded with respect to
\varepsilon
.
Case 2 can be treated similarly. For Case 1,
{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})=\Lambda^{\varepsilon}
,
thus
\big(t|{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})|\rho_{\infty}^{2\gamma_{i}-1}\big)^{p}
is uniformly bounded with respect to
\varepsilon
by choosing
\alpha_{0}
in
\rho_{\infty}(\varepsilon)=\varepsilon^{\alpha_{0}}
such that
\alpha_{0}(2\gamma_{i}-1)>1,\ i=1,2
. When
\rho^{\varepsilon}\in[\rho_{\star},\rho^{\star}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sum_{k}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{({\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}))\cap\{\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}\}}\big(\frac{1}{3}(u^{\varepsilon})^{3}+2e(\rho^{\varepsilon})u^{\varepsilon}\big)\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\big)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\delta\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{4}+\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\big)\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big]

\displaystyle+C_{\delta}C(\rho^{\star})\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sum_{k}\iint_{([0,t]\times{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}))\cap\{\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}\}}\Big(\frac{(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+\frac{1}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\Big)(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big].

We deal with the second term on the right-hand side of the above inequality similarly as above
to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sum_{k}\iint_{([0,t]\times{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}))\cap\{\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}\}}\Big(\frac{(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+\frac{1}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\Big)(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq B_{0}^{2p}(1+\varepsilon^{2})^{p}t^{p}E_{0,p}+C\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\iint_{([0,t]\times{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}))\cap\{\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}\}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{4}+\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big]

\displaystyle+C(p,t)+C(p)\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}+e^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big].

Therefore, using (
2.19
)–(
2.22
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}|G_{\diamond}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)|^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\delta\,\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{4}+\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\big)\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big]+C_{\delta}C(\rho^{\star},p,t)(1+E_{0,p})

\displaystyle+C_{\delta}C(\rho^{\star})\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{4}+\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}+e_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big].

Since
\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})=\frac{(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}+\frac{2e(\rho^{\varepsilon})}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}
, by similar arguments as above, we obtain that, for
j=1,2
, when
\alpha_{0}\gamma_{1}>1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\Big|\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(U^{\varepsilon})\big(\mathfrak{G}_{j}^{\varepsilon}\big)^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big|^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C(p,t)(1+E_{0,p})+C(p)\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{4}+\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}+e_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big],

where we have used the estimate that, for
\rho^{\varepsilon}\in[\rho_{\star},\rho^{\star}]
,

\rho^{\varepsilon}(e(\rho^{\varepsilon}))^{2}\leq C\big(e_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho_{\infty})+\rho_{\infty}(e(\rho_{\infty}))^{2}\big)
.

Combining the above estimates, we conclude that, for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{1}{12}\frac{(m^{\varepsilon})^{4}}{(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{3}}+\frac{e(\rho^{\varepsilon})}{\rho^{\varepsilon}}m^{2}+e_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho_{\infty})\big)(r,x)\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big]

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\iint_{\{\rho_{\star}\leq\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq\rho^{\star}\}}\big((1-\delta)\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\big(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\big)^{2}+2\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big(\partial_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\big)^{2}+\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\big)^{\prime\prime}(\partial_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon})^{2}(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\delta\,\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{4}+\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}\big)(r,x)\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big]

\displaystyle+C_{\delta}C(p,\rho^{\star})\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\iint_{([0,t]\times{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}))}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon})^{4}+\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})(u^{\varepsilon})^{2}+e_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big]

\displaystyle+C_{\delta}C(p,t,\rho^{\star})(1+E_{0,p})+C(p)\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0},m^{\varepsilon}_{0})\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big].

Then the proof is completed by taking
\delta
sufficiently small and using the Grönwall inequality.
∎

Having this higher-order energy estimate at hand,
the higher-order growth of the generating function in the entropy is allowed in the general pressure law case.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
\psi\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
be convex and satisfy

|\psi(s)|\leq C|s|^{3-\delta},\qquad|\psi^{\prime}(s)|\leq C|s|^{2-\delta},

then, when
\rho\geq\rho^{\star}
and
\delta\geq 2\big(1-\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}}\big)
for
\gamma_{2}\leq 2
and
\delta\geq 1
for
\gamma_{2}>2
,

\displaystyle|\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+|q^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+\frac{1}{\rho}|\partial_{u}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|^{2}+\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}|\partial_{u}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\sum_{k}\zeta_{k}^{2}\leq C\big(1+\rho|u|^{4}+g(\rho)+e(\rho)\big).

When
\rho\leq\rho^{\star}
and
\delta\geq 0
,

|\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+|q^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+\frac{1}{\rho}|\partial_{u}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|^{2}+\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}|\partial_{u}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\sum_{k}\zeta_{k}^{2}\leq C(\rho_{\star},\rho^{\star})\big(1+\rho|u|^{4}+g(\rho)\big).

Since the proof is similar to that of Lemma
6.2
, so we omit the details.

Using Proposition
6.4
and
Lemma
6.3
,
it follows from a similar proof to
Proposition
6.2
that the following entropy inequality satisfied by more entropy pairs.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
(\eta,q)
be the entropy pair defined in (
2.13
) with convex generating function
\psi\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
satisfies (
2.35
).
Assume that
U_{0}
satisfies
E_{0,2}<\infty
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho_{0},m_{0})\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{2}\big]<\infty.

Then, for any nonnegative
\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R})
, the following entropy inequality holds
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\eta(\tilde{U})\varphi_{t}+q(\tilde{U})\varphi_{x}\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})\Phi(\tilde{U})\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}W(t)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\,\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\geq 0,

where
\tilde{U}=(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})^{\top}
.

6.3
Compactness of the Martingale Entropy Solution Sequence to (
1.1
)

We now prove (ii) in Theorem
2.4
,
which can be stated in the following:

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
U^{\delta}:=(\rho^{\delta},m^{\delta})
be a sequence of entropy solutions
to (
1.1
) obtained
in
Theorem
2.4

corresponding to a sequence of initial law
\Im^{\delta}=\mathbb{P}\circ(\rho_{0}^{\delta},m_{0}^{\delta})^{-1}

satisfying

\displaystyle\int_{L^{\gamma_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})\times L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})}\big|\mathcal{E}(\rho,m)\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}\Im^{\delta}(\rho,m)+\int_{L^{\gamma_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})\times L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})}\big|\mathcal{E}_{\diamond}(\rho,m)\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}\Im^{\delta}(\rho,m)\leq C,

where constant
C
is independent of
\delta
.
Then, for any
K\Subset\mathbb{R}
, there exists
C(K,p,T)>0
independent of
\delta

such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{K}\big(\rho^{\delta}P(\rho^{\delta})+\frac{|m^{\delta}|^{3}}{(\rho^{\delta})^{2}}+\eta_{\diamond}(\rho^{\delta},m^{\delta})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{p}\big]\leq C(K,p,T)\quad\mbox{for any $t\in[0,T]$},

(6.12)

so that
U^{\delta}:=(\rho^{\delta},m^{\delta})
are tight in

L^{\gamma_{2}+1}_{\rm w,loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\times L^{\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{\gamma_{2}+3}}_{\rm w,loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})

and admit the Skorokhod representation
(\tilde{\rho}^{\delta},\tilde{m}^{\delta})
.
Moreover, when
\gamma_{2}<2
, there exist random variables
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})
such that
almost surely
(\tilde{\rho}^{\delta},\,\tilde{m}^{\delta})\to(\tilde{\rho},\,\tilde{m})

almost everywhere and
(
up to a subsequence
)
almost surely,

(\tilde{\rho}^{\delta},\,\tilde{m}^{\delta})\to(\tilde{\rho},\,\tilde{m})\qquad\text{in $L^{\bar{p}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\times L^{\bar{q}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}

for
\bar{p}\in[1,\gamma_{2}+1)
and
\bar{q}\in[1,\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{\gamma_{2}+3})
. In addition,
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})
is also an entropy solution to (
1.1
), i.e., almost surely satisfies the entropy inequality (
2.34
).

Proof.

We divide the proof into four steps.

1
. The uniform bound (
6.12
) can be proved
by the arguments similar to those for (
5.1
)
and Proposition
6.1
.

2
.
Denote

\mu_{\delta}:={\rm d}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\delta})+\partial_{x}q^{\psi}(U^{\delta}){\rm d}t-\partial_{m}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\delta})\Phi(U^{\delta}){\rm d}W(t)-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\delta})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k})^{2}(U^{\delta}){\rm d}t,

where
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
is any entropy pair defined in (
2.13
) with generating function
\psi\in C_{\rm c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.
When the generating function
\psi\in C_{c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
is convex and
satisfies (
2.33
),
by the proof of Lemmas
5.4
–
5.5

and Proposition
5.2
, we obtain
that
\mu_{\delta}
is stochastically bounded in
H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
.

On the other hand, the entropy inequality (
2.34
) gives that
\mu_{\delta}\geq 0
in the sense of distribution (see Lemma
A.11
).
Thus, combining them together and applying Lemma
A.11
yield
that
\mu_{\delta}
is tight in
W^{-1,p_{1}}
for any
1<p_{1}<2
.

By Proposition
6.5
, when
\gamma_{2}<2
,

\mu^{\delta}_{E}:={\rm d}\eta_{E}(U^{\delta})+\partial_{x}q_{E}(U^{\delta}){\rm d}t-\partial_{m}\eta_{E}(U^{\delta})\Phi(U^{\delta}){\rm d}W(t)-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta_{E}(U^{\delta})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k})^{2}(U^{\delta}){\rm d}t\geq 0

in the sense of distributions. Additionally, using (
6.12
), we obtain that
\{\partial_{t}\eta_{E}(U^{\delta})+\partial_{x}q_{E}(U^{\delta})\}_{\delta>0}

is stochastically bounded in
W^{-1,\frac{4\gamma_{2}+4}{3\gamma_{2}+4}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
.
Then, by similar arguments to those in the justification of
Lemmas
5.4
–
5.5
, we see
that
\mu^{\delta}_{E}
is stochastically bounded
in
W^{-1,\frac{4\gamma_{2}+4}{3\gamma_{2}+4}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
.

3
. Notice that, for any not necessarily convex entropy pair
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
with generating function
\psi\in C_{\rm c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, there exists a constant

\bar{M}:=M(\psi,\rho_{\star},\rho^{\star})
such that
|\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}|\leq\bar{M}\nabla^{2}\eta_{E}
.
In fact,
|\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}|\leq\bar{M}\,\nabla^{2}\eta_{E}
is equivalent
to
|\bm{\xi}\,\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}\,\bm{\xi}^{\top}|\leq\bar{M}\,\bm{\xi}\,\nabla^{2}\eta_{E}\,\bm{\xi}^{\top}

for any vector
\bm{\xi}=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
By direct calculation, we see that
\bm{\xi}\,\nabla^{2}\eta_{E}\,\bm{\xi}^{\top}=\big(\rho e(\rho)\big)^{\prime\prime}\xi_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{\rho}\big(\frac{m}{\rho}\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\big)^{2}
.
Regarding
\eta^{\psi}
as a function of
(\rho,u)
, we have

\displaystyle\bm{\xi}\,\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}\bm{\xi}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\xi_{1}^{2}\kappa^{\prime}(\rho)^{2}\partial_{u}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)+\partial_{u}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\big(\frac{m}{\rho}\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\big)^{2}-2\xi_{1}\big(\frac{m}{\rho}\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\big)\big(\partial_{\rho u}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)-\frac{1}{\rho}\partial_{u}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)\big).

Then, using the fact that
\partial_{u}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)=\rho^{2}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,m)

and
\partial_{\rho u}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)-\frac{1}{\rho}\partial_{u}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)=\rho\partial_{m\rho}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)
,
it follows from
(
5.8
)–(
5.9
) that
there exists a constant
M(\psi,\rho_{\star},\rho^{\star})
such that

|\bm{\xi}\,\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}\,\bm{\xi}^{\top}|\leq\bar{M}\,\bm{\xi}\,\nabla^{2}\eta_{E}\,\bm{\xi}^{\top}
.

Utilizing the idea from
[
10
]
,

\,(\bar{\eta},\bar{q}):=(\bar{M}\eta_{E}-\eta^{\psi},\bar{M}q_{E}-q^{\psi})
is a convex entropy pair.
Define

\bar{\mu}_{\delta}:={\rm d}\bar{\eta}(U^{\delta})+\partial_{x}\bar{q}(U^{\delta}){\rm d}t-\partial_{m}\bar{\eta}(U^{\delta})\Phi(U^{\delta}){\rm d}W(t)-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m}^{2}\bar{\eta}(U^{\delta})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k})^{2}(U^{\delta}){\rm d}t.

Note that the stochastic boundedness of
\mu_{\delta}
is nothing to do with the convexity.
Thus, by the stochastic boundedness of
\mu_{\delta}
and
\mu^{\delta}_{E}
,
we obtain that
\bar{\mu}_{\delta}
is stochastically bounded
in
W^{-1,\frac{4\gamma_{2}+4}{3\gamma_{2}+4}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
.
On the other hand, by the entropy inequality (
2.34
),

\bar{\mu}_{\delta}\geq 0
in the sense of distribution. Thus,
by Lemma
A.11
again,

\bar{\mu}_{\delta}
is tight in
W^{-1,p_{1}}
for any
1<p_{1}<\frac{4\gamma_{2}+4}{3\gamma_{2}+4}
.
By linearity, we obtain that
\mu_{\delta}
is tight in

W^{-1,p_{1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
for any
1<p_{1}<\frac{4\gamma_{2}+4}{3\gamma_{2}+4}
.

4
. By Lemmas
5.4
–
5.5

and Step
3
, we obtain
that
\{\partial_{t}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\delta})+\partial_{x}q^{\psi}(U^{\delta})\}_{\delta}

are tight in
W^{-1,p_{1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
with
1<p_{1}<\frac{4\gamma_{2}+4}{3\gamma_{2}+4}
.
Thus, all the conditions in the stochastic compensated compactness
framework (Theorem
5.1
) are satisfied.
Then Theorem
5.1
implies that
there exist random variables
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})
such that
almost surely
(\tilde{\rho}^{\delta},\,\tilde{m}^{\delta})\to(\tilde{\rho},\,\tilde{m})

almost everywhere and
(
up to a sequence
)
almost surely,

(\tilde{\rho}^{\delta},\,\tilde{m}^{\delta})\to(\tilde{\rho},\,\tilde{m})\qquad\text{in $L^{\bar{p}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\times L^{\bar{q}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}

for
\bar{p}\in[1,\gamma_{2}+1)
and
\bar{q}\in[1,\frac{3(\gamma_{2}+1)}{\gamma_{2}+3})
.

Additionally, by arguments similar to those for the proof
of Proposition
6.2
,
we obtain that
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})
almost surely satisfies the entropy
inequality (
2.34
). This indicates that

(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})
is an entropy solution to (
1.1
).
∎

Having all these at hand, we now prove the existence of global martingale entropy solutions
in the sense that they satisfy conditions (i)–(vi) in
Definition
2.1

and the entropy inequality (
2.34
)
with generating function
\psi
satisfying (
2.33
)
or (
2.35
).
More specifically, conditions (i)–(v) in
Definition
2.1
are
the consequence of Propositions
4.3

and
6.1
.
Condition (vi) in Definition
2.1

is due to Proposition
6.3
.
Inequality (
2.34
) follows from Propositions
6.2
and
6.5
.
Condition (ii) in Theorem
2.4

has been proved in Theorem
6.1
above. Therefore, by
[
6
, Corollary 2.6.4]
, we have verified
Theorems
2.3
–
2.4
.

7
The Polytropic Pressure Case

In the polytropic pressure case, we obtain more information about the martingale entropy solutions, based on the explicit formulas of entropy pairs and the particular features of the system.

First, we have the following simpler properties for the relative internal energy than those
in Lemma
2.2
:

Lemma 7.1
.

For the polytropic pressure case (
1.2
), the following bounds hold
:

\displaystyle e^{*}(\rho,\rho_{\infty})\geq C_{\gamma}\rho(\rho^{\theta}-\rho_{\infty}^{\theta})^{2},

(7.1)

\displaystyle\rho^{\gamma}\leq\bar{C}_{\gamma}\big(e^{*}(\rho,\rho_{\infty})+\rho_{\infty}^{\gamma}\big),

(7.2)

where
C_{\gamma}>0
is a constant, and
\bar{C}_{\gamma}
is another constant depending on
C_{\gamma}
.

These properties follow from Lemma
2.2
by taking
\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{2}=\gamma
and
\rho_{\star}=\rho^{\star}
since the proof does not restrict that
\gamma\leq 3
. For a more detailed proof, see
[
14
, (3.1)]
for (
7.1
).

Lemma
7.1
above, replacing Lemma
2.2
, will be used frequently in the proofs for the polytropic pressure case, as in the general pressure law case.

7.1
Definition of Martingale Entropy Solutions and Main Theorems:

Theorem
7.1

and Theorem
7.2

When the initial density has a positive far-field
\rho_{\infty}>0
,
for the polytropic pressure case (
1.2
), we use the following more precise
definition of solutions:

Definition 7.1
.

We call

(\Omega,\mathcal{F},(\mathcal{F}_{t}),\mathbb{P},\rho,m,W)

a martingale entropy solution with finite relative-energy of (
1.1
)
if it satisfies conditions
(i)–(v)

in
Definition
2.1

with
\gamma_{2}
replaced by
\gamma
and,
for any entropy pair
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
defined in (
2.13
) with
\psi\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
that is convex and satisfies (
7.4
)
(
i.e.
, has subquadratic growth at infinity)
and additionally allows the case
\psi(s)=\frac{1}{2}s^{2}

(
corresponding to the mechanical energy
)
,
the following entropy inequality is almost surely satisfied
for any nonnegative
\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R})
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta(U)\,\varphi_{t}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}q(U)\varphi_{x}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

(7.3)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{m}\eta(U)\Phi(U)\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}W(t)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta(U)\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\,\zeta_{k}^{2}(U)\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\geq 0.

We say a function
\psi\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
has subquadratic growth at infinity if

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}\frac{\psi(s)}{s^{2}}=0,\quad\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}\frac{\psi^{\prime}(s)}{s}=0

\displaystyle\qquad\text{for $\gamma\leq 3$},

(7.4)

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}\frac{\psi(s)}{s^{2}}=0,\quad\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}\frac{\psi^{\prime}(s)}{s}=0,\quad\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s)=0

\displaystyle\qquad\text{for $\gamma>3$}.

Our main result is the following:

Theorem 7.1
.

Assume that the initial data
(\rho_{0},m_{0})\in L^{\gamma}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})\times L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})
have finite
relative-energy
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\rho_{0},m_{0})=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{m_{0}^{2}}{\rho_{0}}+e^{*}(\rho_{0},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x<\infty.

Then, for all
\gamma>1
, there exists a martingale entropy solution with finite relative-energy to (
1.1
) in the sense of
Definition
7.1
.

As in the general pressure law case, the above result can be more general, allowing random initial data.

Theorem 7.2
.

Assume that
\Im
is a Borel probability measure on
L^{\gamma}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})\times L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle supp}\,\Im=\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\rho\geq 0\},

\displaystyle E_{p_{0}}(\Im):=\int_{L^{\gamma}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})\times L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})}\big|\mathcal{E}(\rho,m)\big|^{p_{0}}\,{\rm d}\Im(\rho,m)<\infty\quad\mbox{for some $p_{0}>3$}.

Then, for all
\gamma>1
, there exists a martingale entropy solution with
finite relative-energy to (
1.1
)
in the sense of
Definition
7.1
with initial law
\Im
, i.e., there exists
\mathcal{F}_{0}
-measurable random variables
(\rho_{0},m_{0})
such that
\Im=\mathbb{P}\circ(\rho_{0},m_{0})^{-1}

for which we denote
E_{0,p_{0}}:=E_{p_{0}}(\Im)
.

Remark 7.1
.

For the polytropic pressure case, we have used the same notation
E_{0,p_{0}}
as the general pressure law case for simplicity of notation.

Remark 7.2
.

Theorem
7.1

is the direct corollary of
Theorem
7.2
,
so it suffices to
prove
Theorem
7.2
.

Remark 7.3
.

In
Theorem
7.2
,
we require
p_{0}>3

(
compared to the general pressure law case
)

in order to pass to the limit in the entropy flux and derive the entropy inequality
;

see (
7.18
),

Propositions
3.1

and

6.2
–
6.3
.

When the initial data satisfy

(\rho_{0},m_{0})\to(0,0)\ \text{ as $|x|\to\infty$},

we can also formulate the theorems as above with an obvious change.

7.2
Proof of Theorem
7.2

We use similar strategies to those for the general pressure law case
to prove Theorem
7.2
.

7.2.1
Stochastic parabolic approximation

For the polytropic pressure case, we have the same result, Theorem
3.1
, regarding the parabolic approximation.
In fact, the proof is the same as that in the general pressure law case since it does not restrict that
\gamma\leq 3
.

7.2.2
Higher integrability of the density and the velocity

In the polytropic pressure case,
we have the same uniform energy estimates
as (
3.7
)
since the proof does not restrict that
\gamma\leq 3
.
Regarding the higher integrability of the density and the velocity,
we have similar results to those for the general pressure law case.
Firstly, for the higher integrability of the density,
applying the iteration technique in
[
15
, Lemma 3.3]

and using (
7.2
),
the proof is similar to that of the general pressure law case (even easier). More specifically, in the polytropic pressure case, we obtain the higher integrability of the density for all
\gamma>1
.

Secondly, for the higher integrability of the velocity in the polytropic
gas case, we use a special entropy generated by a non-convex function.
We need the following two lemmas in the proof.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
be an entropy pair in (
2.13
) with
\psi(s)
satisfying

\sup|\psi^{\prime\prime}(s)|<\infty.

Then, for any
(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\bm{\xi}=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

|\bm{\xi}\,\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,m)\,\bm{\xi}^{\top}|\leq M_{\psi}\,\bm{\xi}\,\nabla^{2}\eta_{E}(\rho,m)\,\bm{\xi}^{\top},

where
M_{\psi}
is a constant depending only on
\psi
.

The proof is direct. See
[
23
]
or
[
14
]
.

Let
(\breve{\eta},\breve{q})
be an entropy pair corresponding to
\psi(s)=\frac{1}{2}s|s|
in (
2.13
). We define the relative-entropy pair as

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}(\rho,m)=\breve{\eta}(\rho,m)-\nabla_{(\rho,m)}\breve{\eta}(\rho_{\infty},0)\cdot(\rho-\rho_{\infty},m),

(7.5)

\displaystyle\tilde{q}(\rho,m)=\breve{q}(\rho,m)-\nabla_{(\rho,m)}\breve{\eta}(\rho_{\infty},0)\cdot(m,\frac{m^{2}}{\rho}+P(\rho)).

Note that
(\tilde{\eta},\tilde{q})
is still an entropy pair of (
1.1
). We collect here some properties related to
(\breve{\eta},\breve{q})
and
(\tilde{\eta},\tilde{q})
.

Lemma 7.3
.

The entropy pairs
(\breve{\eta},\breve{q})
and
(\tilde{\eta},\tilde{q})
satisfy
the following properties
:

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\breve{\eta}(\rho,0)=0,\quad\partial_{m}\breve{\eta}(\rho,0)=\rho^{\theta}\int_{-1}^{1}|s|[1-s^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}\,{\rm d}s,

(7.6)

\displaystyle|\tilde{\eta}(\rho,m)|\leq C(\gamma)\eta_{E}^{*}(\rho,m),

(7.7)

\displaystyle|\partial_{m}\tilde{\eta}(\rho,m)|\leq C(\gamma)\big(|\rho^{\theta}-\rho_{\infty}^{\theta}|+|u|\big),

(7.8)

\displaystyle\breve{q}(\rho,m)\geq C(\gamma)^{-1}\big(\rho|u|^{3}+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}\big),

(7.9)

where
C(\gamma)>0
is a constant depending only on
\gamma>1
.

Proof.

(
7.6
)
follows from direct calculation. For (
7.7
)–(
7.8
), see
[
17
, Proof of Proposition 4.1]
. For (
7.9
), see
[
35
]
for the details.
∎

Now we are ready to prove the higher integrability of the velocity.

Proposition 7.1
.

For any
K\Subset\mathbb{R}
, there exists
C(K,p,T,E_{0,p})>0
independent of
\varepsilon
such that
the solution of (
3.1
) obtained
in
Theorem
3.1
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{K}\big(\rho|u|^{3}+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{p}\big]\leq C(K,p,T,E_{0,p})\qquad\mbox{for any $t\in[0,T]$}.

(7.10)

Using Lemmas
7.2
–
7.3
, we can prove Proposition
7.1
similarly as Proposition
3.2
, so we omit the details.

Having these higher integrability results at hand, we have the same results as those in §
4

for the general pressure law case by taking
\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{2}=\gamma
in those proofs,
which do not restrict
\gamma\leq 3
.
More specifically, we have the same tightness results as those in §
4

for all
\gamma>1
.
Now we are going to establish the
Stochastic
L^{p}
Compensated Compactness Framework

for the polytropic pressure case.

7.2.3
Stochastic compensated compactness framework in
L^{p}

For the polytropic pressure case, our Stochastic
L^{p}
Compensated Compactness Framework can be formulated
as follows:

Theorem 7.3
.

Let
\gamma>1
.
Let
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
be a sequence of random variables satisfying that,
for any
K\Subset\mathbb{R}
, there exists a constant
C(K,p,T)>0
independent of
\varepsilon
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{K}\big(\rho P(\rho)+\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{p}\big]\leq C(K,p,T)\qquad\mbox{for any $t\in[0,T]$},

(7.11)

where
P(\rho)
satisfies (
1.2
),
so that
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
are tight in

L^{\gamma+1}_{\rm w,loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\times L^{\frac{3(\gamma+1)}{\gamma+3}}_{\rm w,loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})

and admit the Skorokhod representation
(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})
.
Assume that

\big\{\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{t}+q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{x}\big\}_{\varepsilon>0}\qquad\text{are tight in $H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}

(7.12)

for any weak entropy pair
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
of system (
1.1
)
defined in (
2.13
) with generating function
\psi\in C_{\rm c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.
Then there exist random variables
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})
such that
almost surely
(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\,\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})\to(\tilde{\rho},\,\tilde{m})

almost everywhere and
(
up to a sequence
)
almost surely,

(\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon},\,\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon})\to(\tilde{\rho},\,\tilde{m})\qquad\text{in $L^{\bar{p}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\times L^{\bar{q}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}

for
\bar{p}\in[1,\gamma+1)
and
\bar{q}\in[1,\frac{3(\gamma+1)}{\gamma+3})
.

To establish this framework, the same procedure as that for the general pressure law case
in §
5
can be applied to
the polytropic pressure case with minor changes in the proof.
We first need to verify the tightness of the random entropy dissipation measures (
7.12
).

7.2.4
Tightness of the random entropy dissipation measures

For the polytropic pressure case, we need the following simpler lemma,
Lemma
7.4
,
regarding the properties of the entropy pairs with generating functions of compact support.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
\psi\in C_{\rm c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, and let
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
be a weak entropy pair
defined in (
2.13
).
Then, for the polytropic pressure case,

|\eta^{\psi}_{\rho}(\rho,m)+u\eta^{\psi}_{m}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}(1+\rho^{\theta}),\quad\ |\eta_{m}^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}.

The proof can be achieved by a direct calculation and
[
14
, Lemma 2.1]
.

We can obtain a better tightness result for the random entropy dissipation measures since the entropy pairs have
explicit formulas and better estimates.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
\psi\in C_{\rm c}^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, and let
(\eta^{\psi},q^{\psi})
be a weak entropy pair defined in (
2.13
). Suppose that

(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
with
m^{\varepsilon}=\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}

is a sequence of solutions of
the parabolic approximation equations (
3.1
).
Then, for the polytropic pressure case, the random entropy dissipation measures

\big\{\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{t}+q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{x}\big\}_{\varepsilon>0}\qquad\mbox{are tight in $H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}.

Proof.

For the polytropic pressure case, by
[
14
, Lemma 2.1]
, we have

\displaystyle|\eta^{\psi}(\rho,m)|+|q^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho

\displaystyle\text{when $\gamma\in\left(1,3\right]$},

(7.13)

\displaystyle|\eta^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho,\ |q^{\psi}(\rho,m)|\leq C_{\psi}\rho\max\{1,\rho^{\theta}\}

\displaystyle\text{when $\gamma>3$}.

Then, by the higher integrability of the density and the velocity
in §
7.2.2
,
we conclude that
\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
and
q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
are uniformly bounded in
L^{1}(\Omega;L^{q_{1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}))
with

q_{1}=\gamma+1
for
\gamma\in\left(1,3\right]
, and
q_{1}=2+\frac{\gamma-3}{\gamma+1}
for
\gamma>3
.
Therefore,

\{\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{t}+q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{x}\}_{\varepsilon>0}

is stochastically bounded in
W^{-1,q_{1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
(see Lemma
A.9
).
That is, for each
\beta>0
, there exists
C_{\beta}>0
such that

\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{t}+q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{x}\|_{W^{-1,q_{1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})}>C_{\beta}\big\}<\beta.

On the other hand, as in the general pressure law case,
we apply the Itô formula to
\eta^{\psi}
to obtain

\displaystyle{\rm d}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{x}q^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}t-\varepsilon(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\,\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\,\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\Phi^{\varepsilon}(U^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}W(t)+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,{\rm d}t.

In the polytropic pressure case, we have the same results as
Lemmas
5.2
–
5.5
,
since the proofs can be directly applied to the polytropic pressure case with (i)
in Lemma
5.1
replaced
by Lemma
7.4
.
Therefore, the same as that for the general pressure law case,

\{\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\}_{\varepsilon>0}

and
\{\partial_{t}M^{\varepsilon}(t)\}_{\varepsilon>0}

are tight in
H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
,
and
\{\varepsilon(\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon})^{\top}\nabla^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\partial_{x}U^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
and

\{\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(U^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\}_{\varepsilon>0}

are tight
in
W^{-1,q}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
for any
q\in(1,2)
. These imply that

\{\eta^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{t}+q^{\psi}(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})_{x}\}_{\varepsilon>0}\qquad\text{are tight in $W^{-1,q_{0}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$ with $1\leq q_{0}\leq q$}.

(7.14)

Therefore, our conclusion follows from Lemma
A.9
and (
7.14
).
∎

Then we can also derive the same Tartar commutation relation for the polytropic pressure case,
the proof of which is similar and easier
with (
5.14
)
replaced by (
7.13
).
Moreover, we can prove that

{\rm div}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{1}^{\varepsilon}
and
\text{curl}_{(t,x)}\,\tilde{X}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
are tight
in
H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
and
H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2})
respectively.
Thus, the classical div-curl lemma
[
37
]
is enough for the polytropic pressure case.

The remaining results in §
5

are all valid for the polytropic pressure case
by taking
\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{2}=\gamma
in those proofs, which do not restrict
\gamma\leq 3
,
i.e.
,
for all
\gamma>1
.
In fact, for the reduction of the random Young measure
(Proposition
5.3
) in the polytropic pressure case,
all the computations in
[
14
, §6–7]
with
u
there
replaced by
\frac{m}{\rho}
can be applied.
Therefore, we have established the stochastic
L^{p}
compensated compactness
framework for the polytropic pressure case.

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
\tilde{\mu}
be the limit random Young measure obtained in
Proposition
4.3
. Then
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s., for a.e.
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
,
either
\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}
is concentrated on the vacuum region

\mathcal{V}:=\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\rho=0\}
or
\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}
is reduced to
a Dirac mass
\delta_{(\rho^{*}(t,x,\omega),m^{*}(t,x,\omega))}

on
\mathfrak{T}=\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\rho>0\}
.

Proof.

If
\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}
is not concentrated on the vacuum
region
\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\rho=0\}
, we fix
(t,x,\omega)\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}

such that the Tartar commutation (
5.13
) is satisfied. Then
we use
[
11
, Theorem 8.5]
to obtain
that
\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}
is reduced to a Dirac mass
\delta_{(\rho^{*}(t,x,\omega),m^{*}(t,x,\omega))}

on the region
\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\rho>0\}
.
∎

However, to complete the proof of Theorem
7.2
,
we need much higher integrability for the density.
Thanks to the explicit formula of the entropy,
we have obtained much higher integrability of the density in Proposition
7.1
.
Note that
\gamma+\theta>\gamma+1
when
\gamma>3
.
Then, by the same arguments as those in the proofs of
Propositions
5.1
–
5.4
,
we can obtain better results with (
5.1
) in Proposition
5.1
replaced by
