\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\big(\rho P(\rho)+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}+\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\big)\,{\rm d}\tilde{\mu}_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\big]\leq{\hat{C}},

(7.15)

and with (
5.21
) in Proposition
5.4
replaced by

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\hat{K}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}}\big(\rho P(\rho)+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}+\frac{|m|^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\big)\,{\rm d}\nu_{(t,x)}(\rho,m){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\Big)^{p_{0}}\big]\leq{\hat{C}}(p_{0}).

(7.16)

7.2.5
Completion of the proof of Theorem
7.2

We now prove Theorem
7.2
.

For the energy estimate, Proposition
6.1
can be applied directly
to the polytropic pressure case for all
\gamma>1
so that the same result can be obtained.
Thus, in this section, when Proposition
6.1
is used,
it means that we use the version for the polytropic pressure case.

For the entropy inequality, the following lemma
shows the growth of entropy pairs.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
\psi\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
be convex and satisfy

\displaystyle|\psi(s)|\leq C|s|^{2},\quad|\psi^{\prime}(s)|\leq C|s|,\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\,\,\,\text{for $\gamma\leq 3$},

\displaystyle|\psi(s)|\leq C|s|^{2},\quad|\psi^{\prime}(s)|\leq C|s|,\quad\psi^{\prime\prime}(s)\leq C\qquad\text{for $\gamma>3$}.

Then

\displaystyle|q^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+|\partial_{m}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\sum_{k}\zeta_{k}^{2}\leq C\big(1+\rho|u|^{3}+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}\big),

(7.17)

\displaystyle|\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+\rho|\partial_{m}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|^{2}\leq C\big(1+\rho|u|^{2}+\rho^{\gamma}\big).

(7.18)

Proof.

The proof is similar to and easier than that of
Lemma
6.2
,
since the entropy has an explicit formula.
Here we only show the proof for
|\partial_{m}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\sum_{k}\zeta_{k}^{2}
when
\gamma>3
,
since the other case is simpler.
Recall the explicit formula for the entropy in the polytropic pressure case:

\displaystyle\eta^{\psi}(\rho,m)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}[\rho^{2\theta}-(u-s)^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}\psi(s){\rm d}s=\rho\int_{-1}^{1}\psi(u+\rho^{\theta}s)[1-s^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}{\rm d}s,

where
\lambda=\frac{3-\gamma}{2(\gamma-1)}>-\frac{1}{2}
.
Note that

\lambda=\frac{3-\gamma}{2(\gamma-1)}<0
when
\gamma>3
.
Then we have to use the additional
condition
\psi^{\prime\prime}(s)\leq C
to obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{m}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,m)=\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{-1}^{1}\psi^{\prime\prime}(\frac{m}{\rho}+\rho^{\theta}s)[1-s^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}{\rm d}s\leq\frac{C}{\rho}.

Then the conclusion follows directly.
∎

By Lemma
7.5
, when
\psi
is of subquadratic growth at infinity,
the same arguments as those in the proof of Proposition
6.2

can be applied to the polytropic pressure case here, so we can obtain the desired entropy inequality
as in Proposition
6.2
.
Furthermore, the arguments in Proposition
6.3

can be applied directly to the polytropic pressure case for all
\gamma>1
,
so that we can also prove that
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})
is a weak
solution to (
1.1
).

To complete the proof of Theorem
7.2
, it remains to prove that,
when
\psi(s)=\psi_{E}(s)=\frac{1}{2}s^{2}
, the entropy inequality is still satisfied.
Inspired by the idea in
[
7
]
,
to approximate
\psi_{E}(s)=\frac{1}{2}s^{2}
, we construct a new cut-off function
\psi_{R}
as

\displaystyle\psi_{R}(s)=\int_{0}^{s}\int_{0}^{y}\psi_{R}^{\prime\prime}(w)\,{\rm d}w{\rm d}y,\qquad\psi_{R}^{\prime\prime}(s)=\begin{cases}1\ &\,\,\,\text{if $|s|\leq R$},\\
\frac{2R-|s|}{R}\ &\,\,\,\text{if $R\leq|s|\leq 2R$},\\
0\ &\,\,\,\text{if $|s|\geq 2R$}.\end{cases}

Then we have

\displaystyle\psi_{R}(s)=\begin{cases}\frac{|s|^{2}}{2}\ &\quad\text{if $|s|\leq R$},\\
\frac{R^{2}}{6}-\frac{R|s|}{2}+|s|^{2}-\frac{|s|^{3}}{6R}\,\quad&\quad\text{if $R\leq|s|\leq 2R$},\\
-\frac{7R^{2}}{6}+\frac{3R}{2}|s|\ &\quad\text{if $|s|\geq 2R$}.\end{cases}

(7.19)

The corresponding entropy and entropy flux are

\displaystyle\eta^{R}(\rho,\rho u)

\displaystyle=\rho\int_{-1}^{1}\psi_{R}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)[1-s^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}\,{\rm d}s,

\displaystyle q^{R}(\rho,\rho u)

\displaystyle=\rho\int_{-1}^{1}(u+\theta\rho^{\theta}s)\psi_{R}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)[1-s^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}\,{\rm d}s.

To estimate
q^{R}
, we decompose the interval
[-1,1]
into three parts:

I_{1}:=\{s\in[-1,1]\,:\,|u+\rho^{\theta}s|\leq R\}
,

I_{2}:=\{s\in[-1,1]\,:\,R<|u+\rho^{\theta}s|\leq 2R\}
,
and
I_{3}:=\{s\in[-1,1]\,:\,|u+\rho^{\theta}s|>2R\}
, and obtain

\displaystyle q^{R}(\rho,\rho u)

\displaystyle=\rho\int_{I_{1}}(u+\theta\rho^{\theta}s)\frac{1}{2}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)^{2}[1-s^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}\,{\rm d}s

(7.20)

\displaystyle+\rho\int_{I_{2}}(u+\theta\rho^{\theta}s)\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{6}-\frac{R}{2}|u+\rho^{\theta}s|+(u+\rho^{\theta}s)^{2}-\frac{1}{6R}|u+\rho^{\theta}s|^{3}\Big)[1-s^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle+\rho\int_{I_{3}}(u+\theta\rho^{\theta}s)\Big(-\frac{7R^{2}}{6}+\frac{3R}{2}|u+\rho^{\theta}s|\Big)[1-s^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq CR\rho\big(|u|+\rho^{\theta}\big)^{2}.

Similarly, we have

\eta^{R}(\rho,\rho u)\leq CR\rho\big(|u|+\rho^{\theta}\big).

(7.21)

Therefore, for fixed
R
, by the uniform bound (
7.16
) and Proposition
6.1
(recall that we have the same result for the polytropic pressure case), estimates (
7.20
)–(
7.21
)
imply the uniform integrability with respect to
\varepsilon
.
Thus, similar to the proof in Proposition
6.2
,
the Vitali convergence theorem implies that, for
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,\varphi_{\tau}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi_{\tau}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\qquad\text{for any $t\in[0,T]$},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}q^{R}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\varphi_{x}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}q^{R}(\tilde{U})\varphi_{x}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\qquad\text{for any $t\in[0,T]$}.

Again, similar to the proof in Proposition
6.2
,
(
7.16
) also implies that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\partial_{x}^{2}\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\to 0\qquad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

Since

\partial_{m}^{2}\eta^{R}(\rho,\rho u)=\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{-1}^{1}\psi_{R}^{\prime\prime}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)[1-s^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}\,{\rm d}s\leq\frac{C}{\rho},

we have

\partial_{m}^{2}\eta^{R}(\rho,\rho u)\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\zeta_{k}^{2}\leq C\rho\big(1+\frac{m^{2}}{\rho^{2}}+\rho^{\gamma-1}\big).

Since the constructed cut-off function
\psi_{R}\in C^{2}
and convex, so is the entropy
\eta^{R}
.
Therefore, we can apply the same arguments as in the proof of (
6.5
)
to obtain that, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\,(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\,\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\qquad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

A direct calculation shows that

\partial_{m}\eta^{R}(\rho,\rho u)=\int_{-1}^{1}\psi_{R}^{\prime}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)[1-s^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}\,{\rm d}s\leq CR.

Thus, similar to (
6.7
), we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U})\zeta_{k}(\tilde{U})\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle\leq C(\varphi,B_{0},\gamma,\rho_{\infty})\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}\varphi}\big(1+\frac{\tilde{m}^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}}+e^{*}(\tilde{\rho},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle+C(\varphi,R,\gamma,\rho_{\infty})\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}\varphi}\big(1+e^{*}(\tilde{\rho},\rho_{\infty})\big){\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t\Big)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C(T,E_{0,2},R),

where, in the
first inequality, we have used similar arguments to those
for (
3.18
) and, in the last inequality,
we have used Proposition
6.1
.
Similarly,
using (
3.5
) and (
6.1
),
we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}t\big]\leq C(T,E_{0,2}).

Then, by the same argument as the justification
of (
6.10
), we obtain that, for
a.e.

t\in[0,T]
,

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}^{\varepsilon}}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{U}^{\varepsilon})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tilde{W}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U})\Phi(\tilde{U})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tilde{W}(\tau)\qquad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

Combining the above results gives that,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
for
a.e.

t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi_{t}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}q^{R}(\tilde{U})\varphi_{x}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U})\Phi(\tilde{U})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tilde{W}(t)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\,\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\geq 0.

We now pass to the limit:
R\to\infty
.
From the first equality in (
7.20
), we have

q^{R}(\rho,\rho u)\leq C\rho\big(|u|+\rho^{\theta}\big)^{3},

where
C
is independent of
R
.
Then, by (
7.16
),
this bound and (
7.21
) imply the uniform integrability with respect to
R
.
On the other hand, notice that, in the vacuum region,
q^{R}=q_{E}=\eta^{R}=\eta_{E}=0
(in this proof,
we always use this fact and will not repeat it again).
In the non-vacuum region, we have

\displaystyle q^{R}(\rho,\rho u)-q_{E}(\rho,\rho u)

(7.22)

\displaystyle=\rho\int_{I_{2}}(u+\theta\rho^{\theta}s)\Big(\frac{R^{2}}{6}+\frac{1}{2}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)^{2}-\frac{1}{6R}|u+\rho^{\theta}s|^{3}-\frac{R}{2}|u+\rho^{\theta}s|\Big)[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\lambda}\,ds

\displaystyle+\rho\int_{I_{3}}(u+\theta\rho^{\theta}s)\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)^{2}+\frac{3R}{2}|u+\rho^{\theta}s|-\frac{7R^{2}}{6}\Big)[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\lambda}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq C\rho(|u|+\rho^{\theta})^{3}\int_{\{s\,:\,|u+\rho^{\theta}s|\geq R\}}[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\lambda}\,{\rm d}s,

and similarly,

\eta^{R}(\rho,\rho u)-\eta_{E}(\rho,\rho u)\leq C\rho(|u|+\rho^{\theta})^{2}\int_{\{\,s:\,|u+\rho^{\theta}s|\geq R\}}[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\lambda}\,{\rm d}s,

(7.23)

where the measure of set
\{s\,:\,|u+\rho^{\theta}s|\geq R\}
tends to zero when
R\to\infty
.
Thus, it follows from (
7.16
)
and (
7.22
) that, as
R\to\infty
,

\displaystyle q^{R}(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\rho}\tilde{u})\to q_{E}(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\rho}\tilde{u})\qquad\text{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-{\it a.s.} almost everywhere}.

By Proposition
6.1

and (
7.23
), we also obtain
that
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
for almost every
t\in[0,T]
,

\eta^{R}(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\rho}\tilde{u})\to\eta_{E}(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\rho}\tilde{u})\qquad\text{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-{\it a.s.} {\it a.e.}}.

Therefore, using these convergence results, combined with the integrability ensured
by (
7.16
) and Propositions
6.1
,
we apply the dominated convergence theorem to obtain that,
for
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi_{\tau}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{E}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi_{\tau}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}q^{R}(\tilde{U})\varphi_{x}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{E}(\tilde{U})\varphi_{x}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

We observe that

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{m}^{2}\eta^{R}(\rho,\rho u)-\partial_{m}^{2}\eta_{E}(\rho,\rho u)\big)\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\zeta_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{-1}^{1}\big(\psi_{R}^{\prime\prime}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)-\psi_{E}^{\prime\prime}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)\big)[1-s^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}\,{\rm d}s\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\zeta_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\rho\Big(1+\frac{m^{2}}{\rho^{2}}+e(\rho)\Big)\int_{-1}^{1}\big|\psi_{R}^{\prime\prime}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)-\psi_{E}^{\prime\prime}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)\big|[1-s^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}\,{\rm d}s,

(7.24)

where, in the last inequality, we have used the growth
condition (
2.30
) for the noise coefficient.
Since, as
R\to\infty
,

\psi_{R}^{\prime\prime}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)-\psi_{E}^{\prime\prime}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)=\big(1-\frac{|u+\rho^{\theta}s|}{R}\big)\mathbf{1}_{\{s\,:\,R\leq|u+\rho^{\theta}s|<2R\}}+\mathbf{1}_{\{s\,:\,|u+\rho^{\theta}s|\geq 2R\}}\to 0,

by (
7.16
)
and the bounded convergence theorem, we have

\big(\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta^{R}(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\rho}\tilde{u})-\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta_{E}(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{\rho}\tilde{u})\big)\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\zeta_{k}^{2}\to 0\qquad\text{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-{\it a.s.} almost everywhere \quad as $R\to\infty$}.

Additionally, (
7.24
) also implies that

\displaystyle|\partial_{m}^{2}\eta^{R}(\rho,\rho u)-\partial_{m}^{2}\eta_{E}(\rho,\rho u)|\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\zeta_{k}^{2}\leq C\rho\Big(1+\frac{m^{2}}{\rho^{2}}+e(\rho)\Big),

which, by (
7.16
) again,
enables us to apply the dominated convergence theorem to obtain that, for almost every
t\in[0,T]
,

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\,\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta_{E}(\tilde{U})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\,\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\qquad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

Notice that

\displaystyle\psi_{R}^{\prime}(y)=\begin{cases}y\ &\quad\text{if $|y|\leq R$},\\
-\frac{R}{2}+2y-\frac{1}{2R}y^{2}\ &\quad\text{if $R<y\leq 2R$},\\
\frac{R}{2}+2y+\frac{1}{2R}y^{2}\ &\quad\text{if $-2R\leq y<-R$},\\
\frac{3R}{2}\ &\quad\text{if $y>2R$},\\
-\frac{3R}{2}\ &\quad\text{if $y<-2R$}.\end{cases}

Then we have

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{m}\eta^{R}(\rho,\rho u)-\partial_{m}\eta_{E}(\rho,\rho u)\big)\zeta_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{I_{2}}\big(|u+\rho^{\theta}s|+\frac{R}{2}+\frac{1}{2R}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)^{2}\big)[1-s^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}\,{\rm d}s+\int_{I_{3}}\big(|u+\rho^{\theta}s|+\frac{3R}{2}\big)[1-s^{2}]^{\lambda}_{+}\,{\rm d}s\Big)\zeta_{k}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|u|+\rho^{\theta}\big)\zeta_{k}\int_{\{s:|u+\rho^{\theta}s|\geq R\}}[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\lambda}\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\rho(|u|+\rho^{\theta})^{2}+\frac{1}{\rho}\zeta_{k}^{2}\big)\int_{\{s:|u+\rho^{\theta}s|\geq R\}}[1-s^{2}]_{+}^{\lambda}\,{\rm d}s,

(7.25)

where
C
is independent of
R
.
Thus, by the growth condition of the noise coefficient (
2.30
) and (
7.16
) again, for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, as
R\to\infty
,

\big(\partial_{m}\eta^{R}(\rho,\rho u)-\partial_{m}\eta_{E}(\rho,\rho u)\big)\zeta_{k}\to 0\qquad\mbox{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-{\it a.s.} almost everywhere}.

On the other hand, (
7.25
) also implies that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U})-\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta_{E}(\tilde{U})\big)\zeta_{k}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}t\big]

\displaystyle\leq C(\varphi,\gamma,\rho_{\infty})\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}(\varphi)}\big(1+\frac{\tilde{m}^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}}+e^{*}(\tilde{\rho},\rho_{\infty})\big){\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t\Big)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle+C(\varphi,B_{0},\gamma,\rho_{\infty})\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}(\varphi)}\big(1+\frac{\tilde{m}^{2}}{\tilde{\rho}}+e^{*}(\tilde{\rho},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C(T,E_{0,2}).

Therefore, using a similar argument to the justification
of (
6.9
)
but applying the dominated convergence theorem
(ensured by (
7.25
)) twice to pass to the limit that

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{0}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\big(\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U})-\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta_{E}(\tilde{U})\big)a_{k}\,\zeta_{k}(\tilde{U})\,{\rm d}x\Big|^{2}\,{\rm d}t\big]\to 0\qquad\text{as $R\to\infty$,}

where
N_{0}
is large enough as in the justification of (
6.9
),
we obtain by the same arguments as the justification of
(
6.10
) that, for
a.e.

t\in[0,T]
,

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta^{R}(\tilde{U})\Phi(\tilde{U})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tilde{W}(\tau)\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta_{E}(\tilde{U})\Phi(\tilde{U})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tilde{W}(\tau)\qquad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

Finally, we combine the above results to obtain that,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-
a.s.
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{E}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi_{t}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}q_{E}(\tilde{U})\varphi_{x}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta_{E}(\tilde{U})\Phi(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tilde{W}(t)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta_{E}(\tilde{U})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\,\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\,\varphi\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\geq 0.

This completes the proof of Theorem
7.2
.

7.3
Higher-Order Energy Estimate and More Convex Entropy Pairs for the Entropy Inequality

For the polytropic pressure case, we also have a higher-order energy estimate as
in Proposition
6.4
.
The proof is similar to and much simpler than that for
Proposition
6.4
, once we note that the higher-order
energy
\eta_{\diamond}
is exactly the entropy
\eta^{\psi_{\diamond}}
with generating
function
\psi_{\diamond}=\frac{1}{12}s^{4}
so that this entropy
\eta_{\diamond}
is convex.

In this subsection, we aim at establishing the following theorems:

Theorem 7.4
.

Assume that the initial data
(\rho_{0},m_{0})\in L^{\gamma}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})\times L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})

satisfy
\mathcal{E}(\rho_{0},m_{0})<\infty
and additionally have a finite relative higher-order energy
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\diamond}(\rho_{0},m_{0}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{1}{12}\frac{m_{0}^{4}}{\rho_{0}^{3}}+\frac{e(\rho_{0})}{\rho_{0}}m_{0}^{2}+e_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho_{0},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x<\infty.

Then

(i)

There exists a martingale entropy solution with finite relative-energy to (
1.1
) in the sense of
Definition
7.1
with (
7.4
) replaced by

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}\frac{\psi(s)}{s^{3}}=0,\quad\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}\frac{\psi^{\prime}(s)}{s^{2}}=0\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\text{for $\gamma\leq 3$},

(7.26)

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}\frac{\psi(s)}{s^{3}}=0,\quad\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}\frac{\psi^{\prime}(s)}{s^{2}}=0,\quad\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}\frac{\psi^{\prime\prime}(s)}{s}=0\qquad\,\,\,\text{for $\gamma>3$}.

(ii)

Let
U^{\delta}:=(\rho^{\delta},m^{\delta})
be a sequence of entropy solution to (
1.1
) obtained in
(i)
corresponding to a sequence of initial data
(\rho_{0}^{\delta},m_{0}^{\delta})
satisfying

\mathcal{E}_{\diamond}(\rho_{0}^{\delta},m_{0}^{\delta})\leq C,

where constant
C
is independent of
\delta
.
Then
U^{\delta}
satisfies that, for any
K\Subset\mathbb{R}
, there exists
C(K,p,T)>0
independent of
\delta
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{K}\big(\rho^{\delta}P(\rho^{\delta})+\frac{|m^{\delta}|^{3}}{(\rho^{\delta})^{2}}+\frac{(m^{\delta})^{4}}{(\rho^{\delta})^{3}}+(\rho^{\delta})^{\gamma-2}(m^{\delta})^{2}+(\rho^{\delta})^{2\gamma-1}\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{p}\big]\leq C(K,p,T),

(7.27)

where
P(\rho)
satisfies (
1.2
), so that
U^{\delta}:=(\rho^{\delta},m^{\delta})
are tight in

L^{\gamma+1}_{\rm w,loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\times L^{\frac{3(\gamma+1)}{\gamma+3}}_{\rm w,loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})

and admit the Skorokhod representation
(\tilde{\rho}^{\delta},\tilde{m}^{\delta})
. Moreover, there exist random variables
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})
such that
almost surely
(\tilde{\rho}^{\delta},\,\tilde{m}^{\delta})\to(\tilde{\rho},\,\tilde{m})

almost everywhere and
(
up to a subsequence
)
almost surely,

(\tilde{\rho}^{\delta},\,\tilde{m}^{\delta})\to(\tilde{\rho},\,\tilde{m})\qquad\text{in $L^{\bar{p}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\times L^{\bar{q}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}

for
\bar{p}\in[1,\gamma+1)
and
\bar{q}\in[1,\frac{3(\gamma+1)}{\gamma+3})
. In addition,
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})
is also an entropy solution to (
1.1
), i.e., almost surely satisfies the entropy inequality (
2.34
).

Similarly, the above result can be more general, allowing random initial data.

Theorem 7.5
.

Assume that
\Im
is a Borel probability measure on
L^{\gamma}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})\times L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle supp}\,\Im=\{(\rho,m)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\rho\geq 0\},

\displaystyle\int_{L^{\gamma}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})\times L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})}\big|\mathcal{E}(\rho,m)\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}\Im(\rho,m)+\int_{L^{\gamma}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})\times L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})}\big|\mathcal{E}_{\diamond}(\rho,m)\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}\Im(\rho,m)<\infty.

Then

(i)

There exists a martingale entropy solution with finite relative-energy
to (
1.1
), having initial
law
\Im=\mathbb{P}\circ(\rho_{0},m_{0})^{-1}
,
in the sense of
Definition

7.1

with (
7.4
) replaced by
(
7.26
)
;

(ii)

Let
U^{\delta}:=(\rho^{\delta},m^{\delta})
be a sequence of entropy solution to (
1.1
) obtained in
(i)
corresponding to a sequence
of initial law
\Im^{\delta}=\mathbb{P}\circ(\rho_{0}^{\delta},m_{0}^{\delta})^{-1}

satisfying

\displaystyle\int_{L^{\gamma}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})\times L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})}\big|\mathcal{E}(\rho,m)\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}\Im^{\delta}(\rho,m)+\int_{L^{\gamma}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})\times L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})}\big|\mathcal{E}_{\diamond}(\rho,m)\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}\Im^{\delta}(\rho,m)\leq C,

where constant
C>0
is independent of
\delta
.
Then
U^{\delta}
satisfies that, for any
K\Subset\mathbb{R}
, there exists
C(K,p,T)>0
independent of
\delta
such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{K}\big(\rho^{\delta}P(\rho^{\delta})+\frac{|m^{\delta}|^{3}}{(\rho^{\delta})^{2}}+\frac{(m^{\delta})^{4}}{(\rho^{\delta})^{3}}+(\rho^{\delta})^{\gamma-2}(m^{\delta})^{2}+(\rho^{\delta})^{2\gamma-1}\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{p}\big]\leq C(K,p,T),

(7.28)

where
P(\rho)
satisfies (
1.2
), so that
U^{\delta}:=(\rho^{\delta},m^{\delta})
are tight in

L^{\gamma+1}_{\rm w,loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\times L^{\frac{3(\gamma+1)}{\gamma+3}}_{\rm w,loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})

and admit the Skorokhod representation
(\tilde{\rho}^{\delta},\tilde{m}^{\delta})
. Moreover, there exist random variables
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})
such that
almost surely
(\tilde{\rho}^{\delta},\,\tilde{m}^{\delta})\to(\tilde{\rho},\,\tilde{m})

almost everywhere and
(
up to a subsequence
)
almost surely,

(\tilde{\rho}^{\delta},\,\tilde{m}^{\delta})\to(\tilde{\rho},\,\tilde{m})\qquad\text{in $L^{\bar{p}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\times L^{\bar{q}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}

for
\bar{p}\in[1,\gamma+1)
and
\bar{q}\in[1,\frac{3(\gamma+1)}{\gamma+3})
. In addition,
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})
is also an entropy solution to (
1.1
), i.e., almost surely satisfies the entropy inequality (
2.34
).

As before, it remains to prove Theorem
7.5
.
Thanks to the explicit formula for the entropy pair in the polytropic pressure case,
we have better control of the growth of the entropy pair,
which is stated in
Lemma
7.6
below,
and thus we can obtain
the entropy inequality for more entropy pairs,
compared to the general pressure law case.

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
\psi\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
be convex and satisfy that there exists
\delta\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle|\psi(s)|\leq C|s|^{3-\delta},\quad|\psi^{\prime}(s)|\leq C|s|^{2-\delta}\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{for $\gamma\leq 3$},

\displaystyle|\psi(s)|\leq C|s|^{3-\delta},\quad|\psi^{\prime}(s)|\leq C|s|^{2-\delta},\quad\psi^{\prime\prime}(s)\leq C|s|^{1-\delta}\qquad\text{for $\gamma>3$}.

Then

|\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+|q^{\psi}(\rho,u)|+\frac{1}{\rho}|\partial_{u}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|^{2}+\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}|\partial_{u}^{2}\eta^{\psi}(\rho,u)|\sum_{k}\zeta_{k}^{2}\leq C\big(1+\rho|u|^{4}+g(\rho)+\rho P(\rho)\big).

The proof is similar to that of
Lemmas
6.2
and
7.5
. Therefore, we omit the details.

By Lemma
7.6

and a similar proof
to that of
Proposition
6.5
,
we can establish the entropy inequality for entropy pairs with convex
generating functions
of sub-cubic
growth (see (
7.26
)):

Proposition 7.4
.

Assume that
U_{0}
satisfies
E_{0,2}<\infty
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\diamond}^{*}(\rho_{0},m_{0})\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{2}\big]<\infty.

Then, for any entropy pair
(\eta,q)
defined in (
2.13
)
with convex generating
function
\psi\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})

satisfying (
7.26
),
the following entropy inequality holds
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\eta(\tilde{U})\varphi_{t}+q(\tilde{U})\varphi_{x}\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}}\eta(\tilde{U})\Phi(\tilde{U})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}W(t)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi\partial_{\tilde{m}}^{2}\eta(\tilde{U})\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}\,\zeta_{k}^{2}(\tilde{U})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\geq 0,

for any nonnegative
\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R})
,
where
\tilde{U}=(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{m})^{\top}
.

Then, result (i) in Theorem
7.5

has been proved.
Result (ii) follows from almost the same arguments as those for
Theorem
6.1
, but here we should apply
Theorem
7.3

with
H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
in
(
7.12
) replaced by
W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
, which can actually be established
by almost the same arguments as those for
Theorem
5.1
.
This completes the proof of (ii) in
Theorem
7.5
.

8
Proof of Theorem
3.1

To prove Theorem
3.1
, we first prove the existence and uniqueness for the cut-off equations and then remove the cut-off, based on some uniform estimates with respect to the cut-off.
From now on, we omit subscript
\varepsilon
for simplicity whenever no confusion arises.

8.1
Well-Posedness for the Cut-Off Equations

Given
f\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R})
, we define

\Pi_{R}f=\frac{f}{\|f\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}}\min\{R,\|f\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}\}.

Consider the following cut-off equations:

\begin{cases}{\rm d}\rho+\partial_{x}(\rho\Pi_{R}u)\,{\rm d}t=\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\rho\,{\rm d}t,\\[1.42262pt]
{\rm d}u+\Pi_{R}u\partial_{x}(\Pi_{R}u)\,{\rm d}t+\frac{P^{\prime}(\rho)}{\rho}\partial_{x}\rho\,{\rm d}t\\
\quad=2\varepsilon\rho^{-1}\partial_{x}\rho\partial_{x}(\Pi_{R}u)\,{\rm d}t+\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}u\,{\rm d}t+\rho^{-1}\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\rho,\rho\Pi_{R}u)\,{\rm d}W(t)\end{cases}

(8.1)

with initial data
(\rho,u)|_{t=0}=(\rho_{0},u_{0})
. Inspired by the method in
[
5
]
, we first solve equation
\eqref{eq-cut-off-parabolic-approximation}_{1}
in the pathwise sense for given
u
, obtaining the solution, which will be denoted by
\bar{\rho}
in the proof.
Then we plug
\bar{\rho}
in
\eqref{eq-cut-off-parabolic-approximation}_{2}
and use the Banach fixed point theorem to obtain
the local solution
\bar{u}
for
\eqref{eq-cut-off-parabolic-approximation}_{2}
,
define the map:
u\mapsto\bar{u}
, and finally prove that this map has a fixed point. Then
this local solution can be extended to the whole time interval
[0,T]
.
The result can be stated as follows:

Lemma 8.1
.

Assume that
U_{0}
satisfies the conditions in
Theorem
3.1
and

\|\rho_{0}-\rho_{\infty}\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}<R\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-{\it a.s.}},\qquad u_{0}\in L^{p}(\Omega;H^{4}(\mathbb{R}))\quad\mbox{for $p\geq 1$}.

Then there exist a unique strong solution to the cut-off
equations (
8.1
) in the
solution space
X_{T}
, which contains all predictable processes

(\rho,u)
satisfying that, for
p\geq 1
,

(\rho-\rho_{\infty},u)\in L^{p}(\Omega;C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}))),\quad\,\,\|\rho^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{1}(\varepsilon):=e^{C(T,c_{0},\varepsilon,R)}\,\,\,\,\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$-{\it a.s.}}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into eight steps and only give the sketch of most of the proof.

1
. Given
u\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}))
, there exists a unique solution
\bar{\rho}

of the Cauchy problem for equation
\eqref{eq-cut-off-parabolic-approximation}_{1}
satisfying
\rho^{*}:=\bar{\rho}-\rho_{\infty}\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}))

\mathbb{P}
-
a.s.
.

The proof can be done by the standard Banach fixed-point theorem.

2
.
Using the assumption that

\|\rho_{0}-\rho_{\infty}\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}<R

\mathbb{P}
-
a.s.
and by
direct energy estimate (so we omit the details), we can obtain

\|\bar{\rho}-\rho_{\infty}\|_{C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}))}\leq C(R)\quad\,\,\mathbb{P}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

(8.2)

3
. Claim:
\|{\bar{\rho}}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{1}(\varepsilon):=e^{C(T,c_{0},\varepsilon,R)}

\mathbb{P}
-
a.s.
.

Denote the heat kernel on
\mathbb{R}
by

K(t,x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{4\pi t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4t}}

(8.3)

and the corresponding heat semigroup by
\mathbb{S}(t)
,
i.e.
,

\mathbb{S}(t)f(x)=K(t)*f(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}K(t,x-y)f(y)\,{\rm d}y.

For
K_{\varepsilon}(t,x):=K(\varepsilon t,x)
,
the corresponding heat semigroup is denoted by
\mathbb{S}_{\varepsilon}(t)
.

We first rewrite equation
\eqref{eq-cut-off-parabolic-approximation}_{1}
as

{\rm d}\bar{\rho}+\partial_{x}(\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u)\,{\rm d}t=\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\bar{\rho}\,{\rm d}t,

(8.4)

where
u\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}))
is given.
Let
v=\log\bar{\rho}
. Then,
from (
8.4
), we have

\displaystyle v(t,x)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(t,x-y)v_{0}(y)\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\varepsilon(v_{x}-\frac{\Pi_{R}u}{2\varepsilon})^{2}-\frac{(\Pi_{R}u)^{2}}{4\varepsilon}-\partial_{x}\Pi_{R}u\big)(\tau,y)K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau.

Since

\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(t,x-y)\,{\rm d}y=1,

we have

\displaystyle v(t,x)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(t,x-y)v_{0}(y)\,{\rm d}y+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(-\frac{(\Pi_{R}u)^{2}}{4\varepsilon}-\partial_{x}\Pi_{R}u\big)(\tau,y)K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau

\displaystyle\geq\log c_{0}-\frac{\|\Pi_{R}u\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R})}^{2}}{4\varepsilon}t-\|\partial_{x}\Pi_{R}u\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R})}t

\displaystyle\geq\log c_{0}-\frac{R^{2}}{4\varepsilon}t-Rt:=-C(t,c_{0},\varepsilon,R),

where, in the last inequality, we have used the Sobolev embedding:
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
.
Therefore, we obtain

\|{\bar{\rho}}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{1}(\varepsilon):=e^{C(T,c_{0},\varepsilon,R)}\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

(8.5)

4
. Claim:
\bar{u}\in L^{p}(\Omega;C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R})))

for
p\geq 1
.

We now plug the solution
\bar{\rho}
of
\eqref{eq-cut-off-parabolic-approximation}_{1}
in
\eqref{eq-cut-off-parabolic-approximation}_{2}
and linearize it as follows:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}\bar{u}+\Pi_{R}u\partial_{x}(\Pi_{R}u)\,{\rm d}t+\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}\,{\rm d}t

(8.6)

\displaystyle=2\varepsilon\bar{\rho}^{-1}\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}\partial_{x}(\Pi_{R}u)\,{\rm d}t+\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}\bar{u}\,{\rm d}t+\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}}\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\bar{\rho},\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u)\,{\rm d}W(t),

where
u\in L^{p}(\Omega;C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R})))
is the
given predictable process.

Using the mild formulation,
we represent the solution to (
8.6
) as

\displaystyle\bar{u}(t,x)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(t,x-y)\bar{u}(0,y)\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)\tilde{F}(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}W(\tau),

where

\displaystyle\tilde{F}=-\Pi_{R}u\partial_{x}(\Pi_{R}u)-\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}+2\varepsilon\bar{\rho}^{-1}\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}\partial_{x}(\Pi_{R}u),\qquad\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}}\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\bar{\rho},\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u).

Then, for any
0\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq T
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{u}(t_{2},x)-\bar{u}(t_{1},x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2},x-y)-K_{\varepsilon}(t_{1},x-y)\big)\bar{u}(0,y)\,{\rm d}y+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau,x-y)\tilde{F}(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau,x-y)-K_{\varepsilon}(t_{1}-\tau,x-y)\big)\tilde{F}(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau,x-y)\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}W(\tau)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau,x-y)-K_{\varepsilon}(t_{1}-\tau,x-y)\big)\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}W(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{I}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbb{I}_{5}.

We first estimate
\mathbb{I}_{4}
:

\displaystyle I_{41}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\big[\Big\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau,x-y)\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}W(\tau)\Big\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\sum_{k}\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau,x-y)\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}}a_{k}\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(\bar{\rho},\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u)\,{\rm d}y\Big\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\,{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\|K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\sum_{k}\Big\|\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}}a_{k}\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(\bar{\rho},\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u)\Big\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\,{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{p}\big].

For
\sum_{k}\big\|\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}}a_{k}\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(\bar{\rho},\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u)\big\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}
,
we focus our estimates on two terms:

\sum_{k}\big\|\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}}a_{k}\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(\bar{\rho},\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}
and
\sum_{k}\big\|\partial_{x}^{3}\big(\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}}a_{k}\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(\bar{\rho},\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u)\big)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}
, since the other terms can be treated similarly.

For
\sum_{k}\big\|\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}}a_{k}\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(\bar{\rho},\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}
, we
use (
3.5
)
to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\Big\|\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}}a_{k}\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(\bar{\rho},\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u)\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B_{0}\int_{\text{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k})}\big(C+(\Pi_{R}u)^{2}+e(\bar{\rho})\big)\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq B_{0}\big(C+\|\Pi_{R}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}+\|e(\bar{\rho})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\big)|\text{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k})|\leq C(R),

where, in the last inequality, we have used the Sobolev
embedding:
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})

and estimates (
8.2
)
and
(
2.19
)–(
2.22
).
This simple argument is used frequently
and will not be
repeated.

For the term
\sum_{k}\big\|\partial_{x}^{3}\big(\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}}a_{k}\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(\bar{\rho},\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u)\big)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}
,

using
the fact that
\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(x,\rho,m)\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R}^{+},\mathbb{R})
,
\|\partial_{x}^{i}\partial_{\rho}^{j}\partial_{m}^{l}\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C(\varepsilon)
for

0\leq i,j,l\leq 3
,
estimates
(
8.2
)–(
8.5
),
the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality, and
the Sobolev embedding:
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\Big\|\partial_{x}^{3}\big(\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}}a_{k}\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(\bar{\rho},\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u)\big)\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\leq C(\varepsilon,C_{1}(\varepsilon),R)\big(C(R,\rho_{\infty})+|\text{supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})|^{2}\big).

Therefore, we have

I_{41}\leq C(\varepsilon,C_{1}(\varepsilon),R)(t_{2}-t_{1})^{p}.

For
\mathbb{I}_{5}
, similarly, we have

\displaystyle I_{51}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\big[\Big\|\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau,x-y)-K_{\varepsilon}(t_{1}-\tau,x-y)\big)\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}W(\tau)\Big\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\|K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau)-K_{\varepsilon}(t_{1}-\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\sum_{k}\sum_{j=0}^{3}\Big\|\partial_{x}^{j}\big(\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}}a_{k}\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(\bar{\rho},\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u)\big)\Big\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}(\tau)\,{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{p}\big],

where, by Lemma
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau)-K_{\varepsilon}(t_{1}-\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\partial_{t}K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau)\,{\rm d}t\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\|\partial_{t}K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\,{\rm d}t\leq 4(t_{1}-\tau)^{-\frac{1}{4}}-4(t_{2}-\tau)^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

Since

\displaystyle\Big\|\partial_{x}^{j}\big(\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}}a_{k}\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(\bar{\rho},\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u)\big)\Big\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\leq\Big\|\partial_{x}^{j}\big(\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}}a_{k}\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(\bar{\rho},\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u)\big)\Big\|_{L^{2}(\text{supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}))}^{2}|\text{supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})|,

we use the estimates similar to
\mathbb{I}_{4}
to obtain

\displaystyle I_{51}\leq C(\varepsilon,C_{1}(\varepsilon),R)\Big(\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\big((t_{1}-\tau)^{-\frac{1}{4}}-(t_{2}-\tau)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\big)^{2}\,{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{p}\leq C(\varepsilon,C_{1}(\varepsilon),R,T)(t_{2}-t_{1})^{\frac{p}{2}}.

(8.7)

Next, the other terms in
\mathbb{I}_{1}-\mathbb{I}_{3}
can be estimated similarly, so we omit the details but only estimate one subterm in
\mathbb{I}_{3}
which requires more careful analysis.
We first split a subterm in
\mathbb{I}_{3}
into two parts,

\displaystyle I_{32}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\Big\|\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau,y)\big(K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau,x-y)-K_{\varepsilon}(t_{1}-\tau,x-y)\big)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\Big\|\partial_{x}^{j}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)\tau,y)\big(K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau,x-y)-K_{\varepsilon}(t_{1}-\tau,x-y)\big)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\Big)^{2p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\bigg(\mathbb{E}\big[\Big\|\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau,y)\big(K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau,x-y)-K_{\varepsilon}(t_{1}-\tau,x-y)\big)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\mathbb{E}\big[\Big\|\partial_{x}^{j}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau,y)\big(K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau,x-y)-K_{\varepsilon}(t_{1}-\tau,x-y)\big)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\bigg)

\displaystyle:=C(p)(I_{321}+I_{322}).

For
I_{321}
, we rewrite it as

\displaystyle I_{321}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\Big\|\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau,y)\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\partial_{t}K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)\,{\rm d}t{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\Big\|\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau,y)K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}t{\rm d}\tau\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big].

Note that, denoting

f(\tau,y)=\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau,y)
,
then

\mathcal{U}(t-\tau,x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau,y)K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)\,{\rm d}y=\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(\tau,y)K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)\,{\rm d}y

can be seen as the solution to the following heat equation with fixed
\tau

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathcal{U}_{t}-\Delta\mathcal{U}=0\qquad\mbox{for $x\in\mathbb{R}$},\\
\mathcal{U}(0,y)=f(\tau,y),\end{cases}

where
\Delta=\partial_{x}^{2}
.
Therefore, we have

\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau,y)K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)\,{\rm d}y=\Delta\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau,y)K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)\,{\rm d}y

and

\displaystyle I_{321}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\Big\|\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\Delta\mathbb{S}_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau)\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau)\,{\rm d}t{\rm d}\tau\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\|\Delta\mathbb{S}_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau)\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\,{\rm d}t{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{2p}\big].

For
b>0
and
\nu
satisfying
\frac{b+\nu}{2}=1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Delta\mathbb{S}_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau)\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle=\|-(-\Delta)^{\frac{b}{2}}(-\Delta)^{\frac{\nu}{2}}\mathbb{S}_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau)\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\leq\|(-\Delta)^{\frac{b}{2}}\mathbb{S}_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau)(I-\Delta)^{\frac{\nu}{2}}\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\leq C|t-\tau|^{-\frac{b}{2}}\|\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau)\|_{H^{\nu}(\mathbb{R})},

where, in the first and the second inequality, we have used (i) and (ii)
in Lemma
A.6
respectively.
Then, using the above estimate, taking
b=3
and
\nu=-1
, and by Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle I_{321}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\,{\rm d}t{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{2p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C(C_{1}(\varepsilon),R)\Big(\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\,{\rm d}t{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{2p}\leq C(C_{1}(\varepsilon),R)(t_{2}-t_{1})^{p},

where, in the second inequality, we have used the fact: when
\gamma_{2}-2\geq 0
,

\|\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{\gamma_{2}-2}\leq(\|\bar{\rho}-\rho_{\infty}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\rho_{\infty})^{\gamma_{2}-2},

combined with the Sobolev embedding:
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
(
2.18
)–(
2.21
), (
8.2
), and (
8.5
).

\displaystyle I_{322}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\mathbb{E}\big[\Big\|\partial_{x}^{j}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)(\tau,y)\big(K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau,x-y)-K_{\varepsilon}(t_{1}-\tau,x-y)\big)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{3}\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\|\partial_{y}^{j-1}\big(\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho})}{\bar{\rho}}\partial_{y}\bar{\rho}\big)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}(\tau)\|\partial_{x}K_{\varepsilon}(t_{2}-\tau)-\partial_{x}K_{\varepsilon}(t_{1}-\tau)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\,{\rm d}\tau\Big)^{2p}\big].

Then, using a similar estimate to
I_{321}
, we have

I_{322}\leq C(C_{1}(\varepsilon),R)(t_{2}-t_{1})^{p}.

Thus, combining
estimates for
\mathbb{I}_{1}-\mathbb{I}_{5}
,
we have

\mathbb{E}\big[\|\bar{u}(t_{2})-\bar{u}(t_{1})\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C\big(\mathbb{E}[\|\bar{u}(0)\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}],R,\varepsilon,C_{1}(\varepsilon),T\big)(t_{2}-t_{1})^{\frac{p}{2}}.

Therefore, choosing
\frac{p}{2}>1
and applying the Kolmogorov criterion,
we obtain

\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\bar{u}(t)\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{p}\big]\leq C\big(\mathbb{E}\big[\|\bar{u}(0)\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big],R,\varepsilon,C_{1}(\varepsilon),T\big)

and
\bar{u}\in L^{p}(\Omega;C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R})))
for
p\geq 1
.

5
. The mild solution
\bar{u}
is also a weak solution,
hence also the strong solution.

Recall the representation of
\bar{u}
in Step
4
.
The estimates in Step
4
give

\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k}\Big\|\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}}a_{k}\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(\bar{\rho},\bar{\rho}\Pi_{R}u)\Big\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\,{\rm d}\tau\big]<\infty.

Since
\bar{\rho}
and
u
are predictable, we have
\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}
is predictable.
Hence, we can use
[
20
, Proposition 6.2]

to obtain that the stochastic convolution

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}W(\tau)

has a predictable version, which implies that
\bar{u}
has a predictable version.
Then, by a straightforward adaptation of

[
20
, Proposition 6.4 (i)]
,
we can prove that the mild solution
\bar{u}
is also a weak solution,
i.e.
, satisfies that, for any
t\in[0,T]
and
\phi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,

\langle\bar{u}(t),\phi\rangle-\langle\bar{u}(0),\phi\rangle=\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\langle\bar{u}(\tau),\partial_{x}^{2}\phi\rangle\,{\rm d}\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\langle\tilde{F}(\tau),\phi\rangle\,{\rm d}\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\langle\phi,\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\rangle\,{\rm d}W(\tau)\quad\,\,\mathbb{P}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

Since, by Step
4
,

\bar{u}\in L^{p}(\Omega;C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R})))
for
p\geq 1
,
we also see that
\bar{u}
is also a strong solution.
Therefore, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\bar{u}(t)=\bar{u}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{x}^{2}\bar{u}(\tau)\,{\rm d}\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{F}(\tau)\,{\rm d}\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\Phi}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\,{\rm d}W(\tau)\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-{\it a.s.}}.

(8.8)

6
.
Using the fact that given
f,g\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R})
,
\|\Pi_{R}f-\Pi_{R}g\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\|f-g\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}
for any
R>0
, which can be easily verified, we can directly prove that

\|\bar{\rho}_{1}-\bar{\rho}_{2}\|_{C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}))}\leq C\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}))},

(8.9)

so we omit the details.

7
. There exists sufficiently small
T^{*}>0
such that the map
\mathcal{T}(u):=\bar{u}
is a contraction on
L^{p}(\Omega,C([0,T^{*}];H^{3}(\mathbb{R})))
, which implies that (
8.1
) has a unique strong solution in
X_{T^{*}}
.

By the mild formulation of (
8.6
), we have

\displaystyle\bar{u}_{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(t,x-y)\bar{u}_{i}(0,y)\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{F}_{i}(\tau,y)K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)\tilde{\Phi}_{i\varepsilon}(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}W(\tau),

where

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{i}=-\Pi_{R}u_{i}\partial_{x}(\Pi_{R}u_{i})-\frac{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho}_{i})}{\bar{\rho}_{i}}\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}_{i}+2\varepsilon\bar{\rho}_{i}^{-1}\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}_{i}\partial_{x}(\Pi_{R}u_{i}),\quad\tilde{\Phi}_{i\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{i}}\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\bar{\rho}_{i},\bar{\rho}_{i}\Pi_{R}u_{i}).

Note that

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}_{1}^{\alpha}-\bar{\rho}_{2}^{\alpha}=\int_{0}^{1}\alpha\big(a\bar{\rho}_{2}+(1-a)\bar{\rho}_{1}\big)^{\alpha-1}da(\bar{\rho}_{2}-\bar{\rho}_{1})\qquad\mbox{for $\alpha\in\mathbb{R}$ with $\alpha\neq 0,1$}.

(8.10)

Then, similar to the estimates for
\mathbb{I}_{2}
in Step
4
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{t\in[0,r]}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\tilde{F}_{1}-\tilde{F}_{2})(\tau,y)K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau\Big\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C\big(R,C_{1}(\varepsilon),T\big)r^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{t\in[0,r]}\|(\bar{\rho}_{1}-\bar{\rho}_{2},u_{1}-u_{2})\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{p}\big].

Notice that

\displaystyle\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(V_{1})-\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k}(V_{2})=\int_{0}^{1}(\nabla_{V}\zeta^{\varepsilon}_{k})(aV_{1}+(1-a)V_{2})\,{\rm d}a(V_{1}-V_{2}),

(8.11)

where
V_{i}=(\bar{\rho}_{i},\bar{\rho}_{i}\Pi_{R}u_{i})
for
i=1,2
.
Then,
by (
8.10
)–(
8.11
),
as the estimates for
\mathbb{I}_{4}-\mathbb{I}_{5}
in Step
4
,
we can use the Kolmogorov criterion to prove that the stochastic
integral:

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)\tilde{\Phi}_{i\varepsilon}(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}W(\tau)

is continuous, so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{t\in[0,r]}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(t-\tau,x-y)(\tilde{\Phi}_{1\varepsilon}-\tilde{\Phi}_{2\varepsilon})(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}W(\tau)\Big\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon,C_{1}(\varepsilon),R)r^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{t\in[0,r]}\|(\bar{\rho}_{1}-\bar{\rho}_{2},u_{1}-u_{2})\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{p}\big].

Combining the above estimates, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{t\in[0,r]}\|\bar{u}_{1}-\bar{u}_{2}\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{p}(t)\big]

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon,C_{1}(\varepsilon),T,R)r^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{t\in[0,r]}\|(\bar{\rho}_{1}-\bar{\rho}_{2},u_{1}-u_{2})\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon,C_{1}(\varepsilon),T,R)\big(1+C\big)r^{\frac{p}{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{t\in[0,r]}\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{p}\big],

where, in the last inequality, we have
used (
8.9
).
Hence, choosing sufficiently small
r
,
denoted as
T^{*}
, we conclude that
the map
\mathcal{T}(u):=\bar{u}
is a contraction on
L^{p}(\Omega,C([0,T^{*}];H^{3}(\mathbb{R})))
, which implies that
there exists a unique strong solution to
(
8.1
) in
X_{T^{*}}
.

8
. Claim: There exists a unique global solution
to (
8.1
).

Note that

T^{*}
in Step
7
depends on
C_{1}(\varepsilon)=e^{C(T,c_{0},\varepsilon,R)}
, which depends on the lower bound
c_{0}>0

of the initial data.
However, constant
C(T,c_{0},\varepsilon,R)
depends linearly on time
T
,
which enables us to extend the local solution obtained in Step
7

to obtain the global solution.
More specifically, we start from any time
0<t_{0}<T
, by (
8.5
),
we obtain that the initial density
\bar{\rho}(t_{0})
has a lower bound

e^{-C(t_{0},c_{0},\varepsilon,R)}
.
Then repeating the process in Step
3
and using the semigroup property
yield that, for
t_{0}<t\leq T
,

\displaystyle v(t,x)

\displaystyle\geq-C(t_{0},c_{0},\varepsilon,R)-\frac{\|\Pi_{R}u\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R})}^{2}}{4\varepsilon}(t-t_{0})-\|\partial_{x}\Pi_{R}u\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R})}(t-t_{0})

\displaystyle\geq\log c_{0}-\frac{R^{2}}{4\varepsilon}t_{0}-Rt_{0}-\frac{R^{2}}{4\varepsilon}(t-t_{0})-R(t-t_{0})=-C(t,c_{0},\varepsilon,R),

which implies that the same bound on
[t_{0},T]
is obtained:

\|{\bar{\rho}}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}([t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{1}(\varepsilon)=e^{C(T,c_{0},\varepsilon,R)}.

Therefore, we can start from
T^{*}
and solve
equation (
8.1
) on
[T^{*},2T^{*}]
.
Repeating this process, we obtain the unique global solution
to (
8.1
).
∎

In Theorem
8.1
below,
we relax the additional assumption

\|\rho_{0}-\rho_{\infty}\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}<R
and give the well-posedness result of (
8.1
) for more general initial data. The proof is direct, so we omit it.

Theorem 8.1
.

Assume that
U_{0}
satisfies the conditions in
Theorem
3.1
and

\rho_{0}-\rho_{\infty}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega;H^{3}(\mathbb{R})),\quad u_{0}\in L^{p}(\Omega;H^{4}(\mathbb{R}))\quad\mbox{for $p\geq 1$}.

Then there exist a unique strong solution to the cut-off equations (
8.1
) in the solution space
X_{T}
.

8.2
Regularity Estimates

We now establish the following regularity estimates, which will be used to remove the cut-off in §
8.3
. In the proof, we improve the regularity iteratively, inspired by the idea in
[
3
, Proposition 3.3]
where they obtained the regularity for bounded solutions. Since the cut-off is applied to
u
only, much more careful analysis is required.

Proposition 8.1
.

Assume that
U_{0}
satisfies the conditions
in
Theorem
3.1
and

\displaystyle\rho_{0}-\rho_{\infty}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega;H^{3}(\mathbb{R})),\qquad u_{0}\in L^{p}(\Omega;H^{4}(\mathbb{R}))\,\,\,\,\,\mbox{for $p\geq 1$},

\displaystyle(\rho_{0}-\rho_{\infty},u_{0})\in L^{q}(\Omega;H^{5}(\mathbb{R}))\,\,\,\,\,\mbox{for $q>12$}.

Then the solution
V:=(\rho,u)^{\top}
, obtained in
Theorem
8.1
,
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\partial_{x}^{4}V(r)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C\big(\mathbb{E}[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{5}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}],R,t,C_{1}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon\big)\quad\mbox{for $p>2$},

(8.12)

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\partial_{x}^{5}V(r)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C\big(\mathbb{E}\big[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{5}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big],\mathbb{E}\big[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{4p}\big],R,t,C_{1}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon\big)\quad\mbox{for $p>3$},

(8.13)

where
V_{0}^{*}(y)=(\rho-\rho_{\infty},u)^{\top}(0,y)
.
Therefore, for
p>3
and
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|(\rho-\rho_{\infty},u)(t)\|_{H^{5}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C\big(\mathbb{E}\big[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{5}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big],\mathbb{E}\big[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{4p}\big],R,T,C_{1}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon\big).

(8.14)

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1
. We first rewrite (
8.1
) in the following matrix form:

{\rm d}V+\hat{F}\,{\rm d}t=\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}V\,{\rm d}t+\hat{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}W(t),

(8.15)

where
V=(\rho,u)^{\top}
,
\hat{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}=(0,\frac{1}{\rho}\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\rho,\rho\Pi_{R}u))^{\top}
, and

\hat{F}=(\hat{F}_{1},\hat{F}_{2})^{\top}=(\partial_{x}(\rho\Pi_{R}u),\,\Pi_{R}u\partial_{x}(\Pi_{R}u)+\frac{P^{\prime}(\rho)}{\rho}\partial_{x}\rho-2\varepsilon\rho^{-1}\partial_{x}\rho\partial_{x}(\Pi_{R}u))^{\top}.

Denote
V^{*}=(\rho-\rho_{\infty},u)^{\top},V_{0}(y)=V(0,y)
,
and
V_{0}^{*}(y)=V^{*}(0,y)
.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{3}V(r,x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{S}_{\varepsilon}(r)\partial_{y}^{3}V_{0}(x)-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{x}K_{\varepsilon}(r-\tau,x-y)\,\partial_{y}^{2}\hat{F}(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\varepsilon}(r-\tau,x-y)\partial_{y}^{3}\hat{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}W(\tau).

For
\alpha\in(0,1)
, we consider
(I-\Delta)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\partial_{x}^{3}V
.
By Lemmas
A.1
–
A.2
,
we directly see that

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\big[\|(I-\Delta)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\mathbb{S}_{\varepsilon}(r)\partial_{y}^{3}V_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C\mathbb{E}\big[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big].

(8.16)

With this (
8.16
), using Lemma
A.7
, Lemma
A.6
(ii)–(iii),
and Lemmas
A.1
–
A.2
,
we can obtain by the standard estimate that, for
2p\geq 1
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
,

\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\partial_{x}^{3}V(r)\|_{H^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C(\mathbb{E}[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}],R,t,\alpha,C_{1}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon).

(8.17)

Additionally, repeating the above process for
\partial_{x}^{2}V
,
we see that, for
2p\geq 1
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
,

\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\partial_{x}^{2}V(r)\|_{H^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C(\mathbb{E}\big[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big],R,t,\alpha,C_{1}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon).

2
.
Let
\frac{1}{2}\leq\alpha<1
, and consider
(I-\Delta)^{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{3}V
. Similar to (
8.16
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\big[\|(I-\Delta)^{\alpha}\mathbb{S}_{\varepsilon}(r)\partial_{y}^{3}V_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C\mathbb{E}\big[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{5}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big].

We now consider two cases separately:
\alpha=\frac{1}{2}
and
\frac{1}{2}<\alpha<1
.
When
\alpha=\frac{1}{2}
, choosing
\alpha_{1}
satisfying
\frac{1}{2}<\alpha_{1}<1
, we apply the following decompositions:

\displaystyle(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{S}_{\varepsilon}(r-\tau)\partial_{y}^{3}\big(\frac{1}{\rho}\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\rho,\rho\Pi_{R}u)\big)(\tau)=(-\Delta)^{\frac{1-\alpha_{1}}{2}}\mathbb{S}_{\varepsilon}(r-\tau)(-\Delta)^{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{2}}\partial_{y}^{3}\big(\frac{1}{\rho}\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\rho,\rho\Pi_{R}u)\big)(\tau)

\displaystyle(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{x}K_{\varepsilon}(r-\tau,x-y)\partial_{y}^{2}\hat{F}(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(-\Delta)^{\frac{1-\alpha_{1}}{2}}\partial_{x}K_{\varepsilon}(r-\tau,x-y)(-\Delta)^{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{2}}\partial_{y}^{2}\hat{F}(\tau,y)\,{\rm d}y{\rm d}\tau.

Using (
8.17
), by the mean value theorem, (
2.18
)–(
2.21
), Lemma
A.3
–
A.5
, Lemma
A.6
(iii), Lemma
A.7
and Lemma
A.2
(i),
we obtain that,
for
p>\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\big[\|(I-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x}^{3}V(r)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C(\mathbb{E}[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{5}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}],R,t,\alpha_{1},C_{1}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon\big).

Similarly, when
\frac{1}{2}<\alpha<1
, we obtain that, for
p>\frac{1}{1-\alpha}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\big[\|(I-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x}^{3}V(r)\|_{H^{2\alpha-1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C(\mathbb{E}[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{5}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}],R,t,\alpha,C_{1}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon\big).

Therefore, using Lemma
A.6
(iii), we see that, for
p>\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}
with
\alpha_{1}
satisfying
\frac{1}{2}<\alpha_{1}<1
,

\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\partial_{x}^{4}V(r)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C(\mathbb{E}[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{5}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}],R,t,\alpha_{1},C_{1}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon\big).

and, for
\frac{1}{2}<\alpha<1
and
p>\frac{1}{1-\alpha}
,

\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\partial_{x}^{4}V(r)\|_{H^{2\alpha-1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C\big(\mathbb{E}[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{5}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}],R,t,\alpha,C_{1}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon\big).

Moreover, by Lemma
A.2
, choosing
\alpha>\frac{3}{4}
, we have

\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\partial_{x}^{4}V(r)\|_{C^{2\alpha-1-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C(\mathbb{E}[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{5}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}],R,t,\alpha,C_{1}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon\big).

In addition, repeating the above process regarding
\frac{1}{2}<\alpha<1
for
\partial_{x}^{2}V
, we also obtain that, for
\frac{1}{2}<\alpha<1
and
2p\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\big[\|(I-\Delta)^{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{2}V(r)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C(\mathbb{E}[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}],R,t,\alpha,C_{1}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon\big).

3
. We now consider
(I-\Delta)\partial_{x}^{3}V
. By similar arguments as those in Step
2
, we obtain that,
for
\frac{1}{2}<\alpha\leq\frac{2}{3}
and
p>\frac{1}{2\alpha-1}
,
or for
\frac{2}{3}<\alpha<1
and
p>\frac{1}{1-\alpha}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\big[\|(I-\Delta)\partial_{x}^{3}V(r)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C(\mathbb{E}[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{5}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}],\mathbb{E}[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{4p}],R,t,\alpha,C_{1}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon\big).V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{4p},R,\rho_{\infty},t,\alpha,\gamma,C_{1}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon,|\text{supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})|).

Then, by Lemma
A.6
(iii), we obtain that,
for
\frac{1}{2}<\alpha\leq\frac{2}{3}
and
p>\frac{1}{2\alpha-1}
,
or for
\frac{2}{3}<\alpha<1
and
p>\frac{1}{1-\alpha}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\partial_{x}^{5}V(r)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}\big]\leq C\big(\mathbb{E}[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{5}(\mathbb{R})}^{2p}],\mathbb{E}[\|V_{0}^{*}\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}^{4p}],R,t,\alpha,C_{1}(\varepsilon),\varepsilon\big).

Taking
\alpha=\frac{2}{3}
, then the proof is completed.
∎

8.3
Remove the Cut-Off

We denote the solution obtained
in Theorem
8.1
by
(\bar{\rho}_{R},\bar{u}_{R})

and define
\bar{m}_{R}:=\bar{\rho}_{R}\bar{u}_{R}
.
Then we pass to the limit
R\to\infty
to obtain
the solution to (
3.1
),
i.e.
, remove the cut-off. Toward this end, we define the stopping time

\tau_{R}=\inf\big\{t\in[0,T]\,:\,\|\bar{u}_{R}\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}(t)\geq R\big\}

with the convention that
\inf\emptyset=T
.
Note that
\tau_{R}
is increasing and
(\bar{\rho}_{R},\bar{u}_{R})=(\bar{\rho}_{R+1},\bar{u}_{R+1})
on
[0,\tau_{R}]
by uniqueness.
Therefore, we can define
\tau^{*}=\lim_{R\to\infty}\tau_{R}
and
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u}):=(\bar{\rho}_{R},\bar{u}_{R})
on
[0,\tau_{R}]
, which is the solution to (
3.1
) on
[0,\tau_{R}]
.
Then the next step is to prove that
\mathbb{P}(\tau^{*}=T)=1
,
which implies that
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u})
is the global solution to (
3.1
).
To prove that
\mathbb{P}(\tau^{*}=T)=1
,
we need some uniform
estimates with respect to
R
.

Before proving the uniform estimates, we first use the Sobolev embedding to obtain

Lemma 8.2
.

Assume that
U_{0}
satisfies the conditions in
Theorem
8.1
. Then the solution
(\bar{\rho}_{R},\bar{u}_{R})
satisfies
(\partial_{x}^{2}\bar{\rho}_{R},\partial_{x}^{2}\bar{u}_{R})(t,\cdot)\in C(\mathbb{R})
, so that
the cut-off equations (
8.1
) hold in the classical strong sense that, for any fixed
x
, equations (
8.1
) hold as a finite-dimensional stochastic differential equations.

We now derive the
lower bound for density
\rho
.

Proposition 8.2
.

Assume that
U_{0}
satisfies the conditions in

Theorem
8.1
.
Then the density has lower bound
:

\|{\bar{\rho}_{R}}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times[0,T])}\leq e^{C(T,c_{0},\varepsilon,\|\bar{u}_{R}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R})})}\,\,\,\mathbb{P}
-a.s..

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of (
8.5
) but here we use the integration by parts such that the lower bound depends on
\|\bar{u}_{R}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R})}
only,
i.e.
, is independent of

\|\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{R}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R})}
. So we omit the details.
∎

We now derive the
uniform
L^{\infty}
estimate with respect to
R
.

Proposition 8.3
.

Assume that
U_{0}
satisfies the conditions in

Theorem
8.1
.
Then the solution
(\bar{\rho}_{R},\bar{u}_{R})
on
[0,\tau_{R}]
is bounded, i.e.,
(\bar{\rho}_{R},\bar{u}_{R})\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}}

for all
t\in[0,\tau_{R}]

\mathbb{P}
-a.s..
Therefore,
\|\bar{u}_{R}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau_{R}]\times\mathbb{R})}\leq\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}
and
\|\bar{\rho}_{R}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau_{R}]\times\mathbb{R})}\leq(\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon})^{\frac{1}{\theta_{2}}}

\mathbb{P}
-a.s..

Proof.

We use the idea of the invariant region method
(for the invariant region method in the deterministic case, see
[
23
, §4]

and
[
18
]
for more details)
and take the Riemann invariant

w_{2}(\rho,m)=\frac{m}{\rho}+\mathcal{K}(\rho)
for an example.
Another Riemann invariant
w_{1}
can be dealt with similarly.

Notice that, on
[0,\tau_{R}]
, the cut-off equations (
3.1
) and (
8.1
)
are coincide.
Therefore, restricting ourselves on
\left[0,\tau_{R}\right)
,
by Lemma
8.2
,
we apply the Itô formula to derive the equation for
w_{2}
:

\displaystyle{\rm d}w_{2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}w_{2}\,{\rm d}t+\big(2\varepsilon\frac{\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}_{R}}{\bar{\rho}_{R}}-\lambda_{2}\big)\partial_{x}w_{2}\,{\rm d}t-\frac{\varepsilon(\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}_{R})^{2}}{2(\bar{\rho}_{R})^{2}\sqrt{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho}_{R})}}\big(2P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho}_{R})+\bar{\rho}_{R}P^{\prime\prime}(\bar{\rho}_{R})\big)\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad\,+\sum_{k}\frac{a_{k}}{\bar{\rho}_{R}}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}\beta_{k}(t),

(8.18)

where
\lambda_{2}=\frac{\bar{m}_{R}}{\bar{\rho}_{R}}+\sqrt{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho}_{R})}
.

Since
(\bar{\rho}_{R}-\rho_{\infty},\bar{u}_{R})\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}))

\mathbb{P}
-
a.s.
, by the Sobolev embedding,
(\bar{\rho}_{R},\bar{u}_{R})
is
\mathbb{P}
-
a.s.

continuous with respect to
(t,x)
.
Thus,
w_{2}(\bar{\rho}_{R},\bar{m}_{R})=\frac{\bar{m}_{R}}{\bar{\rho}_{R}}+\mathcal{K}(\bar{\rho}_{R})
is also
\mathbb{P}
-
a.s.
continuous
with respect to
(t,x)
.
Moreover,
\|\bar{\rho}_{R}-\rho_{\infty}\|_{C([0,T],L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}))}<\infty
and
\|\bar{u}_{R}\|_{C([0,T],L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}))}<\infty

\mathbb{P}
-
a.s.
, which implies that
\|w_{2}\|_{C([0,\tau_{R}],L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}))}<\infty

\mathbb{P}
-
a.s.
.
Additionally, since
(\bar{\rho}_{R}-\rho_{\infty},\bar{u}_{R})\in C([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}))

\mathbb{P}
-
a.s.
, we obtain that,
for each
t\in[0,T]
,
(\bar{\rho}_{R}-\rho_{\infty},\bar{u}_{R})(t,x)\to(0,0)
as
|x|\to\infty
.
Therefore, for each
t\in[0,T]
, for any
0<\delta\leq 1
,
there exists
L(t,\delta)>0
such that,
for
|x|\geq L(t,\delta)
,

|\bar{\rho}_{R}-\rho_{\infty}|\leq\delta
and
|\bar{u}_{R}|\leq\delta
.
Recall that
\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}\geq 1+(\rho_{\infty}+1)^{\theta_{2}}
(see (
3.2
)).
Hence, for
|x|\geq L(t,\delta)
, we see that

w_{2}(\bar{\rho}_{R},\bar{m}_{R})(t,x)\leq\delta+(\rho_{\infty}+\delta)^{\theta_{2}}\leq 1+(\rho_{\infty}+1)^{\theta_{2}}\leq\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}
, where we have
used (
2.21
) for the general pressure law case. For each
t\in[0,T]
, define

\tilde{L}(t,1):=\inf\big\{L(t,1)\,:\,w_{2}(t,x)\leq 1+(\rho_{\infty}+1)^{\theta_{2}}\,\,\,\mbox{for any
$|x|\geq L(t,1)$}\big\}.

Suppose that the set

\Sigma:=\big\{(t,x)\in\left(0,\tau_{R}\right]\times\mathbb{R}\,:\,w_{2}(t,x)>\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}\big\}

is nonempty. Then, by continuity of
w_{2}
,
this set
\Sigma
is an open set.
Also,
\Sigma\subset\Sigma_{b}
, where

\Sigma_{b}:=\big\{(t,x)\,:\,t\in[0,\tau_{R}],\ |x|\leq\tilde{L}(t,1)\big\}.

Moreover, on
\Sigma
,
by (
3.4
),
equation (
8.3
) turns into a deterministic equation:

{\rm d}w_{2}=\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}w_{2}\,{\rm d}t+\big(2\varepsilon\frac{\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}_{R}}{\bar{\rho}_{R}}-\lambda_{2}\big)\partial_{x}w_{2}\,{\rm d}t-\frac{\varepsilon(\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}_{R})^{2}}{2(\bar{\rho}_{R})^{2}\sqrt{P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho}_{R})}}\big(2P^{\prime}(\bar{\rho}_{R})+\bar{\rho}_{R}P^{\prime\prime}(\bar{\rho}_{R})\big)\,{\rm d}t

which implies

\partial_{t}w_{2}\leq\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}w_{2}+\big(2\varepsilon\frac{\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}_{R}}{\bar{\rho}_{R}}-\lambda_{2}\big)\partial_{x}w_{2}.

Since
\|w_{2}\|_{C([0,\tau_{R}],L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}))}<\infty
,
we find that
\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}<M:=\sup_{(t,x)\in\Sigma}w_{2}(t,x)<\infty
. Consider
w_{2}^{\beta}:=w_{2}-\beta t
for
\beta>0
. Then

\partial_{t}w_{2}^{\beta}\leq\varepsilon\partial_{x}^{2}w_{2}^{\beta}+\big(2\varepsilon\frac{\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}_{R}}{\bar{\rho}_{R}}-\lambda_{2}\big)\partial_{x}w_{2}^{\beta}-\beta.

Also,
M^{\beta}:=\sup_{(t,x)\in\Sigma}w_{2}^{\beta}(t,x)<\infty
.
If the supremum
M^{\beta}
is attained at an interior point
(t_{0},x_{0})\in\Sigma

with
|x_{0}|<\tilde{L}(t_{0},1)
, then, at this point
(t_{0},x_{0})
(recall that
\Sigma
is an open set), we have

\partial_{t}w_{2}^{\beta}\geq 0,\quad\partial_{x}w_{2}^{\beta}=0,\quad\partial_{x}^{2}w_{2}^{\beta}\leq 0.

Thus, we obtain that
0\leq\partial_{t}w_{2}^{\beta}\leq-\beta<0
,
which is a contradiction.
Therefore,
M^{\beta}=\sup_{(t,x)\in\partial\Sigma}w_{2}^{\beta}(t,x)
.
Taking
\beta\to 0
gives
M=\sup_{(t,x)\in\partial\Sigma}w_{2}(t,x)
.
However, it follows from the definition of
\Sigma

that
w_{2}(t,x)|_{(t,x)\in\partial\Sigma}=\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}<M
,
which is a contradiction again. Therefore, the set
\Sigma
is an empty set and
w_{2}(t,x)\leq\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}
for any
(t,x)\in[0,\tau_{R}]\times\mathbb{R}
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Propositions

8.2
–
8.3
lead to the following simple corollary.

Corollary 8.1
.

Assume that
U_{0}
satisfies the conditions in

Theorem
8.1
.
Then the density has a lower bound
:

\|{\bar{\rho}_{R}}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times[0,\tau_{R}])}\leq e^{C(T,c_{0},\varepsilon,\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon})}

\mathbb{P}
-a.s..

Next, we derive the
uniform energy estimate.

Proposition 8.4
.

Assume that
U_{0}
satisfies the conditions in

Theorem
8.1
. Then the solution
(\bar{\rho}_{R},\bar{u}_{R})
of the cut-off equations (
8.1
) satisfies energy estimate
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sup_{r\in[0,t\land\tau_{R}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\bar{\rho}_{R}(\bar{u}_{R})^{2}+e^{*}(\bar{\rho}_{R},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x

(8.19)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t\land\tau_{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(\big(\bar{\rho}_{R}e(\bar{\rho}_{R})\big)^{\prime\prime}(\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}_{R})^{2}+\bar{\rho}_{R}(\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{R})^{2}\Big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big]\leq C(p,T,E_{0,p}),

where

C(p,T,E_{0,p})
is independent of
\varepsilon
and
R
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.2
,
for any fixed
x
, equations (
8.1
) hold as a finite-dimensional stochastic differential system.
Since the cutoff equations (
8.1
) and (
3.1
) coincide when restricted to
[0,\tau_{R}]
,
we apply the Itô formula to the relative mechanical energy
\eta^{*}_{E}

(
cf.
(
2.16
)) to obtain from (
3.1
) that

{\rm d}\eta^{*}_{E}(U)=-\partial_{x}q_{E}^{*}(U)\,{\rm d}t+\varepsilon\nabla\eta_{E}^{*}(U)\partial_{x}^{2}U\,{\rm d}t+\nabla\eta_{E}^{*}(U)\Psi^{\varepsilon}(U)\,{\rm d}W(t)+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta_{E}^{*}(U)\big(\mathfrak{G}_{i}^{\varepsilon}\big)^{2}\,{\rm d}t,

where
(\eta^{*}_{E},q_{E}^{*})
is an entropy pair with

q_{E}^{*}(\rho,m)=\frac{1}{2}\frac{m^{3}}{\rho^{2}}+m\big(\rho e(\rho)\big)^{\prime}-\big(\rho e(\rho)\big)^{\prime}|_{\rho=\rho_{\infty}}m
and
\mathfrak{G}_{i}^{\varepsilon},i=1,2
,
are defined in the beginning of §
3
.
Then, by integration by parts, we see that, for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{E}^{*}(U)(\tau)\,{\rm d}x+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{x}U)^{\top}\,(\eta_{E}^{*})^{\prime\prime}(U)\,\partial_{x}U\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{E}^{*}(U_{0})\,{\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nabla\eta_{E}^{*}(U)\Psi^{\varepsilon}(U)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}W(r)+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta_{E}^{*}(U)\big(\mathfrak{G}_{i}^{\varepsilon}\big)^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r.

Since the solution satisfies
(\rho-\rho_{\infty},u)\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}))

\mathbb{P}
-
a.s.
,
using the Sobolev embedding:
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
and the fact that

\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}
has compact support,
we can directly prove that the stochastic integral

G^{\varepsilon}(\tau):=\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nabla\eta_{E}^{*}(U)\Psi^{\varepsilon}(U)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}W(r)
is a local martingale.
Hence, by the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{\tau\in[0,t\land\tau_{R}]}|G^{\varepsilon}(\tau)|^{p}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{\tau\in[0,t\land\tau_{R}]}\Big|\int_{0}^{\tau}\sum_{k}\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}}ua_{k}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\beta_{k}(r)\Big|^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C\,\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t\land\tau_{R}}\sum_{k}\Big(\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}}ua_{k}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{2}{\rm d}r\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\delta\,\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sup_{r\in[0,t\land\tau_{R}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho u^{2}\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big]+C_{\delta}\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t\land\tau_{R}}\sum_{k}\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{1}{\rho}a_{k}^{2}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big].

Recall that we have estimated the second term on the right-hand side of the above inequality in (
3.18
).

Therefore, we conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{\tau\in[0,t\land\tau_{R}]}|G^{\varepsilon}(\tau)|^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\delta\,\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sup_{r\in[0,t\land\tau_{R}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho u^{2}\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big]+C_{\delta}C(p,t)

\displaystyle+C_{\delta}C(p)\,\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t\land\tau_{R}}\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})}\big(\rho u^{2}+e^{*}(\rho,\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big].

Notice that
\partial_{m}^{2}\eta_{E}^{*}(U)=\frac{1}{\rho}
, by the same arguments as above, we obtain that,
for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{\tau\in[0,t\land\tau_{R}]}\Big|\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{2}\partial_{m}^{2}\eta_{E}^{*}(U)\big(\mathfrak{G}_{i}^{\varepsilon}\big)^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big|^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C(p,t)+C(p)\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t\land\tau_{R}}\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}(\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon})}\big(\rho u^{2}+e^{*}(\rho,\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big].

Combining the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sup_{\tau\in[0,t\land\tau_{R}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\rho u^{2}+e^{*}(\rho,\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t\land\tau_{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big((\rho e(\rho))^{\prime\prime}(\partial_{x}\rho)^{2}+\rho(\partial_{x}u)^{2}\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\delta\,\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\sup_{r\in[0,t\land\tau_{R}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho u^{2}\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big]+C(p)\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\frac{1}{2}\rho_{0}u_{0}^{2}+e^{*}(\rho_{\varepsilon 0},\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{p}\big]

\displaystyle+C_{\delta}C(p,t)+C_{\delta}C(p)\,\mathbb{E}\big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t\land\tau_{R}}\int_{{\rm supp}_{x}\zeta_{k}^{\varepsilon}}\big(\rho u^{2}+e^{*}(\rho,\rho_{\infty})\big)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}r\Big)^{p}\big].

Then the proof is completed by taking
\delta
sufficiently small and using the Grönwall inequality.
∎

We are now ready to prove the uniform estimates with respect to
R
.

Proposition 8.5
.

Assume that
U_{0}
satisfies the conditions in

Theorem
8.1
and

(\rho_{0}-\rho_{\infty},u_{0})\in L^{p_{0}}(\Omega;H^{5}(\mathbb{R}))\qquad\text{for some $p_{0}>12$.}

Then the solution
(\bar{\rho}_{R},\bar{u}_{R})
of the cut-off equations (
8.1
), obtained
in
Theorem
8.1
, obeys the following uniform estimates
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{r\in[0,\,t\land\tau_{R}]}\|(\bar{\rho}_{R}-\rho_{\infty},\bar{u}_{R})\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t\land\tau_{R}}\|(\partial_{x}\bar{\rho}_{R},\partial_{x}\bar{u}_{R})\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\,{\rm d}r\big]\leq C,

(8.20)

where
C>0
is independent of
R
.

For the proof of Proposition
8.5
, notice that, on
[0,\tau_{R}]
,
the cut-off equations (
3.1
) and
(
8.1
) are coincide.
Additionally,
U_{0}
and
(\bar{\rho}_{R},\bar{u}_{R})
satisfy the condition in Propositions

8.2
–
8.4
. Therefore, restricting ourselves on
\left[0,\tau_{R}\right)
,
we can work on equations (
3.1
) and apply the estimates in Propositions

8.2
–
8.4
, using which the proof can be done by standard careful estimates
for parabolic equations.
We omit the details and only point out that, by the regularity estimates in Proposition
8.1
, we can use the infinite-dimensional Itô formula in
[
20
, Theorem 4.32]
for the functional

\displaystyle F:

\displaystyle L^{2}(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R},

(8.21)

\displaystyle f(t)\mapsto\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\qquad\mbox{for any $t\in[0,T]$},

with
f(t)=\partial^{i}_{x}(\bar{\rho}_{R}-\rho_{\infty})
or
\partial^{i}_{x}\bar{u}_{R}
for

i=0,1,2,3
.

Now we remove the cut-off and obtain the global solution to (
3.1
). By Proposition
8.5
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{\tau^{*}<T\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\{\tau_{R}<T\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\{\|\bar{u}_{R}\|_{C([0,\tau_{R}];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}))}\geq R\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{R^{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{t\in[0,\,\tau_{R}]}\|\bar{u}_{R}\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{R^{2}}\to 0\quad\text{as}\ R\to\infty,

which implies that
\mathbb{P}(\tau^{*}=T)=1
.
Thus,
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u})
, defined by
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u}):=(\bar{\rho}_{R},\bar{u}_{R})
on
[0,\tau_{R}]
, is the global solution to (
3.1
) when
U_{0}
satisfies the conditions in Theorem
8.1
.

8.4
Completion of the Proof of Theorem
3.1

In §
8.3
, we have obtained the global solution
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u})
to (
3.1
) under the assumptions that
U_{0}
satisfies the conditions
in Theorem
3.1
and

\rho_{0}-\rho_{\infty}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega;H^{3}(\mathbb{R})),\quad u_{0}\in L^{p}(\Omega;H^{4}(\mathbb{R}))\,\,\mbox{for}\,\,p\geq 1,\quad(\rho_{0}-\rho_{\infty},u_{0})\in L^{p_{0}}(\Omega;H^{5}(\mathbb{R}))

(8.22)

for some
p_{0}>12
. We now remove these additional assumptions. To do this, we need the following uniqueness result.

Proposition 8.6
.

Assume that
U_{0}
satisfies the conditions
in
Theorem
3.1
and (
8.22
).
Then the strong solution of (
3.1
) satisfying conditions
(i)
–
(ii)

in
Theorem
3.1
is unique.

We give a sketch of the proof for Proposition
8.6
here. To show the pathwise uniqueness, we consider two strong solutions
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
to (
3.1
). We then define the stopping time for
U_{i},i=1,2
:

\tau_{R}^{i}=\inf\big\{t\in[0,T]\,:\,\|\rho_{i}-\rho_{\infty}\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}(t)+\|m_{i}\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R})}(t)\geq R\big\}

with the convention that
\inf\emptyset=T
and

\tilde{\tau}_{R}=\tau_{R}^{1}\land\tau_{R}^{2}
.
Then
\tilde{\tau}_{R}
is increasing.
By (
8.20
),
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{\sup_{R\in\mathbb{N}}\tilde{\tau}_{R}<T\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\{\tilde{\tau}_{R}<T\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1,2}\mathbb{P}\{\|\rho_{i}-\rho_{\infty}\|_{C([0,\tau_{R}^{i}];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}))}+\|m_{i}\|_{C([0,\tau_{R}^{i}];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}))}\geq R\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{R^{2}}\to 0\qquad\,\text{as $R\to\infty$}.

Restricting to
[0,\tilde{\tau}_{R}]
,
we can prove the uniqueness by the standard estimate, with the aid of
(ii) in Theorem
3.1
.
Then, sending
R\to\infty
, we can complete the proof.

We now remove the additional conditions of the initial data and
prove Theorem
3.1
.
More specifically, we consider the general initial data
(\rho_{0},u_{0})

satisfying the conditions in
Theorem
3.1
.
For any given
N>0
, we define

A_{N}=\{(\rho,u)\,:\,\ \|(\rho-\rho_{\infty},u)\|_{H^{5}(\mathbb{R})}\leq N\}.

Then, for the initial data
(\rho_{0},u_{0})1_{(\rho_{0},u_{0})\in A_{N}}
,
the argument in §
8.3
gives us the unique
global strong solution
(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{u}_{N})

to (
3.1
).
Moreover, by the uniqueness result in
Proposition
8.6
,
we obtain that, when
N\geq M
,

(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{u}_{N})1_{(\rho_{0},u_{0})\in A_{M}}=(\tilde{\rho}_{M},\tilde{u}_{M})1_{(\rho_{0},u_{0})\in A_{M}}.

Since
(\rho_{0}-\rho_{\infty},u_{0})\in H^{5}(\mathbb{R})

\mathbb{P}
-
a.s.
, we see that

\mathbb{P}\{(\rho_{0},u_{0})\in\cup_{N=1}^{\infty}A_{N}\}=1
.
Define

(\rho,u)=(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{u}_{N})\qquad\text{when $(\rho_{0},u_{0})\in A_{N}$}.

We find that
(\rho,u)
is the unique global strong solution to (
3.1
) satisfying
(\rho-\rho_{\infty},u)\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}))\cap L^{2}([0,T];H^{4}(\mathbb{R}))

\mathbb{P}
-
a.s.
,
i.e.
condition (i)
in Theorem
3.1
.
Additionally, condition (ii) in
Theorem
3.1
follows from Proposition
8.3
and Corollary
8.1
, conditions (iii) and (iv)
in Theorem
3.1
follow
from Lemma
8.2
and Proposition
8.4
, respectively. This completes the proof
of Theorem
3.1
.

Appendix A
Appendix

In this appendix, we recall some analytic results used in this paper,
present a stochastic version of the generalized Murat lemma
and a generalized div-curl lemma,
and provide the proofs of Proposition 4.1.

A.1
Some Analytic Results

A.1.1
Some properties related to the heat semigroup

Using the same notation as in the proof
of Lemma
8.1
,
we denote
\mathbb{S}_{\varepsilon}(t)
as the heat semigroup and
K_{\varepsilon}(t,x)

as the heat kernel corresponding to the following heat equation:

\partial_{t}f-\varepsilon\Delta f=g\qquad\mbox{for $x\in\mathbb{R}$ and $t\in[0,T]$}.

where and hereafter
\Delta=\partial_{x}^{2}
.
In this appendix, we consider the case
\varepsilon=1
and simply
use the notation
\mathbb{S}(t)
and
K(t,x)
.
By the representation of the heat kernel (
8.3
),
we can directly prove the following well-known properties:

Lemma A.1
.

For all
p\in[1,\infty],\,l,m\in\mathbb{N}
, and
t>0
,
