\|\partial_{t}^{l}\partial_{x}^{m}K(t)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C(l,m,p)t^{-l-\frac{m}{2}-\frac{1}{2P^{\prime}}},

where
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}=1
, and
C(l,m,p)>0
is a constant depending on
l,m
,and
p
.

We now recall the definition and an embedding theorem for the Bessel potential spaces:

Definition A.1
.

For
\ell\in\mathbb{R}
and
p\in(1,\infty)
,
the Bessel potential space
H_{p}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})
is the space of all distributions

u
such that
(I-\Delta)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}u\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R})
.
For
u\in H_{p}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})
, the norm is defined as

\|u\|_{H_{p}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}=\|(I-\Delta)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}u\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R})}.

When
p=2
,
H_{2}^{\ell}=W^{\ell,2}
,
i.e.
,
H_{2}^{\ell}

coincides with the Sobolev space
W^{\ell,2}
, so
we simply denote
H_{2}^{\ell}
by
H^{\ell}
.

Lemma A.2
.

The following embeddings hold
:

(i)

If
{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying
\ell_{1}\leq\ell_{2}
,
then
H_{p}^{\ell_{2}}\hookrightarrow H_{p}^{\ell_{1}}
;

(ii)

If
p{\ell}>1
and
\ell-\frac{1}{p}
is not an integer,
then
H_{p}^{\ell}\hookrightarrow C^{\ell-\frac{1}{p}}
.

The proof of (i)–(ii) can be found in
[
32
, Corollary 13.3.9]

and
[
32
, Theorem 13.8.1]
, respectively.

We also recall the definition of the Besov space and some of its properties.
By
[
43
, Theorem 1.2.5]
, we define the Besov space:

Definition A.2
.

Let
0<\ell<1,\,1<p<\infty
, and
1\leq q\leq\infty
. The Besov space is defined by

B_{pq}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})=\Big\{f\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R})\,:\,\|f\|_{B_{pq}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}=\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R})}+\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|h|^{-{\ell}q}\|\Delta_{h}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R})}^{q}\frac{{\rm d}h}{|h|}\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}<\infty\Big\},

where
\Delta_{h}f(x):=f(x+h)-f(x)
.

Using Definition
A.2
, we can check the following property (see also
[
3
, (B.12)]
):

Lemma A.3
.

For any locally Lipschitz
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying
f(0)=0
,
then

\|f(u)\|_{B_{pq}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\leq L_{M}(f)\|u\|_{B_{pq}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\qquad\mbox{for any $u\in B_{pq}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})$},

where
L_{M}
denotes the Lipschitz constant of
f
on interval
[-M,M]
with
M:=\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}
.

From
[
43
, Theorem 1.3.2 (iv)]
, we have the following relation between
the Besov space and the Bessel potential space.

Lemma A.4
.

For
0<\ell<1
,
H_{2}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})=B_{22}^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})
.

The next lemma states the algebraic property of the Bessel potential space.

Lemma A.5
.

[
42
, Proposition 1.1]

If
\ell>\frac{1}{2}
and
f,g\in H^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})
,
then

\|fg\|_{H^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\|f\|_{H^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}\|g\|_{H^{\ell}(\mathbb{R})}.

Lemma A.6
.

If
\alpha>0
, then

(i)

(-\Delta)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}(I-\Delta)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}
is bounded in
L^{p}(\mathbb{R})

for
1\leq p\leq\infty
;

(ii)

\|(-\Delta)^{\alpha}\mathbb{S}(t)u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C(\alpha)t^{-\alpha}\|u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}
;

(iii)

(\partial_{x})(-\Delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and
(\partial_{x})(I-\Delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
are bounded in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

Proof.

For (i), see
[
41
, Lemma V.3.2]
for the details.
For (ii), it follows from
[
31
, Theorem 14.11]
.

For (iii), using the Fourier transform and the definition of the Riesz potential, as well as (i), it can be easily proved.
∎

Lemma A.7
.

For
\ell>0
, the two norms
\|\cdot\|_{H_{p}^{\ell}}

and
\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}}+\|(-\Delta)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}\cdot\|_{L^{p}}
are equivalent.

Lemma
A.7
follows from
[
41
, Lemma V.3.2]
and
[
26
, Example 6.2.9]
.

A.1.2
A compactness lemma

The following lemma can be found in
[
39
, Corollary B.2]
.

Lemma A.8
.

Let
a=\{a_{n}\}
be a sequence of positive constants,
let
B_{n}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be an open ball with center
at the origin and radius
n
,
and let
\alpha>0,\,1<r,p<\infty
, and
-\infty<\ell\leq k

satisfy
\frac{1}{p}-\frac{k}{d}\leq\frac{1}{r}-\frac{\ell}{d}
. Then the set

A(a):=\big\{g\in C_{\rm w}([0,T];W^{k,p}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\,:\,\|g\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];W^{k,p}(B_{n}))}+\|g\|_{C^{\alpha}([0,T];W^{\ell,r}(B_{n}))}\leq a_{n},\ n\in\mathbb{N}\big\}

is a metrizable compact set in
C_{\rm w}([0,T];W^{k,p}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, i.e., the embedding
:

L^{\infty}([0,T];W^{k,p}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\cap C^{\alpha}([0,T];W^{\ell,r}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\hookrightarrow C_{\rm w}([0,T];W^{k,p}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))

is compact.

A.2
Stochastic Version of Murat’s Lemmas

The following lemma is a generalized version of
[
25
, Lemma A.3]
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
; also see

[
8
,
22
]
.

Lemma A.9
.

Assume that
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is a bounded open set with smooth
C^{\infty}
boundary. Suppose that
\phi^{\varepsilon}=\psi^{\varepsilon}_{1}+\psi^{\varepsilon}_{2}
satisfies

(i)

\{\psi^{\varepsilon}_{1}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is tight in
W^{-1,p_{1}}(\mathcal{O})
with
p_{1}>1
,

(ii)

\{\psi^{\varepsilon}_{2}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is tight in
W^{-1,p_{2}}(\mathcal{O})
with
p_{2}\in(1,2)
,

(iii)

\{\phi^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is stochastically bounded in
W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})

for some
p>\min\{p_{1},p_{2}\}
, i.e., for any
\delta>0
, there exists
C_{\delta}>0

such that
\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\mathbb{P}(\|\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{W^{-1,p}}>C_{\delta})<\delta
.

Then
\{\phi^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is tight in
W^{-1,q}(\mathcal{O})
with
\min\{p_{1},p_{2}\}\leq q<p
.

Proof.

The proof is almost similar to
that of Lemma A.3 in
[
25
]
.
In fact, it suffices to notice the following two points:
The first point is that, in the proof of Lemma A.3 in
[
25
]
,
it is used that the uniform boundedness of a sequence in measures
implies its pre-compactness in
W^{-1,q}(\mathcal{O})
with
q\in(1,2)
.
Therefore, actually, we only need the tightness of
\psi^{\varepsilon}_{2}

in
W^{-1,q}(\mathcal{O})
with
q\in(1,2)
.
The second point is that, in the proof of Lemma A.3 in
[
25
]
,
the final conclusion that
\{\phi^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is tight
in
H^{-1}(\mathcal{O})
comes from the classical interpolation (
i.e.
, Murat’s lemma).
Here to conclude,
we should use the compact interpolation theorem
(see
[
8
,
22
]
):
For
1<\tilde{p}_{2}<\tilde{p}_{1}\leq\infty
and
\tilde{p}_{2}\leq\tilde{p}_{0}<\tilde{p}_{1}
,

\big(\text{compact set of $W^{-1,\tilde{p}_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}\big)\cap\big(\text{bounded set of $W^{-1,\tilde{p}_{1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}\big)\subset\big(\text{compact set of $W^{-1,\tilde{p}_{0}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}\big).

∎

We next establish another stochastic-version Murat’s lemma used in the proof of the compactness of
the martingale entropy solutions,
i.e.
, Lemma
A.11

(for the deterministic one, see
[
38
]
),
before which we state a lemma from
[
38
]
without proof.

Lemma A.10
.

Let
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a bounded domain with Lipschitz
boundary
\partial\mathcal{O}
.
Then, for any
\alpha>0
small enough and
1<p_{0}<q_{0}<\infty
,
there exists
\psi^{\alpha}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{O})
such that,
for any
f\in W^{1,q_{0}}_{0}(\mathcal{O})
,

\|(1-\psi^{\alpha})\varphi\|_{W^{1,p_{0}}_{0}(\mathcal{O})}\leq\alpha\|\varphi\|_{W^{1,q_{0}}_{0}(\mathcal{O})}.

(A.1)

Lemma A.11
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
is an open bounded domain with Lipschitz boundary,
1<p<\infty
,
and
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}=1
. If
\{f^{\delta}\}_{\delta>0}
satisfies

(i)

\{f^{\delta}\}_{\delta>0}\subset W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})
is stochastically bounded in
W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})
,
i.e., for any
\beta>0
there exists
C_{\beta}>0
such that

\sup_{\delta>0}\mathbb{P}\{\|f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}>C_{\beta}\}<\beta
;

(ii)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,
f^{\delta}\leq 0
in the sense of distribution, i.e.,
\langle f^{\delta}(\omega),\varphi\rangle\leq 0

for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{O})
with
\varphi\geq 0
,
where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denotes the pairing between
W^{-1,p}
and
W^{1,p^{\prime}}_{0}
.

Then
\{f^{\delta}\}_{\delta>0}
is tight in
W^{-1,p_{0}}(\mathcal{O})
for any
1<p_{0}<p
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that in the deterministic case.

1
. Claim: For any
K\Subset\mathcal{O}
, there exists
C_{\beta}(K)
such that,
for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{O})
with
{\rm supp}(\varphi)\subset K
and any
\delta>0
,

\{\|f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C_{\beta}\}\subset\{|\langle f^{\delta},\varphi\rangle|\leq C_{\beta}(K)\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\}
.

In fact, for any
K\Subset\mathcal{O}
, there exists
\Phi_{K}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{O})
such that
\Phi_{K}\equiv 1
on
K
and
\Phi_{K}\geq 0
in
\mathcal{O}
.
Then, for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{O})
with
{\rm supp}(\varphi)\subset K
,

-\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O})}\Phi_{K}(x)\leq\varphi(x)\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O})}\Phi_{K}(x)
in
\mathcal{O}
,
i.e.
,
\varphi(x)+\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O})}\Phi_{K}(x),\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O})}\Phi_{K}(x)-\varphi(x)\geq 0
in
\mathcal{O}
. By (ii), we see that

\langle f^{\delta}(\omega),\varphi+\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O})}\Phi_{K}\rangle\leq 0

and
\langle f^{\delta}(\omega),\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O})}\Phi_{K}-\varphi\rangle\leq 0
, which imply that

|\langle f^{\delta},\,\varphi\rangle|\leq-\langle f^{\delta},\,\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O})}\Phi_{K}\rangle\leq C_{\beta}(K)\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O})}.

Thus,
\{\|f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C_{\beta}\}\subset\{|\langle f^{\delta},\varphi\rangle|\leq C_{\beta}(K)\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\}
.
Then, by (i),
\sup_{\delta>0}\mathbb{P}\{|\langle f^{\delta},\varphi\rangle|\leq C_{\beta}(K)\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\}>1-\beta
.

2
. Suppose that
\Phi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{O})

with
{\rm supp}(\Phi)\subset\mathcal{O}
. Note that

\|\Phi f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}=\sup_{\varphi\in W^{1,P^{\prime}},\|\varphi\|_{W^{1,P^{\prime}}}\leq 1}|\langle\Phi f^{\delta},\varphi\rangle|\leq C({\rm supp}\Phi)\|f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}},

which implies that there exists
C_{\beta,1}({\rm supp}(\Phi))>0
such that

\{\|f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C_{\beta}\}\subset\{\|\Phi f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C_{\beta,1}({\rm supp}(\Phi))\}.

Additionally, by Step
1
, there exists
C_{\beta,2}({\rm supp}(\Phi))

such that

\{\|f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C_{\beta}\}\subset\{|\langle\Phi f^{\delta},\varphi\rangle|\leq C_{\beta,2}({\rm supp}(\Phi))\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\},

which further implies that
\{\|f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C_{\beta}\}\subset\{\|\Phi f^{\delta}\|_{\big(C_{0}(\bar{\mathcal{O}})\big)^{*}}\leq C_{\beta,2}({\rm supp}(\Phi))\}
.

3
.
By the Sobolev embedding theorem and the duality principle,

\big(C_{0}(\bar{\mathcal{O}})\big)^{*}\hookrightarrow W^{-1,r^{\prime}}(\mathcal{O})
is compact, for
1\leq r^{\prime}=\frac{r}{r-1}\leq\frac{n}{n-1}
.
Then there exists a deterministic compact set
B_{1,\beta}\Subset W^{-1,r^{\prime}}(\mathcal{O})
such that

\{\|f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C_{\beta}\}\subset\{\|\Phi f^{\delta}\|_{\big(C_{0}(\bar{\mathcal{O}})\big)^{*}}\leq C_{\beta,2}({\rm supp}(\Phi))\}\subset\{\omega:\Phi f^{\delta}\in B_{1,\beta}\}
.

4
.
By interpolation between
W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})
and
W^{-1,r^{\prime}}(\mathcal{O})
, there exists

B_{2,\beta}\Subset W^{-1,p_{0}}(\mathcal{O}),1<r^{\prime}\leq p_{0}<p
such that

\{\|f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C_{\beta}\}\subset\{\omega:\Phi f^{\delta}\in B_{1,\beta}\}\cap\{\|\Phi f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C_{\beta,1}({\rm supp}\Phi)\}\subset\{\omega:\Phi f^{\delta}\in B_{2,\beta}\}.

5
. For any
\alpha>0
small enough, let
\psi^{\alpha}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{O})
be the function obtained
in Lemma
A.10
satisfying (
A.1
)
and
(1-\psi^{\alpha})f^{\delta}\in W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})
. Then it follows from
(
A.1
) that,
for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{O})
,

|\langle(1-\psi^{\alpha})f^{\delta},\varphi\rangle|\leq\alpha\|f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}\|\varphi\|_{W_{0}^{1,q^{\prime}}}\qquad\mbox{for any $q^{\prime}>p^{\prime}$},

which implies that
\|(1-\psi^{\alpha})f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,q}(\mathcal{O})}\leq\alpha\|f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}
. Therefore,
\{\|f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C_{\beta}\}\subset\{\|(1-\psi^{\alpha})f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,q}(\mathcal{O})}\leq\alpha C_{\beta}
. On the other hand, by Step
4
,
we see that
\{\|f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C_{\beta}\}\subset\{f^{\delta}-(1-\psi^{\alpha})f^{\delta}\in B_{2,\beta}\}
. Combining together gives

\{\|f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C_{\beta}\}\subset\{f^{\delta}-(1-\psi^{\alpha})f^{\delta}\in B_{2,\beta}\}\cap\{\|(1-\psi^{\alpha})f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,q}(\mathcal{O})}\leq\alpha C_{\beta}\}.

Thus, letting
\alpha\to 0
, we obtain that
\{\|f^{\delta}\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C_{\beta}\}\subset\{f^{\delta}\in B_{2,\beta}\}
. The proof is completed.
∎

A.3
Generalized Div-Curl Lemma

For the general pressure law case,
an improvement of the classical div-curl lemma in
[
37
]
is needed.

Lemma A.12

(
[
19
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an open bounded set
and
p,q\in(1,\infty)
with
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=1
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}
and
\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}
are sequences of vector fields satisfying

\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\mathbf{v}\ \text{in $L^{p}(\mathcal{O};\mathbb{R}^{n})$},\quad\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\mathbf{w}\ \text{in $L^{q}(\mathcal{O};\mathbb{R}^{n})$}\qquad\,\,{\text{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}}.

Suppose further that
\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}
is equi-integrable and

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle div}\,\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}\qquad\,\,\,\text{is {\rm(}pre-{\rm)}compact in $W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathcal{O})$},

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle curl}\,\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}\qquad\text{is {\rm(}pre-{\rm)}compact in $W^{-1,1}_{\rm loc}(\mathcal{O};\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})$}.

Then
\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}
converges to
\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{w}

in the distributional sense
:

\mathbf{v}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathbf{w}^{\varepsilon}\,\rightharpoonup\,\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{w}\qquad\,\,\text{in $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$}.

In Lemma
A.12
above,
W^{-1,1}(\mathcal{O})
is the dual of
W^{1,\infty}_{0}(\mathcal{O})
.

A.4
Proof of Proposition
4.1

The proof is in the spirit of Ball
[
2
]
.
Since
L^{\infty}_{\rm w}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}))
is relatively compact
in
L^{\infty}_{\rm w}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+};\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}))
, it remains to prove that
\Lambda_{C}
is closed.

Let
\{\mu_{n}\}_{n}\subset\Lambda_{C}
such that

\mu_{n}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\mu
. Define the cut-off functions
\chi_{k}\in C_{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2};\mathbb{R}_{+})
for
k>0
by

\chi_{k}(\mathbf{f})=\begin{cases}1\ &\quad\text{if}\ |\mathbf{f}|\leq k,\\
1+k-|\mathbf{f}|&\quad\text{if}\ k\leq|\mathbf{f}|\leq k+1,\\
0&\quad\text{if}\ |\mathbf{f}|>k+1.\end{cases}

Then
\varsigma\chi_{k}\in C_{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2};\mathbb{R}_{+})
. By the monotone convergence theorem, we have

\displaystyle\int_{[0,T]\times\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\varsigma(\mathbf{f})\,{\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{f})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{[0,T]\times\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\varsigma\chi_{k}(\mathbf{f})\,{\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{f})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{[0,T]\times\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\varsigma\chi_{k}(\mathbf{f})\,{\rm d}\mu_{n}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{f})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{[0,T]\times\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\varsigma(\mathbf{f})\,{\rm d}\mu_{n}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{f})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\leq C.

Furthermore, by the weak-* lower semicontinuity of the norm, we have

\|\mu\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}}\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\|\mu_{n}\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}}=1.

Next, we prove that
\|\mu\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}}=1
. Since
\mu_{n}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\mu

and
\chi_{k}\in C_{0}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2};\mathbb{R}_{+})
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{[0,T]\times\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\chi_{k}(\mathbf{f})\,{\rm d}\mu_{n}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{f}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,T]\times\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\chi_{k}(\mathbf{f})\,{\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{f}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}\leq\int_{[0,T]\times\hat{K}}\|\mu(\mathbf{x})\|_{\mathcal{M}}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}.

On the other hand, denoting
N(k):=\inf_{|\mathbf{f}|>k}\varsigma(\mathbf{f})
satisfying that

N(k)\to\infty
as
k\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\hat{K}|T}\int_{[0,T]\times\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\big(1-\chi_{k}(\mathbf{f})\big)\,{\rm d}\mu_{n}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{f}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\hat{K}|T}\int_{[0,T]\times\hat{K}}\int_{\{|\mathbf{f}|>k\}}\,{\rm d}\mu_{n}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{f}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N(k)}\frac{1}{|\hat{K}|T}\int_{[0,T]\times\hat{K}}\int_{\{|\mathbf{f}|>k\}}\varsigma(\mathbf{f})\,{\rm d}\mu_{n}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{f}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}\leq\frac{C}{N(k)}\frac{1}{T\times|\hat{K}|},

which implies

1-\frac{C}{N(k)}\frac{1}{|\hat{K}|T}\leq\frac{1}{|\hat{K}|T}\int_{[0,T]\times\hat{K}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\chi_{k}(\mathbf{f})\,{\rm d}\mu_{n}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{f}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}.

Letting
n\to\infty
first and then letting
k\to\infty
, we obtain

\frac{1}{|\hat{K}|T}\int_{[0,T]\times\hat{K}}\|\mu(\mathbf{x})\|_{\mathcal{M}}\,{\rm d}\mathbf{x}\geq 1,

which implies that
\|\mu(\mathbf{x})\|_{\mathcal{M}}=1
almost everywhere, since
T
and
\hat{K}
are arbitrary and
\|\mu(\mathbf{x})\|_{\mathcal{M}}\leq 1
almost everywhere. Therefore,
\|\mu\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}}=1
and
\mu\in\Lambda_{C}
. This completes the proof of Proposition
4.1
.
